# BotW 底座 1080p60 执行记录

## 2026-11-01：阶段 0
- 目标：BotW 1.9.0（v1114112）、TB323FU / Android 16；底座输出 1920×1080，内部动态 1280×720–1600×900，`DM_RENDER_SCALE` 不设，目标 60 fps。
- 场景：初始台地自有存档 `plateau-open-area-owned-seed.tar`（SHA-256 `ef69bda1…`）；每轮重置副本，主存档不参与游戏运行。
- 验收：预热 60 s、SurfaceFlinger 实际呈现测 120 s；固定 ×2 ≥59.5 fps、>17.5 ms ≤1%、无 >50 ms；移动 ×2 ≥59 fps、>17.5 ms ≤2%、>50 ms ≤2 次、无 >100 ms；另按计划做 30 分钟浸泡、验证层和正式版冒烟。
- 分支 `perf/docked-1080p60`；初始提交 `dde9db82`，与 `backup/codex-goal-2026-11-01` 比较，仅增加根 AGENTS.md 和 perf-plan.md。
- 新脚本：`<BUILD>/android/bench/bench.py`、`present_trace.py`；支持所需参数、基线组、按轮原始数据和 results.csv；A/B 固定视角顺序为 OFF/ON/ON/OFF。
- 离线验证：存档、1,063 个预热 key、驱动缓存、Perfetto 工具及回退 APK 校验通过；测量/恢复保护测试 9/9；仓库 unittest 104 项，0 失败、22 跳过（默认 PATH 无主机 C 编译器）。
- 路线：左摇杆前/后各 1 s，再右摇杆水平 0.3 s；无跳跃和上下转视角，开始/结束截图在阶段 1 首次移动时验看，最多修两次。
- 限制：现有 APK 只按 600 帧日志提供管线创建计数；CSV 明确标注范围，不能把它当成精确驱动编译次数，2a 验收仍需补齐证据。
- 本阶段未操作平板；下一步只读确认设备空闲，然后执行阶段 1。

## 2026-11-01：测量准备补充
- MinGW / NDK 下 unittest 101 项通过；3 项 Yaz0 原生测试指定已有 DLL 后单独通过，104 项均已有通过结果。
- B 固定 2 首次尝试在进入游戏前解锁超时；改为等待锁屏界面稳定后输入，随后成功。没有性能数据，不计作性能实验。
- B 固定 2 第二次尝试因结束检查两次读取 PID 的竞态误报退出失败；改为单次读取并增加竞态回归测试，脚本测试 10/10 通过。
- 第二次尝试的原始帧窗口可恢复（44.070 fps，最大 41.669 ms），但缺少结束截图/温控，只作参考；第三次完整重测及结束检查通过，作为正式 B 固定 2。
- 失败尝试均已验证主存档不变、恢复正式版和原设置、熄屏；原始记录保留在 `<BENCH>/runs/`。

## 2026-11-01：阶段 1，A/B 固定视角
- 假设：正式源码的 HOLD 默认开启会落入 30 fps 档；仅关闭 HOLD 应有可重复的整帧收益。
- 方法：底座目标配置、同一存档，每轮预热 60 s / 测 120 s，按 A/B/B/A 完成；两次失败尝试不计入对比。
- A1 / A2：29.964 / 29.679 fps，>17.5 ms 95.161% / 95.171%，P99 均 50.003 ms，最大均 58.337 ms，>50 ms 238 / 295 次。
- B1 / B2：44.160 / 44.804 fps，>17.5 ms 53.113% / 50.995%，P99 均 33.335 ms，最大 83.338 / 41.669 ms，>50 ms 2 / 0 次；全部窗口 >100 ms 均 0。
- 结论：关闭 HOLD 平均 44.482 fps，对比默认开启 29.822 fps，提升 14.660 fps；两个 B 均高于两个 A，超过有效收益门槛。
- 输出均 1920×1080；A1 内部 1600×900 → 1280×720，其余前后均 1600×900；均载入 1,063 keys，约 4,600 draws/frame，截图场景一致。
- CPU 上限 1,996,800 / 2,438,400 kHz，前后 CPU/GPU/skin 温控等级均 0，音频正常，无新 RED。
- 数据：`<BENCH>/results.csv` 及 `runs/*-A-fixed-*` / `runs/*-B-fixed-*`；下一步移动 A/B 和 C/D，尚无私有性能栈的目标配置结论。

- 已在熄屏冷却期间保存完成窗口的管线 key 文件：A/B 固定与 A 移动共 1,143 个唯一 key，较种子新增 80 个；基线后续仍使用原 1,063 个种子 key，保持条件一致。
- B 移动：48.225 fps，>17.5 ms 33.103%，P99 33.335 ms，最大 191.677 ms，>50 ms 19 次、>100 ms 6 次；内部前后均 1600×900，约 4,236 draws/frame，温控/音频/RED 检查同 A。
- B 开始/结束截图的人物与地面可见；数据在 `runs/20261101-192842-B-moving-1/`，本轮新增后的 key 文件共 1,132 条，已保存。
- 结论：关闭 HOLD 的移动窗口比 A 高 15.056 fps，长帧仍不达标；下一步按计划测试 C/D，不增加节奏实验。

## 2026-11-01：阶段 1，C/D 基线
- C 固定 1 / 2：57.893 / 58.127 fps，平均 58.010 fps；>17.5 ms 3.828% / 3.483%，P99 均 33.335 ms，最大 116.673 / 175.010 ms。

- C 移动：57.646 fps，>17.5 ms 3.888%，P99 33.335 ms，最大 191.677 ms，>50 ms 29 次、>100 ms 7 次；内部前后 1600×900，温控均 0，无新 RED，音频正常，结束截图人物和地面可见。
- D 首次尝试在解锁等待超时前未进入游戏；原因是代理处理本地测试输出时未及时响应，未形成性能窗口。组已验证主存档不变并恢复正式版、原设置。
- 补测启动检查发现屏幕由代理在上一轮退出后点亮，尚未修改应用；明确使用 `--allow-awake`（本任务点亮的屏幕）后开始唯一一次 D 补测，仍失败则停止。
- D 补测通过：33.933 fps，>17.5 ms 59.651%，P99 50.003 ms，最大 925.051 ms，>50 ms 656 次、>100 ms 3 次；前后内部 1600×900、温控均 0，音频正常、无新 RED。
- 结论：选 `DM_FPS_HOLD=off`；C 三窗口综合均值 57.889 fps（固定均值 58.010、移动 57.646），按计划记录当前限频下底座 60 帧需要降分辨率或解除限频；进入有限的 2a–2d，不追加实验。
- 恢复验证通过：正式版 `41a37988`、原 env/settings、主存档逐文件哈希不变，应用停止且屏幕关闭；2c 已向用户询问，未答复时继续其他步骤。

## 2026-11-01：阶段 3 源码与测试准备（与剩余基线并行）
- 已按差异分类移植编码线程、尾批、提前通知、延后提交和比较采样器兼容路径；核心新增开关默认关闭，BotW 配置尚未更改。
- 丢弃私有同步跟踪、采样/几何审计、独立回收线程、Turnip 加载与 1.25 缩放上限；保留所移植功能的简要启用与累计计数日志。
- 单元测试移入编码顺序/参数所有权、双完成边界和延后提交，覆盖错误、超时、拷贝与提交顺序，并使用破坏性对照验证检测能力。
- 比较采样器测试增加全层级初始化、无需源数据拷贝、内容相关采样前替换，以及 NEVER/ALWAYS 分类；描述符测试夹具随新增状态类型更新。
- 两处夹具适配问题已修复：弱符号后端改为独立链接，描述符夹具引入生产类型；完整 `python -m unittest discover tests -v` 108/108 通过，0 跳过，78.380 s。
- 源码提交 `d8c12315`；测试提交 `f4db36d8`。本批源码尚未构建 APK 或进行实机验收，当前基线使用原有私有包。

- 移动：56.758 fps，>17.5 ms 4.198%，P99 41.669 ms，最大 241.680 ms，>50 / >100 ms 为 50 / 13 次。
- 两窗口前后内部均 1280×720、输出 1920×1080；温控均 0、音频正常、RED 0；截图人物、地面和 UI 可见。
- 结论：相对 C 固定均值 / 移动分别低 0.837 / 0.887 fps，没有整帧收益；720p 仅列为备选，默认仍按目标动态分辨率。
- 数据：`runs/20261101-202457-E-fixed-720/`、`runs/20261101-203111-E-moving-720/`；组恢复检查通过，主存档不变。

## 历史

以下为原 docs/PORT_STATUS.md 中的 BotW 实验记录，原样迁移；当时的设置、分支和结论不代表本计划的最终状态。

Local source-pipeline verification for Breath of the Wild (2026-10-28): the supplied combined XCI was extracted as base + update v1114112 + two DLCs. A lossless NSP rewrap of its secure partition then passed the full conversion pipeline: all 19,592 shaders translated, a signed APK and 48,177 data files (about 16 GiB). The APK and data were installed on the tablet. This source check is separate from the upstream frozen-bundle release checks linked above.

The material fix was checked with the same save in headless runs on the tablet while its display was off: the albedo attachment changed from all black to the expected textured scene, and character skin and environment materials returned in the final frame. A subsequent release-build on-screen test confirmed these materials, touch movement, camera input, menus and save loading. One non-fatal `BufferInitialize` range warning remains (a `0x40000` buffer over a `0x24000` pool); it was also present before the material fix. The release APK passes signature and 16 KiB alignment checks.

On-screen measurements (2026-10-28) use the game's SurfaceFlinger layer and the actual presentation timestamp column documented in [AOSP FrameTracker](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/native/+/cdb6b16dec3a541b455be99d075004cb2f0a0cd7/services/surfaceflinger/FrameTracker.cpp). Repeated samples were deduplicated; zero and pending-fence timestamps were excluded. These are short tests of the same Shrine save, not full gameplay or sustained thermal validation.

| Release test | Duration | Presented intervals | Mean fps | Frame interval p95 / max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Performance mode, idle, 30 fps target | 30.31 s | 905 | 29.86 | 41.67 / 66.67 ms |
| Performance mode, touch movement and camera, 30 fps target | 35.04 s | 1,039 | 29.65 | 41.67 / 116.67 ms |
| Resolution mode, 60 fps target, pass timing enabled | 35.29 s | 763 | 21.62 | 58.34 / 108.34 ms |

The measured Resolution-mode main geometry pass was 1280x720; a 1920x1080 final image does not establish native 1080p scene rendering. Replay summaries in the ordinary run were about 21 ms/frame, including about 10 ms of descriptor work, with about 27 ms of GPU time. In the instrumented Resolution run these were about 28 ms, 13 ms and 35 ms respectively. These overlapping costs must not be added together or treated as a controlled measurement of resolution scaling alone. The descriptor cache already reuses identical sets, but still collects and hashes their contents before finding a hit. The backend also uses whole-pipeline memory barriers and normally loads/stores all attached targets. These are concrete candidates for general state-version caching and dependency-based synchronization/load-store optimization. Per-submission pass timing is capped at 128 passes, so its summed pass times are not a complete accounting of GPU execution. No 1080p60 result is claimed. Diagnostic settings were removed and the tablet was returned to display-off state after testing.

### General optimization measurements and Apple source comparison

The Android checkout was updated through `debdb60` on 2026-10-28. The large-pool chunking fix prevents an allocator failure; the recorded pools in this BOTW scene were already zero-copy, so no copy-bandwidth improvement is attributed to that update. The interval index and buffer-range lookup were retained across the chunked-pool changes. Host builds compiled all 138 sources; 51 local tests, 29 NSP-pipeline tests, the million-query pool-index check and the 47 frame-step checks passed.

General CPU changes reduce repeated pipeline lookup, uniform overlay scanning, and copies of uniform bytes that conservative SPIR-V analysis proves unused. Shadow cache entries survive unrelated writes but are invalidated at scratch-lifetime boundaries. The frame-step controller now retains the shorter ADPF deadline when it lengthens the display hold, and owns pacing instead of competing with the Android governor. A same-debug-build pacing on/off/on run measured about 13 ms replay with the corrected deadline against 18 ms with the old path; its presentation rates varied between runs, so this is evidence of CPU savings, not a stable frame-rate gain.

With the updated runtime, a separate on-screen display-timing on/off/on test, with 15 s warm-up and about 25.59 s per sample, measured 20.005 fps in all three phases. The p95 interval was 50.00 / 58.34 / 50.00 ms. Removing desired-present timestamps did not recover throughput and made intervals less even. The 60 fps target remained at a three-vsync frame-step hold; no 60 fps gameplay result is claimed. A separate 30 fps release target measured 29.77 fps before the frame-step deadline change.

The final release APK (current library SHA-256 prefix `e5b440e07dd6979f`) was installed and checked again with the original 30 fps configuration: 766 presented intervals over 25.568 s, 29.96 fps, p95 33.34 ms and maximum 58.34 ms, with one interval over 50 ms and none over 100 ms. Steady replay summaries were 14.61-15.31 ms, including 6.36-6.72 ms of descriptor work; GPU timestamp spans were 25.80-26.00 ms. The Shrine materials were intact, with no red diagnostic lines in the captured log. APK v2 signing, 16 KiB APK/ELF alignment and the packaged library hash passed. The app was stopped and the tablet returned to display-off state. This remains an idle-scene baseline, not sustained or full-game validation.

The diagnostic "GPU busy" value is the sum of submissions' first-to-last GPU timestamp spans. Early command-buffer submission can leave CPU-feed gaps inside a span, so this value is not an isolated measure of active GPU computation. It overlaps CPU work and must not be added to replay time. Quantifying those gaps and acquire/present waits remains necessary before assigning the remaining time solely to shader execution.

Experiments were kept outside the default runtime: eliminating consecutive full barriers removed about 46% of those calls without establishing a time saving, so that implementation was withdrawn; optimizing 670 recorded shaders with SPIRV-Tools preserved reflection and passed validation, but did not establish a repeatable GPU improvement. A driver-constant-block state cache recorded no hits in this scene and was also withdrawn. Of those 670 shaders, reflection contains 1,529 guest-uniform bindings and 1,720 direct sampled-texture bindings, but just one driver-block binding. Optimizing that separate driver-block path does not address the observed hot work. The original shader bundle remains in normal use.

The Apple comparison used upstream `a3d603acf519c8d6da5a66ab7782a012cf09ff4b`, not just the older Metal copy in the Android subtree:

| Mechanism | Apple source | Current Android path and implication |
|---|---|---|
| Resource binding | `encode_args_` checks dirty state before building arguments, then binds GPU addresses | Upstream `c74b56d` adds a stage-input memo before descriptor collection, with dynamic uniform offsets refreshed and generation-based invalidation; its device measurements are separate from the older results below |
| Uniform read extent | `cb_extent.py` bounds accesses in translated MSL, with full-range fallback | The new SPIR-V extent analysis applies the same conservative principle to Vulkan |
| Uniform copies | `nvn_rec.h` also queues large shadow copies to a worker and waits at GPU commits and CPU reads | An experimental `DM_VK_UNIFORM_COPY_THREAD=1` worker preserves those waits and shadow dependencies. Byte verification and native queue stress checks pass, but same-binary on/off/on runs did not establish a saving; the default remains synchronous |
| Driver encoding | [`nvn_rec.h`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/blob/a3d603acf519c8d6da5a66ab7782a012cf09ff4b/dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_rec.h) sends Metal calls to an encoding worker, enabled by default on iOS | Android has a present worker and an optional, default-off asynchronous NVN replay path; these do not reproduce the newer driver's encoding pipeline |
| Frame buffering | [`snap_take`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/blob/a3d603acf519c8d6da5a66ab7782a012cf09ff4b/dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_mtl.m#L10973) copies a present into a bounded host-image ring; acquire waits for ring capacity as pacing | Experimental `DM_PRESENT_SNAP=2` uses a bounded Vulkan host-image ring, waits for capacity at acquire and preserves source-image lifetimes. It is off by default; ordinary presents retain the original image until the present worker blits it |
| Frame-rate decision | With snapshots, measured presentation and sustained lateness judge a hold | Android now records actual `VK_GOOGLE_display_timing` presentation results by present ID, excluding host redraws and stale results. The experimental snapshot path uses that feedback and the newer frame-step rules; the ordinary path retains its existing decision |

The Apple [BOTW profile](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/blob/a3d603acf519c8d6da5a66ab7782a012cf09ff4b/dm/games/01007EF00011E000/game.tsv#L27) records 300 s at 60 fps on an iPhone 17 Pro Max with present snapshots and the encoding worker. That is the maintainer's platform-specific result; it proves neither Android throughput nor native 1080p rendering. The mechanisms above can be implemented generally, but Metal APIs and profile opt-ins cannot simply be copied as Vulkan defaults. The 1080p60 objective remains unmet.

The Vulkan snapshot experiment presented correctly in the Shrine, with no ordinary-image fallbacks or red diagnostics. A same-session on/off/on change to its desired-present lead estimate measured 24.03 / 24.16 / 25.37 fps over about 25 s each, with 50.00 ms p95 intervals; this does not establish an improvement. A separate diagnostic disabled adaptive frame-step holds (`DM_FPS_HOLD=off`) while retaining snapshots and the 60 fps target. SurfaceFlinger then measured 822 presented intervals over 25.568 s: 32.15 fps, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 41.67 ms and no intervals over 50 ms. Game time advanced at 1.004 times real time. The difference shows that adaptive holds can further reduce presentation rate, but disabling them does not recover 60 fps throughput.

In that last diagnostic, steady per-frame replay took 11.10-11.47 ms for about 2,400 draws, including 4.75-4.93 ms of resource-descriptor work and about 14 MB of uniform shadows. GPU timestamp spans were 24.16-24.48 ms, and whole-frame medians about 31 ms. These overlap and are not additive; the GPU span still includes possible command-feed gaps. Shader execution, synchronization and CPU-feed delays have not yet been isolated from one another. The observed main 3D target remains 1280x720; a 1920x1080 final present or snapshot is not evidence of native 1080p rendering. These experiments are distinct from the validated 30 fps release baseline above, and neither experimental worker nor snapshots are enabled in normal settings.

`DM_VK_SEGMENT_TIMES=1` now optionally timestamps each main command buffer, including early parts. The diagnostic merges overlapping timestamp intervals before summing them, so pipelined overlap is not counted twice. A native reference test covers 100,000 random interval sets, invalid counters and timestamps near the integer limit. In a further on-screen Shrine run, complete steady samples measured 20.57-20.87 ms of covered command-buffer spans plus 3.53-3.76 ms between parts, for 24.16-24.49 ms envelopes and about 6.7 parts per frame. Covered spans still include stalls within a command buffer and exclude upload buffers and final presentation work; they are not hardware active-cycle counters. The gaps establish some command-feed starvation, but removing those gaps alone would not establish 60 fps throughput.

An Android Simpleperf capture of all app threads collected 11,722 user-cycle samples in 20 s, with no lost samples. Symbol-level evidence confirms descriptor preparation, copies, pool lookups and Vulkan calls on the replay thread, and several guest workers retrying an exclusive acquire loop. About 62% of frame-pointer callchains were incomplete, so inclusive callchain percentages are not a complete attribution of game work. An attempted general exclusive-retry pause introduced long frame delays: the first version measured 23.70 fps, and a second version with separate acquisition-attempt accounting measured 23.20 fps, with p95 intervals of 116.67 and 100.01 ms. Both runtime and rewriter implementations were withdrawn. Restoring the original module, using the same diagnostic APK and settings, recovered 32.08 fps over 25.626 s, p95 33.34 ms and maximum 41.67 ms, with no intervals over 50 ms. The rejected patch and measurements remain in the game's diagnostic folder, outside the repository.

A native capability probe on this tablet found the newer Android performance-hint entry points, but actual feature queries report surface binding and automatic CPU/GPU timing unsupported; graphics-pipeline sessions are supported. Function presence alone must therefore not enable automatic timing. The [Android API reference](https://developer.android.com/ndk/reference/group/a-performance-hint) documents the timing requirements; the device's `createSessionUsingConfig` ABI was also checked against its system library. A manual prototype reported completed CPU/GPU frame intervals, including upload command buffers, using serialized session operations and a legacy CPU fallback. Thousands of complete reports returned no API errors, but on/off/on measurements established no repeatable presentation gain: dual-session runs measured 28.88 / 28.87 / 30.19 fps; single-session runs measured 31.93 / 31.86 / 32.43 fps, with the last graphics-session recreation refused (`EBUSY`) and therefore not a valid GPU-hint phase. The prototype was withdrawn and saved in the game's diagnostic folder. Optional upload timestamps remain available through `DM_VK_UPLOAD_TIMES=1`; normal builds retain only the verified CPU hint path.

### Upstream integration on 2026-10-29

The checkout was fast-forwarded from `debdb60` to `c74b56de8808531b60f75ccfb72d4df84daf4a4e`, with local work preserved in a Git checkpoint and stash before the update. The merge retains the local container pipeline, text entry, controller mapping, material fix, uniform extent/version caches and frame-step deadline fix. It adopts upstream stage descriptor memoization, shared descriptor layouts, replay timing and layout shortcuts, copied-pool synchronization fixes, and sRGB presentation handling. Descriptor memo invalidation shares the uniform-write generation; real display feedback retains duplicate/stale-result guards and a separate unsupported-device fallback.

Debug and release builds compiled all 139 sources. All 55 root tests and 41 nested tool tests passed, including the three new upstream checks. Native checks passed for one million indexed pool lookups, uniform extents and overlays, version invalidation, asynchronous copies, 100,000 random GPU interval sets, concurrent presentation history, and 64 frame-step cases. These establish integration correctness under the tested inputs, not a device frame-rate improvement or general game compatibility.

The first gameplay memo check reported hash differences despite identical Vulkan fields. Extra diagnostics isolated uninitialized trailing padding in `VkDescriptorImageInfo`: hashing its entire structure included bytes Vulkan never reads. Image descriptors now hash canonical sampler, view and layout fields. A regression check poisons the padding differently, verifies equal keys for equal fields, and verifies that every field, array element and order still changes the key. Two subsequent headless gameplay windows of 600 frames each reported zero memo differences with uniform-byte verification enabled. The temporary extra descriptor diagnostics were removed.

A valid same-debug-binary on/off/on comparison then checked both live-control acknowledgements and the actual memo-hit counters. The 60 fps target, disabled frame-step holds, two-image snapshots and segment diagnostics were held constant. SurfaceFlinger measured 32.97 / 32.26 / 32.82 fps in approximately 25.5 s per phase; all phases had 33.34 ms p95 intervals, 41.67 ms maximum intervals, and no intervals over 50 ms. With the memo enabled, complete steady summaries measured 9.59-9.74 ms replay, including 3.20-3.23 ms descriptor work; the last fully disabled window measured 10.61 and 4.15 ms, with zero memo hits. This is a small CPU and throughput improvement, not 60 fps. An earlier three-phase release run could not change its controls because `DM_RELEASE` disables `DM_CTL`; those artifacts are explicitly marked invalid as a memo comparison and retained only as repeated enabled-memo release baselines (32.74-33.05 fps).

The enabled-memo GPU samples covered 20.45-20.76 ms of main command-buffer spans plus 3.09-3.15 ms between parts, with no incomplete gameplay submissions. The last 600 instrumented gameplay frames had a 30.40 ms median whole-frame interval and 9.99 ms median present-thread CPU time. Process CPU time was 35.79 ms per frame across all threads; it is not elapsed time and cannot be added to GPU time. These observations still do not isolate shader arithmetic from memory or synchronization stalls. The main 3D target remains 1280x720 and the general 1080p60 goal remains unmet.

The upstream asynchronous NVN replay path (`DM_NVN_ASYNC=1`) passed two headless gameplay verification windows with no memo differences, then one on-screen probe measured 32.05 fps, p95 33.34 ms and maximum 41.67 ms. The Shrine materials remained visible and there were no red diagnostics. That separate launch is not a controlled proof of a regression, but it established no improvement over the synchronous samples above, so asynchronous replay remains off by default. The game's original pacing is 30 fps; the Android profile also explicitly selects `DM_FPS_TARGET=30` for normal use. The unheld 60-target probes above distinguish that setting from the current throughput limit.

The final release (`libdmhost.so` SHA-256 prefix `c9e15a7e7e8315fc`) was installed and retested with the original 30 fps environment and normal presentation: 767 actual presented intervals over 25.635 s, 29.92 fps, p95 33.34 ms and maximum 50.003 ms, with no intervals over 100 ms and no red diagnostics. Steady replay summaries were 15.43-16.31 ms and GPU timestamp spans 26.14-26.30 ms; this longer-deadline power state is separate from the 60-target comparison. APK v2 signing, 16 KiB APK/ELF alignment, and the installed library hash passed. The production APK in the game folder was updated with the old APK retained as a diagnostic backup. The app was stopped, the tablet was verified asleep, and its original environment and settings matched their backups byte for byte. Snapshots, asynchronous replay and uniform-copy workers remain disabled in normal use.

### Follow-up upstream audit on 2026-10-29

Android was fast-forwarded again, from `c74b56d` to [`9d67edec2175ae5f4beadbd65f37eaa88105d132`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/9d67edec2175ae5f4beadbd65f37eaa88105d132). All local changes were checkpointed and stashed before the update, restored without conflicts, and left unstaged as before. The three additional commits add Super Mario Bros. Wonder's Android profile, upload CPU-written linear render targets from their storage, and exclude background/menu/text-entry holds from movie-decoder idle time. The Wonder status is the upstream maintainer's result, not a test of that game on this tablet.

The Apple source audit was refreshed from `a3d603a` through [`d8bc9210a5def1e390462fe4aee58f0e635cc05d`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/d8bc9210a5def1e390462fe4aee58f0e635cc05d). Its migratable-waker exemption was adapted to Android's existing bounded same-core handoff, and the initial main-thread affinity mask now names only its own logical core, following Horizon. Legacy/modern Chinese language-code comparisons were also ported without collapsing the system language table. Host regression checks compile the actual modified hooks: they exercise the full language matrix, invalid and unterminated codes, original table values, migratable and pinned wakers, host-owned peers, priority exclusions, the timeout bound, and completion of a pinned peer's work. All 57 root tests and 41 tool tests passed; debug and release builds compiled all 139 sources. No shader-chain tool changed in this integration.

Two headless gameplay windows of 600 frames passed uniform and memo verification. An on-screen unheld 60-target probe of the new release measured 856 presented intervals over 25.601 s: 33.44 fps, p95 33.34 ms and maximum 41.67 ms, with no intervals over 50 ms or red diagnostics. Steady main-command spans covered 20.21-20.45 ms with 3.13 ms between parts. This separate launch does not establish a causal frame-rate gain from the scheduler change. The main scene remains 720p; native 1080p60 is still unverified.

The upstream depth-only `DM_VK_UBWC=2` image policy was checked against the former mode 0 in four alternating launches of the same release, keeping the unheld 60 fps target and all other diagnostic settings fixed. Actual presentation rates were 33.44 / 34.59 / 33.75 / 34.58 fps (mode 0 / 2 / 0 / 2), with 33.34 ms p95 intervals, maxima below 41.67 ms and no red diagnostics. Complete mode-2 main-command spans covered 19.20-19.41 ms plus 3.05-3.19 ms between parts, about 1 ms shorter than the corresponding mode-0 windows. These are short Shrine tests, not sustained thermal or full-game results.

Depth-only mode 2 is now the general default. Depth/stencil views already use the image's original format, and image usage is built from that format's own supported features; the unused mutable-format and extended-usage flags can therefore be removed. The [Vulkan flag definitions](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkImageCreateFlagBits.html) describe those capabilities. Non-mutable depth images now use no image-format-list extension structure, preserving the Vulkan 1.1 creation path and any unrelated creation chain. Colour-image narrowing remains opt-in, and compressed images retain their original flags. The policy is based on image properties and has no game/build-ID branch; `DM_VK_UBWC=0` selects the former flags at startup. A regression check compiles the actual policy and checks all modes, compressed/colour cases, preservation of format, size, usage and unrelated flags, and creation-chain handling. All 58 root tests pass. Two more headless gameplay windows passed uniform/memo verification with the new default; the Shrine materials remained intact.

The resulting release (library SHA-256 prefix `adf0b782c47a6397`) measured 34.57 fps over 25.543 s with the unheld 60-target diagnostics, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 41.67 ms and no intervals over 50 ms. Its normal 30-target test measured 29.81 fps over 25.526 s, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 83.34 ms and seven intervals over 50 ms. A separate launch of the same binary with the former depth flags at the 30 target measured 29.84 fps, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 58.34 ms and also seven intervals over 50 ms. Neither phase had intervals over 100 ms or red diagnostics. This does not isolate the cause of the occasional long frames; the full logs show no new pipeline/shader compilation at those events. The 30 fps tail and the 1080p60 objective remain open work.

The final APK passed v2 signature verification and 16 KiB APK/ELF alignment, and its installed library matched the build hash. It replaces the production APK with the previous production file preserved in the game's diagnostic folder. The app was stopped, the screen was verified off, and the original environment and settings were restored byte for byte. The new depth policy is selected by the generic runtime default, without a per-title environment override.

### Button-font update and further Apple audit on 2026-10-29

Android was fast-forwarded from `9d67ede` to [`3e9d4940aeba6c21c55e9c062cbc31e4433c1753`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/3e9d4940aeba6c21c55e9c062cbc31e4433c1753). The update generates and packages the shared controller-icon font and includes fontTools in dependency checks and frozen builds. Local work was checkpointed before merging; conflicts in the data stage and its architecture table were resolved to preserve both embedded and external data modes. NSP/XCI handling and the existing controller/IME implementation remain in place. The Android front end now finds a packaged font under its actual data root; embedded packs redirect the generated external-font path to their prepared directory. A regression check builds both data modes and verifies that font files, FPS files and both environment entries survive together.

The Apple audit reached [`6625a93806b3633c1fb2efee2992eadde001367c`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/6625a93806b3633c1fb2efee2992eadde001367c). Its new Bastion work contributes general MGFX 5 effect extraction and audio parsing fixes: startup lists that fit multiple command-header layouts defer detection, SDK 5 sinks use 16-bit input indices, and malformed/truncated headers are rejected before field reads. These were adapted while retaining Android's audio output and the local Yaz0/archive optimizations. Tests compile the actual audio parser against the upstream SDK 4/5/6/16/21 startup, truncation and stereo-PCM cases; synthetic MGFX 5 and 8 effects exercise both shader stages, offsets, zero padding and oversized shader claims. The new Metal present-thread upload fix and Mac replay/regression tooling were audited but not transplanted as Android renderer code. No shader-chain tool or chain version changed, and these changes do not establish that Bastion works on Android.

Optional pass diagnostics now count omitted/invalid queries, protect completion-thread statistics, retain true pass ordinals, and permit bounded capture with `DM_VK_PASS_TIMES=2`. A new `DM_VK_PASS_CAPTURE` path requests 1-16 complete frames (default four); timestamp queries stop after that window. Raw same-queue timestamps and attachment dimensions are written to TSV. Normal builds allocate no pass query pool unless timing is requested. Checks cover query bounds across all slots, ordinal overflow, finite capture, completion after sampling stops, and concurrent accumulation/reporting. The textual sum of ordinal means is explicitly labelled and is not treated as a frame duration.

All 64 root tests and 41 tool tests passed; debug and release builds compiled 139 sources with zero failures. Two headless 600-frame gameplay windows passed uniform and descriptor-memo verification without red diagnostics. Headless captures showed 1024x576 main targets, so their timings are kept separate from on-screen results. An on-screen debug run with the unheld 60 fps target, two-image snapshots, segment timing and eight sampled frames measured 881 actual presented intervals over 25.526 s: 34.51 fps, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 41.67 ms and no intervals over 50 ms. This is similar to the previous release observations, not evidence of a speed increase from the upstream update.

All eight on-screen captures had contiguous pass ordinals and zero untimed, invalid or unranked samples; periodic extra passes make ordinal means unsuitable for total-frame accounting. Per-frame interval unions instead averaged 18.23 ms within timed passes and 5.53 ms between them, with a 23.75 ms mean envelope. The largest attachment groups were 1280x720 format 122 (5.74 ms per frame), 1280x720 format 37 (3.99 ms) and 640x360 format 37 (2.01 ms). Complete 600-frame main-command summaries measured 19.11-19.46 ms covered spans plus 3.08-3.22 ms between command-buffer parts, alongside 9.39-9.71 ms CPU replay. Pass and command-buffer spans measure different boundaries; neither is a hardware active-cycle counter, and TOP/BOTTOM pass timestamps can include work and dependencies from adjacent passes. In particular, a long 8x8 pass does not demonstrate that its own pixel shader is expensive. Attachment traffic, pass boundaries and synchronization remain priorities for investigation, while arithmetic versus bandwidth is still unresolved. Native 1080p60 remains unachieved.

The final release (library SHA-256 prefix `75a9ac68e3129be9`) was tested again with the original 30-target environment: 759 actual presented intervals over 25.551 s, 29.70 fps, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 75.00 ms, fourteen intervals over 50 ms and none over 100 ms. No red diagnostics appeared, but the occasional long frames remain unresolved. APK v2 signing and 16 KiB APK/ELF alignment passed; the installed APK matched the release byte for byte. The game-folder APK was updated with its previous version backed up. The icon font was generated and transferred separately, without retransferring game data. The app was stopped, the tablet was asleep, and the original environment and settings were verified byte for byte using `adb exec-out`.

### Depth-path attribution on 2026-10-29

Two general depth-access experiments passed host state-transition checks and two headless 600-frame cache-verification windows each, but established no presentation-rate improvement. The first replaced depth stores with `VK_ATTACHMENT_STORE_OP_NONE` only for proven read-only, depth-only passes on Vulkan 1.3, ending the pass before a later writing draw or clear. In particular, partial depth clears were kept writable; using NONE after any write would discard needed values according to the [Vulkan store-op definition](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkAttachmentStoreOp.html). The second additionally detached unused depth attachments while keeping logical attachment dimensions and restoring depth before a later testing draw. Both retained stencil-format fallbacks and used the same APK throughout their individual on/off/on comparisons.

| Experiment | Enabled, first | Disabled | Enabled, second | Outcome |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Read-only depth stores | 34.52 fps | 34.81 fps | 34.36 fps | No established benefit |
| Unused depth detachment | 34.38 fps | 34.41 fps | 34.55 fps | No established benefit |

Each phase measured about 25.6 seconds of actual SurfaceFlinger presentation times with the unheld 60 fps target, two-image snapshots and segment timing. All six phases had 33.34 ms p95 intervals, maxima below 41.67 ms, no intervals over 50 ms, and no red diagnostics. Live-control acknowledgements and conversion counters confirmed the tested modes; the disabled phases stopped accumulating conversions. Complete main-command envelopes remained around 22.4-22.7 ms. Both prototypes and their test were withdrawn, with patches, binaries, checks and measurements retained in the game's diagnostic directory. A rebuilt debug library matched the pre-experiment verified APK byte for byte.

The eight-frame capture identified a more useful lead after grouping by draw structure rather than ordinal: a nine-draw, full-screen, format-122 pass took 4.71-5.79 ms, usually about 4.73 ms. A subsequent trace identified its nine fragment shaders and showed every draw using the fragment-depth replacement variant. This variant deliberately writes `gl_FragDepth = gl_FragCoord.z` to bypass Adreno's incorrect early depth rejection after a shader writes the depth image; the existing renderer documents actual light/triangle losses in Hades II and Xenoblade Chronicles 3 without that protection. The sampled pass uses a D16 depth attachment, depth comparison EQUAL and no depth writes. The extracted SPIR-V modules contain 1,106-1,708 instructions, 8-11 static image-sampling instructions each, and no loop merges. Those static counts do not measure dynamic shader cost.

A diagnostic-only, same-debug-APK `DM_VK_FDZ=1/0/1` comparison then measured 34.71 / 47.06 / 34.46 fps over approximately 25.5 seconds per phase. Maximum intervals were 41.67 / 33.34 / 41.67 ms; none exceeded 50 ms. All phases had no red diagnostics. Complete disabled-variant GPU summaries covered 13.41-13.48 ms plus 2.97-2.99 ms between command-buffer parts, about 16.4 ms in total, against roughly 22.2-22.5 ms with the workaround. CPU replay stayed around 9.0-9.4 ms. The sampled Shrine screenshots retained visible scene materials, but three screenshots and a short static scene cannot rule out transient or other-game rendering failures. Disabling a known compatibility workaround is therefore not a validated general optimization, and its default remains enabled. The diagnostic result localizes a substantial avoidable cost if a correct alternative can be found; it does not establish 60 fps or native 1080p rendering.

After the attribution tests, all 64 root checks passed, the verified `75a9ac68e3129be9` release was restored, and the installed APK matched it byte for byte. The two experimental source files matched their pre-experiment checkpoint, the temporary test was removed, normal environment/settings were restored exactly, and the game was stopped with the tablet asleep. Measurements and the final restoration audit remain in the game's diagnostic directory.

### Read-only D16 depth snapshots on 2026-10-29

A general alternative to the costly fragment-depth workaround is now enabled for eligible D16 attachments. Before a read-only depth-testing pass that would use FDZ, the host copies the depth values into a separate image and uses that image for depth testing with the original fragment shader. Sampled texture bindings still reference the original image. A later depth-writing draw or depth clear ends the copied pass before updating the original; a partial depth clear also disqualifies that pass from copying. Deferred destruction keeps both the temporary view and its image alive through submission completion. Failed allocations and unsupported format features retain FDZ, and a failed attempt is not repeated for every draw of the same pass.

Eligibility is based on image and draw properties, without a title, build-ID, shader-hash or device branch: pure D16 depth, one level/layer/sample, the whole 2D attachment, transfer-source support, no protected image, resolve, depth clear, depth-writing shader or polygon offset, and a recent fragment-depth writer. Floating-point and stencil formats keep the existing compatibility path by default. `DM_VK_DEPTH_CLONE=0` selects the former FDZ path; diagnostic mode 1 also tries other pure-depth formats, while the default mode 2 admits D16 only. The global FDZ protection remains enabled.

A same-debug-APK, live-acknowledged mode-2/0/2 comparison measured **45.90 / 34.77 / 45.63 fps**, approximately a 32% improvement, over about 15.4 seconds per phase. Enabled p95 intervals were 25.00 ms, versus 33.34 ms disabled; all maxima were below 33.34 ms and no interval exceeded 50 ms. Complete enabled main-command envelopes measured 16.59-16.87 ms, compared with about 22.01 ms disabled; CPU and GPU work overlap. Three additional camera angles showed no obvious missing Shrine geometry or materials, and two 600-frame headless windows passed uniform and descriptor verification. These remain short, limited-scene observations.

A standalone Vulkan probe on the Adreno 840 executed the production copy helper in 512 cases: D16 and D32, all eight comparison functions and 32 depth patterns. Copied-depth rendering matched the FDZ reference byte for byte, and source-depth bytes remained unchanged. The original early-depth path also passed this probe: it does not reproduce or disprove the intermittent driver failures documented in other games. Host checks compile the production eligibility, allocation, clear and draw-transition code, exercising resource failures, ownership, suballocations, deferred destruction and retry suppression. The original game modules and shader bundle are unchanged. Native 1080p60 remains unachieved.

### Apple upstream audit through 123e3b4 on 2026-10-29

Fetching Android upstream found no commits after `3e9d494`. The Apple audit advanced from `6625a93` through [`8325eefbc68a935c5f684d4fbdff571e2540b4b4`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/8325eefbc68a935c5f684d4fbdff571e2540b4b4), twelve commits. The general SDK fixes in [`971d336`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/971d336516a20dde998f0ede29db7e380a8ee6dd) were adapted: snapshot-span success returns true, unresolved network errors return false, audio-device notification acquisition returns a Result and refuses a second acquisition, and the VR permission/refusal results agree. The existing Android Vulkan sampler already passes LOD bias; Metal argument blocks, controller motion reactivation, build stamps and iOS image-name case handling use different platform code and were not copied into the Android host.

A final fetch also inspected `920a846` (README footer styling) and [`123e3b4`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/123e3b4), which adds Baba Is You's profile and a short Mac level-00/save-reload check. These two commits do not change the shared host or renderer. The Mac profile has not been imported as an Android playable claim; it adds no demonstrated BOTW optimization. The audit therefore covers fourteen new Apple commits, while the validated Android runtime below retains the same build.

The architecture preflight from [`fae1db0`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/fae1db0) now runs after extraction in both packing paths, including every version of a game with a registered profile. A known A32 program is rejected before build-ID checks and rewriting, naming an available registered version; truncated META flags produce a clear error. This works on the extracted program regardless of NSP or XCI input. Tests cover all-version checking, supported and missing metadata, truncated flags and the Android pipeline stopping before later stages. Host tests exercise the actual modified hooks, including concurrent notification acquisition. All **71 root tests and 41 tool tests passed**, and both builds compiled 139 sources without failures. Two further headless 600-frame gameplay windows passed verification without red diagnostics.

The Metal-specific zero-3D texture fallback and the expanded capture/replay regression runner were inspected, but no equivalence with Android view binding or Android replay validation is claimed. The tool-lock refactor changes the Apple chain version; those chain tools were not transplanted, and no Android shader rebuild was required. Apple's new [BOTW depth-equal investigation](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/5d2bf73) documents nondeterministic edge pixels in long Metal passes, not a demonstrated Android performance fix; its temporary LESS_OR_EQUAL probe was not retained upstream and was not applied here.

The resulting release (`libdmhost.so` SHA-256 prefix `350cbbc7724c8f86`) measured **45.94 fps** with the unheld 60 fps target and two-image snapshots: 1,162 actual presented intervals over 25.293 s, p95 25.00 ms, maximum 33.34 ms and none over 50 ms. Its normal 30-target run measured **29.67 fps**: 758 intervals over 25.551 s, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 133.34 ms, seven intervals over 50 ms and one over 100 ms. Neither log had red diagnostics. An earlier release of the same depth-copy change before the SDK update measured 45.65 fps at the unheld target and 29.73 fps normally, with an occasional 108.34 ms interval. The 30-target tail remains unresolved; these separate launches do not attribute it to any one change. The main 3D scene remains 720p, and these results do not establish native 1080p or sustained/full-game performance.

APK v2 signing, 16 KiB APK and ELF load alignment, and the packaged library hash passed. The installed APK matched the release byte for byte. The production `ZeldaBreathOfTheWild.apk` was replaced, with its preceding version retained as `android/optimization/before-depth-clone-production.apk` in the game's build directory. The app was stopped, the tablet was verified dozing, and the original environment and settings were restored byte for byte. The D16 optimization is a runtime default; normal use still selects the original 30 fps configuration without diagnostic snapshots or asynchronous replay.

### Attribution after the D16 improvement on 2026-10-29

Asynchronous NVN replay was retested because the shorter GPU work could change its value. Two headless 600-frame verification windows passed, but a same-release-APK on/off/on comparison measured **44.01 / 45.56 / 44.19 fps**, with 25.00 ms p95 intervals, maxima below 33.34 ms and no intervals over 50 ms. The enabled timeline reported roughly 1.8-2.4 ms game gaps, 0.4-0.5 ms queue delay, 9.7-10.0 ms replay and 11.1-11.3 ms GPU tail; main GPU envelopes stayed near 16.7-16.9 ms in both modes. There was no established saving, so asynchronous replay remains off by default.

Optional `DM_VK_PRESENT_TIMES=1` now measures the final swapchain command buffer, which the main-command spans exclude. Five timestamp queries per in-flight presentation slot separate its initial dependency, clear/blit, overlay and final layout transition. Results are read after the slot's fence, require available queries, honor the queue's valid timestamp bits and reject invalid durations. Host-menu redraws are excluded. Query pools are created only when requested and supported and are destroyed after device idle when the swapchain is dropped. Host tests compile the actual query lifecycle and decoder against Vulkan headers, covering unavailable/failed results, 64-bit and shorter-counter wraparound, opt-in allocation, one-time reads, slot reuse and destruction. All 72 root tests passed and the debug build compiled 139 sources without failures.

A same-debug-APK query-on/off/on check measured 46.99 / 46.31 / 45.29 fps; this does not establish a speed change from instrumentation. Completed steady windows measured about **0.35 ms of final presentation GPU work** at 3040x1904 output: 0.309-0.316 ms clear/blit, 0.038-0.039 ms overlay, and about 0.001 ms each for the initial dependency and final layout. No timestamp samples were invalid. CPU slot/query work was around 0.008-0.009 ms, recording 0.145-0.155 ms, submission 0.060-0.068 ms, feedback 0.005 ms and queue presentation 0.183-0.196 ms. Acquire time varied between sampling windows. These measurements do not support treating the final scaling or controls overlay as a multi-millisecond bottleneck.

Segment diagnostics also record CPU submission setup, recording before the first queue call, and remaining recording. A live-acknowledged, same-debug-APK **128/64/128 draw-threshold** comparison measured **44.79 / 44.81 / 45.31 fps**. The smaller threshold increased command-buffer parts from about 6.7 to 8.8 without an established saving. CPU setup/slot waits were about 0.20-0.22 ms and first queue calls followed about 1.8-2.0 ms of recording. Complete main-plus-upload envelopes were about 16.8-17.3 ms; upload-buffer spans around 1.4 ms overlap other spans and must not be added to the main envelope. Steady combined coverage was about 15.5 ms with 1.6-1.7 ms between parts, including internal stalls. The original depth safety rules and 128-draw default are retained. All phases had no red diagnostics or intervals over 50 ms.

The existing **color-format narrowing** option (`DM_VK_UBWC=3`, retaining depth mode 2) produced a useful diagnostic result: **52.42 / 45.09 / 53.34 fps** in a same-release-APK 3/2/3 comparison. Complete enabled main-command envelopes were 14.52-14.90 ms, versus 16.77-17.05 ms disabled. All phases had 25.00 ms p95 intervals, maxima below 33.34 ms and no intervals over 50 ms or red diagnostics. Two headless verification windows passed, no format-substitution messages occurred in the sampled Shrine logs, and the screenshots showed no obvious missing geometry or materials. The virtual pad's visibility differed between screenshots because of input auto-hide; its separately measured presentation cost is small and excluded from the reported main-command envelopes. This remains diagnostic evidence, not a general correctness claim.

At that checkpoint, color narrowing was **not a default optimization**: the existing implementation substituted an image's original format when a requested reinterpretation view was outside its short format list. A general implementation must preserve those view semantics before enabling it broadly. The faster Shrine result is neither a delivered universal 53 fps mode nor evidence of native 1080p60. The original 720p main targets and 30-target normal configuration remain unchanged.

A further prototype removed only the unused `EXTENDED_USAGE` flag from uncompressed color images, preserving mutable compatible views and all other creation fields. Host contract checks and two headless 600-frame windows passed. With identical temporary pad settings, a same-debug-APK 6/2/6 comparison measured **45.67 / 45.67 / 45.85 fps**, with no red diagnostics or intervals over 50 ms. This did not recover the narrowing option's saving. The prototype and its test addition were withdrawn exactly to their checkpoint; its patch, APK and measurements remain in the game's diagnostic folder. The unsupported experimental mode is not retained in the runtime. Correct fallback for otherwise restricted format views remains the next color-compression work item.

After withdrawing that prototype, all **72 root checks passed**, and debug and release builds each compiled 139 sources without failures. The new optional diagnostics remain in source; the installed production APK is still the previously validated `350cbbc7724c8f86` library, matched byte for byte to the game-folder APK. No experimental runtime policy was promoted. Original environment/settings were restored exactly, both app and headless processes were stopped, and the tablet was verified dozing. The 30-target long-frame tail and native 1080p60 remain open.


### Upstream audit and compatible color compression on 2026-10-29

Android upstream advanced from `3e9d494` to [`ba91663`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/ba91663ff91d2b57c154918dfe010f15bdc81e7c), four commits. Its memory-pool binary search is already covered by the local interval index, including overlapping ranges and locked lookup; the new scan was not layered on top. The general fix from [`7d0841e`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/7d0841e) is adapted: adaptive lateness compares each displayed frame against the longer of the chosen period and the game's requested present hold. A deliberately held two-vsync frame is no longer counted late solely because the host targets one vsync. Tests still count a missed hold or discarded frame as late. The new game checklist and Wonder's updated device observations were inspected; they do not validate additional games on this tablet.

Apple upstream advanced from `123e3b4` to [`ac9598a`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/ac9598ad83e2706881568cafe345d262459ccd05), seven commits. The shared-host fix in [`137c229`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/137c2291955a968168a2c23fe34efa49592ef8a6) is adapted for controller, keyboard, account, Mii, shop and error applets: the game sees focus lost while the applet is open and focus restored when it closes, respecting its notification mode and the front end's actual lifecycle. Nested applets retain the outer applet's focus. Both controller-support overloads return the cancellation Result when fewer players than the requested minimum are connected. Host checks compile the production queue/focus code and scoped cleanup, cover nesting/background/early-return behavior, and exercise both controller overloads for all 256 player masks and minimum/maximum counts 0 through 8. The three new Mac game profiles, regression-runner changes, SDK-13 address-alignment cuts and Luigi's Mansion offline Metal archive setting were audited; no Android playability or BOTW speed benefit is claimed for them.

The Android upstream default-compression change in [`a1b4f54`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/a1b4f54) agrees with the earlier local color-narrowing measurements, but its fallback substitutes the original format and asks affected games to select depth-only mode. The local implementation instead keeps the requested compatible view format. When such a view is first needed, it creates unrestricted mutable storage of the same shape and base format, queues all prior writes, copies every mip and layer, queues the copy, invalidates cached views/descriptors and publishes the new physical image behind the same logical image. Later uploads follow the copy. Continuation submissions preserve conditional-render state, and attachment, graphics and compute bindings retry if their collection spans a promotion. Old views and storage are freed only after their deferred fence. Allocation failure preserves the old image and reports an error with a per-frame retry limit. Vulkan 1.1 without format-list support retains unrestricted color images, and failed narrowed creation retries the former creation info. `DM_VK_UBWC=3` is now the default; `2` retains unrestricted color images and `0` retains the former flags. No game-ID or shader-hash exception was added.

On the Adreno 840, a standalone Vulkan probe executing the production allocation/copy helpers passed **576 copies** and **448 partial writes through compatible reinterpretation views**, across eight formats, nine shapes, multiple mips/layers, cube faces, 3D slices and four-sample images. Source bytes stayed unchanged and copied/rewritten bytes matched. Floating-point image-to-buffer readback canonicalized some NaN payloads on both the former unrestricted path and the promoted path, so a separate integer-sampling compute probe checked the actual stored bits: **128 format/pattern cases, zero path or CPU-input bit mismatches**, including NaN/Inf/subnormal payloads. Probe shader and game data were separate; the game's shader bundle and rewritten modules were unchanged.

Two 600-frame game windows with diagnostic forced promotion passed descriptor/uniform verification: **904 images widened, zero failed promotions, no red diagnostics**. A further two normal mode-3 windows after the shared-host update passed with zero differences or red diagnostics and zero requested promotions in the sampled Shrine. Host checks compile the production creation/fallback block, allocation, copy, publication and continuation logic, covering failure ownership, suballocations, complete subresources, deferred lifetimes and preservation of logical dirty/alias/write history. All **77 root tests and 41 tool tests passed**, and debug/release builds each compiled 139 sources without failures.

Apple's current BOTW [render-scale description](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/blob/ac9598ad83e2706881568cafe345d262459ccd05/dm/games/01007EF00011E000/README.md#render-scale) defines 1600x900 docked and 1280x720 handheld scene bases, a separate 1920x1080 output, and a maximum scale of 1.0. Its [profile](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/blob/ac9598ad83e2706881568cafe345d262459ccd05/dm/games/01007EF00011E000/game.tsv) records 300 seconds at 60 fps on iPhone 17 Pro Max with snapshots and encoding work. This is a useful platform comparison, but it is not a documented native 1920x1080 scene-rendering result. The user's native 1080p60 objective remains open.


A controlled same-debug-APK **3/2/3** test with identical saved pad settings measured **53.17 / 45.78 / 52.67 fps**, about a 16% gain from narrowing. Each phase sampled roughly 25 seconds of actual SurfaceFlinger presentations after warm-up. All p95 intervals were 25.00 ms, maxima below 33.34 ms and no interval exceeded 50 ms; logs had zero red diagnostics and zero promotions in this scene. Complete main-command envelopes were about 14.5-14.8 ms enabled versus 16.9 ms disabled. One early first-run screenshot caught the loading fade completely black; it is not used as material validation, and later fixed-condition captures now wait through the ten-second warm-up. The second enabled capture retained visible Shrine geometry, materials and Link. These are limited-scene checks, not full-game visual proof.

The resulting release (library SHA-256 prefix `25d59e1fdde4fae9`) with the default compression policy measured **52.43 fps** at the unheld 60 target: 1,319 displayed intervals over 25.160 seconds, p95 25.00 ms, maximum 33.34 ms and none over 50 ms. Normal 30-target environment with the temporary hidden-pad test setting measured **29.63 fps**, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 108.34 ms, ten intervals over 50 ms and one over 100 ms. The long-frame tail therefore remains open. A first run labelled docked still had `docked=0` in saved settings, which overrides the environment; its 52.40 fps result is another handheld observation and is excluded from docked comparisons. Corrected docked settings and fully original normal settings are tested separately below.


With corrected saved `docked=1` settings, the release's unheld 60-target run measured **42.91 fps**, 1,075 intervals over 25.051 seconds, p95 25.00 ms, maximum 33.34 ms, no intervals over 50 ms and no red diagnostics. Main-command envelopes were about 17.71-17.94 ms. Startup target logs alone do not establish its steady scene resolution because target creation logging is bounded; a separate offscreen pass inspection follows. With both the **fully original environment and settings**, the final normal run measured **29.62 fps**, 743 intervals over 25.085 seconds, p95 33.34 ms, maximum 91.67 ms, eleven intervals over 50 ms and none over 100 ms. No red diagnostics appeared and the warmed screenshot retained the scene materials. The occasional 30-target hitches remain unresolved.

APK v2 signing, 16 KiB ZIP/ELF load alignment and packaged-library equality passed. The installed release matched the new game-folder APK byte for byte (APK SHA-256 prefix `d7066ce4edcb3a67`, library prefix `25d59e1fdde4fae9`). Its predecessor is preserved as `android/optimization/before-color-promotion-production.apk` in the game's build folder. The original shader bundle, rewritten game modules and primary saves were not replaced. The audited shared logging serialization and Mac watchdog `dump` command from `1191af5` were not transplanted; the runtime integration covers the focus and pacing fixes described above, not a wholesale merge of both repositories.


The offscreen copied-save docked inspection completed two further 600-frame verification windows with zero memo differences or red diagnostics and no requested color promotions. Its major geometry and depth passes were still **1280x720**, with 896x896 shadow passes and a separate 1920x1080 output. Thus the on-screen docked observation above is not a verified 900p measurement. The loading-transition timing summary included 291 invalid pass timestamp samples; both steady summaries had zero untimed, invalid or unranked samples. Ordinal mean sums are not used as whole-frame GPU cost. This confirms that simply selecting docked mode does not establish native 1080p rendering on this build.

At final audit the installed and production APKs agreed, the app and offscreen process were stopped, the tablet was dozing, and original environment/settings matched the immutable backups byte for byte. Normal configuration still explicitly requests `DM_FPS_TARGET=30`, whereas the game's source profile has `fps_target 60`; the 52.43 fps observation uses a temporary unheld 60-target diagnostic. These changes improve the measured rendering path but do not complete native 1080p60 or resolve the normal-mode long-frame tail.


### Frame-target and fixed-scale attribution on 2026-10-29

The original Switch game's 30 fps and this port's target are separate settings. The shared BOTW profile has `fps_target 60`; its Android bring-up `android.tsv` explicitly supplies `DM_FPS_TARGET=30`. The packer's `--fps60` option, also exposed by the GUI checkbox, overrides that row with 60. The installed normal environment therefore deliberately requests 30, and its occasional long frames remain unresolved. The Android profile and Chinese README now explain this distinction.

A read-only, build-ID-gated inspection of the running copied-save process resolved the docked-resolution uncertainty. For main build `cd57b23fa4bbad65803d9788c01821ee`, the system mode was 1, output width 1920, base scene width 1600, dynamic scale 0.8 and both view scale ratios about 0.666667: the game's own dynamic resolution reduces the docked scene to 1280x720. The existing render-scale stubs are present in the rewritten module. Their default 1.0 deliberately preserves dynamic resolution. The supported diagnostic scale 0.999 instead fixed both view ratios at 0.8325, producing **1598x899** main geometry and 799x449 smaller depth targets. This is close to 900p, not an exact 1600x900 or 1080p result. No game-memory write or new per-title runtime branch was used by the inspector; it only reads fields from that known build.

An offscreen same-process `DM_VK_X_NOBAR=0/1/0` diagnostic did not establish a useful benefit from omitting the extra pass-start barriers. Two completed 600-frame GPU envelopes in each phase were 29.61/29.89, 29.63/29.33 and 29.97/29.99 ms; replay stayed around 21 ms. These offscreen conditions are not the app's on-screen throughput and cannot be compared as absolute performance. The diagnostic is not a synchronization-correctness proof, and normal barriers remain enabled.

A depth trace found seven eligible copies per steady frame: six from one smaller D16 image and one from a larger D16 image. A prototype reused only allocation/view storage by size/format, always copying the current source values before each read-only pass. It retained at most eight entries and 32 MiB of requested image storage, deferred evictions view-before-image and flushed entries when disabled. Host checks exercised actual allocation/copy code, own/suballocated memory, failures, repeated reuse, byte/count bounds and LRU retirement. Two headless 600-frame uniform/descriptor verification windows had no differences or red diagnostics, and about 7.6 MiB of resources served over 17,000 reuse hits. A standalone Adreno 840 probe used the production helpers for **1,024 copies across 512 D16/D32 cases**, with a depth overwrite and second reuse within each command buffer: copied rendering matched FDZ and source-depth checks passed. As before, the source early-depth path passed too, so this does not reproduce the other games' known intermittent LRZ issue.

A same-debug-APK **reuse-on/off/on** comparison at the fixed 0.999 scale measured **42.33 / 42.15 / 43.16 fps**, roughly 25.6 seconds per phase. All p95 intervals were 25.00 ms, maxima below 41.67 ms, no interval exceeded 50 ms and logs had no red diagnostics. A separate four-frame pass capture after each timed sample verified the main 754-draw geometry pass was 1598x899 throughout. This does not establish a repeatable saving large enough to retain the extra cache. The trace and cache prototype were withdrawn exactly to their source checkpoint, including their test changes; its patch, debug APK, standalone probe and captures remain outside the repository in the game's diagnostic folder. A fixed resolution near 900p therefore currently measures about 42-43 fps in this short Shrine scene, not 60.

After withdrawal, all **77 root checks passed**; debug and release builds each compiled 139 sources without failures. Both rebuilt libraries matched their pre-experiment APKs byte for byte. The installed app was restored to the verified production release, matching the game-folder APK (`d7066ce4edcb3a67` / library `25d59e1fdde4fae9`). Original environment and settings were restored exactly, app and headless processes were stopped, and the tablet was dozing. Fixed docked 900p60 is a useful intermediate validation stage; native 1080p60 remains the user's final objective and remains unachieved.

### Android and Adreno capability checks on 2026-10-29

The stock Adreno 840 driver exposes `VK_QCOM_tile_memory_heap` with 14,929,920 bytes (about 14.24 MiB), `queueSubmitBoundary=false` and `tileBufferTransfers=true`. An isolated, display-off resource probe successfully bound 19 tile images across D16, D32, RGBA8, packed HDR and RGBA16F formats and several sizes. RGBA16F at 1920x1080 did not fit; adding image transfer source/destination usage was rejected for all five formats. Thus the current transfer-based depth clones cannot simply be allocated in that heap. Its contents also expire at submission-batch boundaries, and the renderer submits parts early. These are resource/lifetime constraints to solve before a backend experiment, not evidence of a speed gain. See the [Khronos extension description](https://docs.vulkan.org/features/latest/features/proposals/VK_QCOM_tile_memory_heap.html). No tile-memory path is enabled in production.

The device supports ADPF graphics-pipeline sessions, with at most five threads, but not native-surface binding or automatic CPU/GPU timing. A stock-driver clock probe found device and `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` timestamp domains with 48 valid GPU bits; 100 calibrations had at most 260 ns reported uncertainty and no host-bracket failures. An optional prototype used calibrated final-presentation timestamps after completed fences and reported the end-to-end work span separately from overlapping CPU/GPU components, following the [Android NDK contract](https://developer.android.com/ndk/reference/group/a-performance-hint). It did not add a blocking GPU wait. Missing capabilities, headless/asynchronous attribution and repeated report failures retained or restored CPU-only hints. All 78 prototype root checks passed, and its 139-source debug build succeeded.

A same-APK graphics-session **on/off/on** comparison at the fixed 0.999 docked scale measured **42.09 / 43.18 / 42.24 fps** over 25.54 / 25.22 / 25.03 seconds. All maxima were below 33.34 ms, with no intervals over 50 ms or red diagnostics. The first phase's requested post-measurement pass capture was unavailable because its environment omitted pass timing; the display measurement and runtime log were retained. Corrected post-measurement captures in the off and final on phases confirmed 1598x899 main geometry. Accepted steady graphics reports measured about 34.3-34.7 ms end-to-end, 23.2-23.5 ms CPU and 17.8-18.4 ms GPU; these overlapping measurements must not be summed or equated with frame throughput. The session did not improve performance and was withdrawn exactly to its source checkpoint. The patch, APK and comparison remain in the game's diagnostic folder. The rebuilt debug library again matches the stable debug APK byte for byte, and the production release was reinstalled with original settings. CPU-only ADPF remains the normal path. Android's game manager lists only standard/custom modes for this package; no performance-mode intervention was applied. Native 1080p60 remains open.


### Depth-pass submission and pool-cap crash on 2026-10-29

The retained generic Vulkan submission policy splits eligible render passes after 128 draws and submits their recorded prefixes early. On Qualcomm devices the depth path is automatic only for the existing LRZ/FDZ workaround configuration and single-sample, single-mip D16/D32 depth-only attachments with transfer support, no resolve, a valid base-level view and one rendered layer. A single-layer view of an array is eligible; layered rendering, stencil, multisampling, mip views, transform feedback and raster discard stay whole. Existing LRZ touches and attachment LOAD/STORE semantics remain intact. `DM_VK_PASS_SPLIT=0` restores whole passes; `DM_VK_PASS_SPLIT_DEPTH=0` holds depth passes whole. The command-buffer budget reserves slots for the remaining ordinary cuts.

An Adreno 840 differential probe compared whole 512-draw passes with four early 128-draw submissions using the production LRZ touch and barriers. All 1,792 color/depth comparisons passed across formats, compare functions, depth writers, depth bias, oversized depth images and individual views of three-layer arrays; untouched array layers were checked too. Two full uniform/descriptor verification windows passed. A same-APK fixed-0.999-scale on/off/on display comparison measured 59.28 / 42.70 / 58.98 fps at 1598x899. The default automatic array policy later measured 59.74 fps in a short static Shrine sample at the game's actual 1600x900 docked target, with 16.668 ms p99, a 25.001 ms maximum and no intervals over 50 ms. These are intermediate scene-specific measurements, not native 1080p60 validation.

Release pacing still needs investigation: otherwise similar short release samples measured 51.58 fps with the saved FPS counter off and 59.45 with it on. A causal explanation has not been established. An untested, default-off final-pass diagnostic was withdrawn before the pool fix. The user's subsequent navigation used the original handheld/30-target settings and crashed during progression at about 189 seconds: the direct fatal was `[vk] too many memory pools`, from the backend's fixed 4,096-slot registry. The earlier BufferInitialize bounds warning does not itself terminate the process.

The registry now grows its slot table and interval index as needed, while pool records retain their addresses across growth for returned pointers and thread-local caches. Removal retains the previous generation invalidation, swap-delete ordering, deferred GPU buffer retirement, zero-copy copy-back and multi-chunk release. Optional `DM_VK_POOL_LOG=1` records add/remove serials and live counts without backtraces. Host regression checks exercise 12,000 live pools, 8,000 removal/reuse cycles, cached lookup after swap-removal, all chunks of a guest pool, exactly-once retirement and allocation failure before and after the initial allocation. All 78 root tests pass; debug and release each compile 139 sources without failures. A copied-save progression run crossed the former limit and reached 4,353 peak / 4,341 current live pools, with 4,856 additions, 515 removals, no unmatched removal serials and no duplicate live address ranges; no fatal or red diagnostics occurred. Subsequent copied-save navigation reached the Great Plateau. Committed and working primary saves were backed up first and were not replaced. The final native 1080p60 objective remains open.


The first long Shrine-to-exit run exposed saturated blue/green lighting at the stairs. Turning pass splitting off live did not remove it. Reloading a diagnostic save at that same location with color narrowing disabled restored neutral/warm colors, but the reverse control with color narrowing enabled also rendered without the saturated artifact. Thus the observations do **not** isolate color narrowing as its cause; fresh-load comparisons cannot validate the prior transition history. An untested 3D-image exclusion was withdrawn exactly. The persistent visual defect and the yellow/brown outdoor sky remain open correctness checks. Rendering is not considered validated solely because cache verification logs contain no differences or the process no longer crashes.

### Texture layout and five-project source audit on 2026-10-29

The source audit was pinned to Android [`421fe067`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/421fe067d25b9951547e9487e4f8c8ebc82b9664), Apple [`f78eec79`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/f78eec796ff180b8453f41c7864df4be2be572a0), nx2apk [`d0b52674`](https://github.com/ghostyshell/nx2apk/commit/d0b5267420e7c633f092ef00fffbba065dac997e), Taikou5-mobile [`ed52763c`](https://github.com/wdjwxh/Taikou5-mobile/commit/ed52763cb2c2b142c2023fc9a1373867a9b435c1), and d2r-mobile [`1e0e7934`](https://github.com/jhaizhou-ops/d2r-mobile/commit/1e0e7934c9ccdf95494aee5065d1d07ab3cc3918). These are reviewed snapshots, not five repository merges. The two newest Android commits concern read-only RomFS exact-name lookup and repair of external-directory ownership; neither is a new GPU optimization, and neither has been transplanted in this audit.

The Apple texture-layout implementation exposed a concrete generic gap. Before the change, only 9 of 22 original-NVN-driver shape cases matched: packaged 3D block depth, packaged 2D block height, standalone LINEAR_RENDER_TARGET, linear block-compressed pixel rows, MINIMAL_LAYOUT padding, mip/layer strides and sparse tile shape were affected. `nvn_tex.c` now follows the reviewed upstream layout, with its existing deko3d and driver-oracle attribution retained. The public descriptor layout is unchanged; the header adds the sparse-tile query. Block-linear copy helpers reject either linear flag, including LINEAR_RENDER_TARGET alone. The regression compiles the actual layout/deswizzle functions and checks 23 driver observations, mip/layer swizzle round trips, guard bytes and linear-target rejection. All **79 root tests pass**; debug and release each compile all 139 sources without failures. No shader-chain tool or chain version changed.

A signed diagnostic debug APK with both the pool-cap fix and texture-layout correction is installed. The game-folder production APK remains the older `d7066ce4edcb3a67` build; it has not been silently promoted. Initial on-screen tests loaded a copied Great Plateau save and captured system screenshots both before and after raw framebuffer capture. The sampled trees/rocks had natural green/gray colors without the earlier neon bands. This is a short observation, not proof that transition-dependent corruption is fixed: a previous binary also produced clean reloads. A prior native system screenshot independently captured the blue/white scenery and wide yellow/green light bands, so that earlier defect cannot be dismissed as raw-image decoding. The outdoor sky still needs an equivalent time/weather reference.

The first native comparisons used an external shell-owned save clone and correctly mounted its committed copy read-only after failing to clear its working directory. Subsequent native validation uses a separately seeded, app-owned internal test-save directory. Primary committed and working saves were backed up before testing; committed primary-file hashes and the restored normal environment/settings were checked. No test progression replaces the primary save.

The other projects provide narrower, useful reference points:

- [nx2apk's plan](https://github.com/ghostyshell/nx2apk/blob/d0b5267420e7c633f092ef00fffbba065dac997e/PLAN.md) reports DMC at 720p and about 60 fps on a Mali-G76, but about 48 fps in its heavy 1600x900 scene. Its later dynamic-resolution controller targets 45 fps down to 480 lines; its projection/resolution rewrite is title-specific. Async replay, conservative uniform extents and immutable-texture handling are useful comparisons, subject to object lifetime rules; these figures do not validate native 1080p60 here.
- [Taikou5-mobile's validation](https://github.com/wdjwxh/Taikou5-mobile/blob/ed52763cb2c2b142c2023fc9a1373867a9b435c1/docs/VALIDATION.md) distinguishes actual partial CPU/GPU texture readback/merge fixtures from scenes where that path never ran. Its fence/arena lifetime discipline and preservation of untouched GPU texels are worth adapting to this backend. The C++ engine is different from the Android C backend and cannot be substituted as a small patch. Its early menu frame rates came from an Android emulator; later phone input/save checks do not establish BOTW outdoor performance.
- [d2r-mobile's Android results](https://github.com/jhaizhou-ops/d2r-mobile/tree/1e0e7934c9ccdf95494aee5065d1d07ab3cc3918#android-new-in-v20) report median 60 fps in camp and two fights on Adreno 730, with a 3D-scale ceiling of 0.85 and dynamic reductions. Its render-scale writes target that game's renderer and are not a general native-1080p solution. The project also records an unresolved Adreno 830 lighting artifact. Its [writeback work](https://github.com/jhaizhou-ops/d2r-mobile/blob/1e0e7934c9ccdf95494aee5065d1d07ab3cc3918/docs/android-notes/2026-10-18-writebacks.md) and [first-play pipeline seed comparison](https://github.com/jhaizhou-ops/d2r-mobile/blob/1e0e7934c9ccdf95494aee5065d1d07ab3cc3918/docs/android-notes/2026-10-22-first-play-stutter.md) are useful general methods. This backend already uses imported AHardwareBuffer pools, written-range copy-back, submission-scoped descriptor epochs and pipeline caches; those costs need measurement before adding duplicate mechanisms. D2R's quad/32-lane warp corrections target specific subgroup instructions. A read-only census of all **19,592** shaders in the current BOTW bundle found none of the core subgroup opcodes they rewrite, so there is no evidence for transplanting those rewrites as this game's color fix.

Apple's first-use CPU-written compressed-texture handling and alpha-test work remain separate candidates. The latter includes shader-chain changes and cannot be integrated only at the HLE layer against the existing shader bundle. No untested first-use upload, subgroup rewrite, skipped draw, dynamic-resolution policy or title-specific brightening was enabled by this audit. Native 1080p60 and sustained outdoor graphics correctness remain open objectives.

The app-owned clone subsequently passed atomic save commits with no red diagnostics. A first movement script walked off the cliff and reached the game's Game Over screen; its mixed gameplay/menu presentation sample is excluded. A replacement route used short forward/back movements and camera turns without leaving the plateau edge. System screenshots before, during and after the route contained no recurrence of the earlier neon bands; long traversal and exact time/weather color matching are still unverified. The debug diagnostic run measured 643 actual presentation intervals over 24.351 seconds, averaging **26.41 fps**, with a 33.335 ms median and a 50.003 ms maximum. Screenshots and diagnostic logging were active, and the default handheld scene settings were retained with a temporary 60 fps target: this is neither a clean production benchmark nor a native-1080p measurement. Steady renderer windows reported roughly 13.2-13.7 ms replay and 18.0-18.3 ms GPU busy; those overlapping components must not be added as a complete frame time. Both primary save trees remained byte-identical (16 files total), original environment/settings were restored exactly, the app stopped and display state OFF was verified.

### First-use NVN storage content on 2026-10-29

A concrete Vulkan omission explained the sky and lighting anomalies. The shared HLE already records CPU-flushed pool ranges (`nvn_flushed`); Apple's `tex_init_fill` uses them to seed private images, but the Android path only transitioned other new images from UNDEFINED to GENERAL. Their prepopulated bytes never reached the image. Device logs confirmed the game's 256x32x16, format-0x29 sky table in a CPU_CACHED pool. Previously clean reloads were not proof of correctness: undefined image content could also produce a gray/yellow sky or saturated lighting.

The Vulkan texture path now uploads initial storage once for eligible color textures in prepopulated CPU_NO_ACCESS/CPU_UNCACHED pools or explicitly CPU-flushed cached storage. Packaged and linear paths keep their existing handling; multisampling, depth/stencil, virtual/sparse storage and COMPRESSIBLE targets are excluded. Storage bounds and pointer/offset agreement are checked before reading. An explicitly flushed all-zero texture is valid content too. The one-time upload does not install dirty-page protection or mark the image as a repeatedly uploaded packaged texture. Subsequent GPU writes and later NVN objects sharing that initialized image retain its contents. The UNDEFINED-layout check also covers an image first allocated outside a submission and used later. `DM_NVN_FLUSH_FILL=0` disables the flushed-cached branch for cold-start comparison.

The new regression executes the actual production policy/upload functions and real NVN layout/swizzle/deswizzle helpers against recorded Vulkan copy arguments.
