It checks 2D and 3D mip chains, array layers, compressed blocks, explicit zero contents, tracked versus untracked metadata, shared-image/GPU ownership, invalid storage bounds and excluded resource classes. This is a CPU test, not an independent GPU integration fixture. All **80 root tests pass**; debug and release each compile 139 sources without failures. APK v2 signing and 16 KiB ZIP/ELF LOAD alignment pass. No shader-chain tool or bundle changed.

An on/off/on comparison used the same debug APK and three identical app-owned outdoor save clones, with color narrowing and pass splitting kept at their defaults. System screenshots were captured before any raw framebuffer readback. **On:** blue sky and natural scenery, with the sky-table initial upload explicitly logged. **Off:** the previous blue/white scenery and large green/yellow light bands reappeared. **On again:** blue sky and natural scenery returned, including after camera turns and short movement. All phases had zero red/fatal diagnostics. This provides causal evidence for the first-use fix in the reproduced scene, rather than attributing it to color-format narrowing or weather. Long traversal, every weather/time condition and full-game graphics remain unverified.

The 25-second actual-presentation samples measured 33.34 / 38.39 / 35.18 fps. The disabled phase renders wrong and is not a valid performance alternative. Post-measurement captures contained four complete, contiguous frames per phase and confirmed 1280x720 main geometry, not 1080p. Sampled pass spans included substantial gaps between timed passes; neither their sum nor a single GPU-busy average establishes a complete CPU/GPU bottleneck. Before this fix, a stripped release with no captures during timed phases measured 34.02 fps stationary and 31.50 during Android-joystick movement, with no intervals over 50 ms; those are separate baseline observations, not a controlled speed gain.

A further outdoor characterization enabled asynchronous replay, two-image present buffering, the acquire-replayed opt-out and segment timing together. It measured 23.37 fps over 25.08 seconds; subsequent two-window uniform/descriptor verification passed without differences or red diagnostics. The combination did not help, and its several changes do not isolate the cost of any one option. These options remain disabled normally. Both primary save trees still matched their pre-test hashes (16 files) after the debug experiments. Native 1080p60 remains unachieved.

The corrected stripped release was then tested with the original controller/overlay preferences and the FPS counter off. Its temporary 60-target samples measured **31.45 fps stationary** over 25.03 s and **32.40 fps moving** over 25.03 s; the moving sample's maximum was 41.67 ms with no intervals over 50 ms. The normal 30-target movement sample measured **29.67 fps** over 25.04 s, with a 125.01 ms maximum, twelve intervals over 50 ms and one over 100 ms. That occasional long-frame problem remains open. No captures or pass timing ran during these timed release phases. System screenshots before and after movement retained blue sky and natural scenery, and both release logs had no red/fatal diagnostics. These measurements use handheld settings; the 1280x720 dimensions above came from the separate diagnostic pass captures, not a query inside the release benchmark.

The game-folder `ZeldaBreathOfTheWild.apk` and installed package now match the corrected release byte for byte (APK SHA-256 `e8ec5cd85e92be59f476d14100450eb1db5835640c6a39e729f569c89da1de26`). The preceding production APK was preserved as `android/optimization/before-initial-content-production-d7066ce4.apk`. This release includes the growable pool registry, reviewed texture layout and first-use content fix. The profile's tested-status row now describes these outdoor results instead of presenting the earlier static Shrine result as its current performance. Both primary save trees were checked after release testing and remained unchanged (16 files). The original environment/settings were restored exactly, the app is stopped and display OFF is verified. No asynchronous replay/buffering experiment or temporary 60-target setting was made a normal preference.

### Outdoor pacing, buffer lookup and native-1080p probe on 2026-10-29

A private diagnostic build has now rendered the copied Great Plateau scene with its **main 3D attachment at 1920x1080**, not only a 1080p window. Four complete consecutive pass captures showed the main format-37 attachment and 1920-wide draw bounds, with 5,872 main-pass draws across those frames. The 20.776-second actual-presentation sample averaged **53.91 fps**, with a 25.001 ms p95, 33.335 ms maximum and no intervals over 50 ms. Short movement and camera turns completed without red/fatal diagnostics; two additional 600-frame sampled buffer/uniform/descriptor verification windows reported no differences. This is an intermediate static result, not sustained moving 1080p60 or full-game graphics validation.

That build temporarily allowed a 1.2 render factor in the existing view-scale mechanism and checked every initialized texture's storage against its pool before use. The scene reached gameplay without a bounds failure. The four captures, rather than a screenshot's output dimensions, establish the main rendering size. A separate fresh clone with the same APK at fixed 0.999 docked scale measured **53.67 fps** over 20.701 seconds at 1598x899. This short comparison suggests that reducing pixel count alone does not remove the current scene's limit; it does not identify a complete CPU/GPU critical path. The probe used the existing `-O 3` build option without fast-math. No controlled compiler speedup has been established. Both temporary source changes were restored byte for byte, and neither the enlarged render-scale limit nor O3 has been adopted as a production default.

Pacing was a larger measured effect than the lookup cache below. A same-APK comparison on fresh identical internal clones, with the FPS counter off and fixed 1278x719 main targets, measured **31.44 fps** with the normal adaptive hold and **53.86 fps** with `DM_FPS_HOLD=off`. The latter retained real-time game progression (0.998 and 1.000 of wall time in logged windows) and held one vsync per frame. The normal adaptive policy had selected two-vsync holds after its 60 fps trials failed. Its reported GPU envelope can include work-supply gaps; the requested acquire delay is included in `@work_ms`. These fields must not be added as independent execution costs or described as an unexplained 15 ms GPU stall. The normal hold policy and saved 30-target preferences remain unchanged.

An on-device 20-second simpleperf user-cycle capture recorded 14,040 samples with none lost; 63.76% of call chains had unwind errors, so inclusive attribution is limited. The replay path, descriptor collection and buffer lookup were visible costs. The simultaneous display sample averaged 31.77 fps; per-frame means were 13.73 ms NVN replay, 17.98 ms GPU envelope and 15.37 ms requested acquire wait. Off-CPU profiling was unavailable: this device exposes no usable `sched:sched_switch` tracepoint. The build already targets API 31 with native TLS; emulated-TLS removal is not an applicable optimization here.

NVN diagnostic configuration is now applied once on the first function lookup, after the Android front end loads its environment. Previously constructor-time reads silently missed `DM_NVN_LOG`/`DM_NVN_PROF` from env.txt. The change preserves original, big-stack and present thunks, wraps each implemented entry only once and uses an explicit profiling-enabled flag for summaries. A regression exercises concurrent late lookup, disabled modes and wrapper ownership. The device confirmed 416 timing wrappers and per-function reports with profiling enabled; normal calls remain unwrapped when it is disabled.

The Vulkan buffer-range query now has a 64-entry per-thread exact-query cache. It copies the buffer handle, offset and available length, never a mutable registry pointer. Both address and normalized requested length are keys: overlapping registrations may have a different winning slot for a longer range. The registry's release/acquire generation invalidates snapshots after registration or removal, including swap-delete. Failed lookups retain the previous output contract. `DM_VK_BUFFER_CACHE=0` restores locked lookup; mode 2 checks one in 64 hits against the canonical range index, comparing only within the same generation and repairing a mismatch. The new regression covers a linear overlap oracle, zero/overflow bounds, collisions, swap-delete, cross-thread handoff, four concurrent readers during 5,000 registry mutations and a deliberately corrupted cached offset. All **82 root tests pass**.

In a fixed-1598x899 same-process on/off/on sample, display rates were 53.05 / 52.44 / 57.15 fps; these alone do not establish the size of a cache gain. A closer off/on/on/off comparison measured 53.12 / 51.52 / 56.62 / 54.56 fps. Its per-phase replay means were 11.617 / 11.474 / 11.252 / 11.591 ms: the mean replay saving was about **0.24 ms (2.1%)**, while average display throughput changed little. The first test completed 118,494 sampled canonical buffer checks with zero differences; the native-1080p probe completed 216,023 with zero differences. No red/fatal diagnostics occurred. The cache is enabled by default after replay configuration for this modest CPU saving, not advertised as a native-1080p60 solution. The preceding verified release remains the published APK while the updated release is prepared.

A subsequent read-only upstream review reached Android [`1e30ac83`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/1e30ac8335bbc9afb1e8d909b4ed13f9c4bc972d) and Apple [`32b12b4d`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/32b12b4de075f13fa8658b980f164c992e6136a1). Android's intervening changes include NSZ/XCZ input, lower-left-origin presentation, SDK allocator/tail-call fixes, additional title profiles and audio diagnostics. Apple's include vertex-stream zero padding, Unreal texture-pool handling and shader-chain fixes. These changes were inspected selectively and were not merged wholesale. No shader-chain tool or shader bundle changed during this work. Native 1080p60 remains open.

The next private native-1080p comparison used a live 32/64 command-buffer capacity and 128/64-draw quantum. The 32/128, 64/128, 64/64 and repeated 32/128 phases measured 50.73 / 53.73 / 50.43 / 51.79 fps, all at the same captured 1920x1080 main size. A smaller quantum increased replay cost; the larger capacity showed a modest possible gain, not 60 fps. All four temporary source edits were restored, including the segment-timestamp capacity. A separate asynchronous-replay-only probe, with the adaptive hold disabled and normal acquire safety retained, measured 37.83 fps. It is not enabled normally.

An attempted synchronous follow-up instead caught an intermittent startup crash. The exact APK library and return instructions identified `df_add` at library offset `0x102d44`, reached through `vk_defer_free_buffer` / `mtl_pool_del` / `h_nvnMemoryPoolFinalize`. It stored through a null deferred-list pointer while the observed count/capacity were already nonzero. Source review exposed missing exclusion: pool updates from game workers skipped `g_sub_mu` in synchronous mode, and neither synchronous nor asynchronous presentation took that gate although presentation also changes the backend's active submission. The observed inconsistent slot fields are consistent with that race; an allocation failure or a game-specific pool rule is not the demonstrated cause.

Resource mutation/readback now always takes the shared replay gate, and both presentation paths use a gated `present_now` helper. Submission retains its existing gate; fence, retirement and image-content rules are unchanged. A native regression executes the actual present helper and physical-pool finalizer on competing threads in both replay modes. It checks exclusion, return tickets, pool and alias retirement, and notification cleanup; a negative control restoring the old synchronous bypass fails. Three subsequent diagnostic game boots completed without red/fatal diagnostics. This is not a long-duration stress guarantee.

The user's report that Link's lower legs disappear into the ground is a separate unresolved correctness issue. A copied-save 30-target run reproduced it while standing. A thumb-stick press entered a distinct crouch pose and displayed the crouch tutorial, so crouching does not explain the original standing image. The symptom remained after moving, jumping/landing and looking down from above. Fixed-pass, original-depth, float-depth and uncached-binding comparisons did not remove it. A cold `DM_FPS_TARGET=0` run retaining the game's original step also reproduced it, including with pass splitting, depth clones, FDZ and LRZ touches disabled together. Disabling depth produced an unusable overdraw image, not a validated fix. The actual collision position and the rendering cause have not yet been separated. Earlier sky/lighting observations must not be read as validation of character-ground contact or full graphics.

The later upstream refresh reached Android [`794a9abb`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/794a9abb471345f535c6081f7fc4a42c57606346); Apple remained at `32b12b4d`. The Android delta mostly adds themed overlays, opacity/layout editing, audio accounting and lifecycle fixes. The focused [`ce52af23`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/ce52af23d1838a638347d8cb9ec71f34e76ffdac) lifecycle fix is now adapted: background entry passes through OutOfFocus, Android's window-focus callback restores InFocus, and notify-only mode reports OutOfFocus behind HOME. The existing pre-start background tracking and nested-applet focus handling are retained. The existing regression now checks notify-only behavior and the full HOME/resume sequence across all modes. All **83 root tests pass**; debug and release each compile 139 sources. An actual HOME/resume cycle logged 2/3/2/1 focus transitions and accepted a jump afterward. The theme overhaul and Apple's shader-chain changes have not been imported.


The subsequent bias/clip diagnostic also reproduced the standing defect with polygon offset disabled and with zero-to-one clip control. Its two temporary backend overrides were withdrawn byte for byte; their APK remains a private probe. No shader-chain or driver-format rule was changed on that evidence.

The updated stripped O2 release was then tested with normal handheld/30-target settings, the FPS counter off, no pass captures or per-frame TSV, and a fresh internal save clone. The 25.535-second moving display sample averaged **29.57 fps**, p95 33.335 ms, p99 50.003 ms and maximum **208.345 ms**, with eight intervals over 50 ms and one over 100 ms. The long-frame problem remains unresolved; this is not a demonstrated performance gain over the preceding release. HOME/resume restored focus (2/3/2/1), followed by accepted jump input. There were no red/fatal diagnostics, and the lower-leg rendering defect remained visible.

The installed package and game-folder production APK now match release SHA-256 'f8d8dfbca687b88aafadd5118e73e233e3f020257a49c02caab8767464812917'. The previous release is retained as 'android/optimization/before-focus-resource-production-e8ec5cd8.apk'. The new release includes the exact-query cache, resource-state exclusion and focused upstream lifecycle fix; it retains normal renderer/pacing defaults. APK v2 signing, 16 KiB ZIP alignment and all ELF LOAD alignments pass. Both primary save trees were read back after testing and matched all 16 original file hashes. Normal environment/settings were restored byte for byte, the app stopped and display OFF was verified. The profile's tested-status text in both the repository and external build profile now records these results and the open ground-occlusion defect. Native 1080p60 is not complete.

### Upstream runtime refresh on 2026-10-30

The review reached Android [`6444d676`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/6444d67694a4015003fc31c6c74f110fac43502a) and Apple [`e4292dde`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/e4292dded574fc13f7f8f59ee13ed178b80c491a). Android's eight commits after `794a9abb` concern release 0.1.3, documentation/screenshots and the Windows RomFS shader-source path fix; this worktree already has the latter normalization. There is no new Vulkan renderer change in that delta. Apple's thirteen commits were inspected selectively; no wholesale merge was performed.

Two general NVN/host fixes are now adapted:

- [Decoder-driven movie pacing](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/5782527c91dc): successful video output keeps frame-step players at their original 30 fps cadence for half a second. Audio and unsuccessful polls do not renew it; stopping or destroying video clears it. Output and Stop update the signal under the decoder mutex. The host publishes the state to the frame-step page before presenting. A disabled frame-step target still leaves cadence to the game. An empty-save launch did not reach a prerendered movie, so local cinematic playback remains unverified; the actual decoder queue, Stop/destructor and cap functions are exercised by the regression.
- [CPU access on memory-pool/buffer maps](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/682fdd26726d): CPU_NO_ACCESS returns NULL while GPU addresses retain their existing storage address and offset. A native regression checks all 1,024 combinations of the pool flag bits against the actual hooks and object layouts. The other Frostbite/SDK 22 changes in that upstream commit were not imported.

All **85 root tests pass**; debug and stripped O2 release each compile 139 sources. Vulkan already retries descriptor collection when a physical image generation changes, covering the corresponding concern in Apple's ended-encoder fix. Apple's storage-backed render-target writeback needs a Vulkan implementation and validation before adoption. Its early-fragment/atomic shader-chain update reports zero changed BotW Metal shaders upstream; that is not a Vulkan rebuild or proof that our shader translation is correct. No chain tool, chain version, guest module or game shader bundle changed here.

The ground investigation recovered one frame's 42 attachment snapshots (23 colour and 19 depth, including 19 float depth arrays). The lower-leg silhouette is already truncated in the main geometry images. Vertex trace output now reads the same `va_st` fields as rendering and includes the actual stream, format, offset, stride and NVN bound length; the previous diagnostic decoded the stream from the wrong bits. Rendering already used `va_st`, so this changes diagnostics only. A separate screen-off Adreno 840 compute probe checked every R16_UNORM value with texelFetch, nearest sampling and linear sampling at texel centres, against R32_SFLOAT: maximum absolute error was 2.9802e-8 over 65,536 values per path, with none above 1e-7. It does not explain the ground defect or validate arbitrary filtering, upload/layout or vertex calculations.

The final release's 25.443-second copied-save outdoor movement sample, using normal handheld/30-target settings with the FPS counter off and no pass/TSV capture, averaged **29.87 fps**; p95/p99 were 33.335 ms and maximum 66.670 ms, with three intervals over 50 ms and none over 100 ms. HOME/resume logged 2/3/2/1 and accepted jump input. No red/fatal diagnostics occurred. The lower-leg defect remains. This short sample does not establish a performance gain or resolution of the previously observed long frames; sustained moving native 1080p60 remains open.

The installed and game-folder production APKs both match SHA-256 `624f2e15b4c72504f6b444acdb6452eb63778d5c99e1cac0331c6a0a2b48720c`; the preceding APK is preserved as `android/optimization/before-upstream-runtime-production-f8d8dfbc.apk`. APK v2 signing, 16 KiB ZIP and ELF LOAD alignment pass. Both primary save trees again match all 16 original file hashes. Normal environment/settings are restored exactly, the app is stopped and physical display OFF is verified. Detailed build and device results are in the external game folder's `android/optimization/upstream-runtime-*-manifest.json` and `upstream-runtime-release-verification.json`.

### Further upstream review on 2026-10-30

Android main is still [`6444d676`](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/6444d67694a4015003fc31c6c74f110fac43502a). Apple main has eight additional commits, through [`a662f29e`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/a662f29ee79109ab54bbd7210a660247b9c69813). They cover missing-RomFS diagnostics, iCloud save synchronization, capture/replay correctness and capture-thread scheduling, Capstone compatibility, a Frostbite test fixture, and archive budget accounting. None supplies an Android rendering or native-1080p60 fix.

The general [Capstone API check](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/49feff863e9fb3958ff35153b68c73533d99a0b0) is adapted for the Android builder. Requirements now constrain Capstone to `>=5,<6`; both setup/doctor and port preflight check the loaded API major before extraction, and the standalone rewriter rejects an incompatible API before importing the renamed ARM64 symbols. The repair command uses the current Python and quotes the version constraint for Windows. Three regressions cover API majors 4/5/6, stopping before game processing, and the standalone early exit. All **88 root tests pass**. No dependency was installed or downgraded, and no shader-chain tool, chain version, translated game module or shader bundle changed.

Apple's capture changes require its Metal capture/replay infrastructure, which this Android tree does not have. The capture-time reduction is not a measured improvement in game frame rate. Its new Yaz0 fix corrects double charging in an archive budget introduced by a newer scanner; this tree's scanner does not have that budget, so the reported false-overlap failure is not present in the corresponding local path. These changes, cloud saves, and the independent RomFS diagnostic have not been merged wholesale.

Open pull requests were checked separately from main. Android [#13](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/pull/13) changes remote file-size enumeration to improve repeated installs; [#7](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/pull/7), head `ba896663`, adds opt-in CPU-capacity selection and an owned-context KGSL request with custom-driver integration. The latter's device evidence is an older overlay, stationary Wonder gameplay and Adreno 730, not a matched BotW/Adreno 840 result; it remains a candidate for controlled testing. Apple [#76](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/76), head `ecba0671`, enables BotW-specific variable frame steps and larger render pools, with render scale above 1.0 restricted to Mac. It does not by itself supply the separate performance work needed to sustain the higher targets. None of these unmerged changes is adopted by this review.

Private GPU vertex diagnostics further narrow the standing lower-leg defect. The lower-body draw contains the full pants and boots; forcing that draw in front temporarily exposes them, and restoring normal depth hides them again. A transform-feedback capture of all 7,158 requested lower-body vertices matches an independent CPU reference using the raw half-float positions, normalized weights, byte bone indices and captured bone matrices: maximum world-coordinate error is 0.000291, with maximum height error 0.000038. This does not support a large vertex-fetch or skinning offset in those draws. All 33,231 requested vertices of the nearby visible terrain were also recovered. At 31 samples near the feet, its interpolated height exceeds the feet by 0.4869 to 0.5706 world units (median 0.5381). This is an observed geometric discrepancy, not yet a demonstrated terrain or physics root cause.

Earlier tightly packed diagnostic transform-feedback regions produced incomplete terrain captures; their terrain coverage conclusions are discarded. The later 256-byte-aligned captures recover every requested vertex, with no empty draws across the six captured terrain variants. A CPU copy of the height texture's native storage differs strongly from the rendered height and has not been verified against the current GPU image contents. It is not a valid texture oracle yet; a GPU image readback is needed before attributing the discrepancy to layout, filtering or upload. No arbitrary character/ground offset is applied. The private capture code and alternative SPIR-V modules are withdrawn from the normal source and installed package.

The normal debug host rebuild compiles 139 sources successfully. The installed and production APKs retain release SHA-256 `624f2e15b4c72504f6b444acdb6452eb63778d5c99e1cac0331c6a0a2b48720c`; all 16 primary save hashes still match, normal settings match byte for byte, the app is stopped and the physical display is OFF. Review and restoration records are in `android/optimization/upstream-oct30-a662-*.json` in the external game build folder. Sustained moving native 1080p60 and correct character-ground contact remain open.

### GPU height readback and fixed-floor comparison on 2026-10-30

A private diagnostic now reads the actual R16_UNORM height image after the render pass containing its captured terrain vertices, with transfer/host barriers and a wait through the normal submission fence. All eight layers and four mip levels are recovered: 22,282,240 bytes, 1024x1024 at the first level. The image is GPU-written and not packaged (`gpu_written=1`, `packaged=0`); the earlier CPU storage copy is not its current image content. This observation alone does not show that the game reads that CPU copy or that a missing storage writeback causes the defect.

Using the GPU image, the captured uniforms and a float64 bilinear reference, all 180 terrain vertices within 1.5 world units of the feet match their captured GPU heights within **0.000063**. All 33,231 terrain vertices are present. The wider reference has a maximum height difference of 0.056762 after repeat addressing is used; hardware filtering precision and the remaining differences are not fully characterized, so this is not validation of all terrain rendering. Sampling each mip at the foot location gives heights 252.6589 / 252.6558 / 252.6558 / 252.6558. Changing the mip therefore does not explain the roughly half-unit contact discrepancy at this location.

Three copied-save positions, separated by two 1.2-second forward-stick holds, recover every requested actor and terrain vertex. Median terrain height above the sampled low boot vertices is **0.5382 / 0.5129 / 0.5025** world units. The game then saves successfully through its own System/Save dialog into that test clone. Its new `PlayerSavePos` is `(-1026.358154, 251.721710, 1809.384644)`; the captured boot minimum is Y=251.161558, and the nearby terrain samples are Y=251.6963 to 251.7363. Save position, actor origin, collision reference and skeleton origin are not assumed interchangeable; this is evidence for checking their relationship, not proof of a physics bug.

Independent samples of the owned RomFS height data also put this area near the rendered height. The files group four sibling height tiles per SARC archive, as described by the [independent tile reader](https://github.com/ntngel1/botw-renderer/blob/main/crates/botw-formats/src/terrain/tile.rs); raw heights are little-endian u16 ([height reader](https://github.com/ntngel1/botw-renderer/blob/main/crates/botw-formats/src/terrain/hght.rs)). At the three captured foot-centre XZ positions, a level-5 tile reference differs from GPU mip 0 by 0.0056 / 0.0218 / 0.0153 units. The exact cache resampling is not fully reconstructed. These small differences do not explain the observed half-unit discrepancy, and no terrain offset or texture-format change is adopted.

A separate boot from the original shrine save provides a useful control: on its fixed stone floor, both boots and lower legs are fully visible. Captured lower-body vertices reach Y=237.377108, within 0.007 of that save's Y=237.383942. The same draw key and vertex-input formats are used as outdoors. This narrows the observed problem to the outdoor terrain/contact path in these tests, rather than supporting a universal character translation. The next investigation is the relationship between the outdoor actor, terrain contact and skeleton transforms; neither a global offset nor a root cause has been established.

All three diagnostic sessions completed without fatal/crash diagnostics. The standalone GPU-height and shrine sessions have no red lines. The route session has one `BufferInitialize: past the end of the pool` warning (requested 0x40000 bytes from a 0x24000-byte pool), after all three position captures and during the save-menu phase. The save committed successfully, but the range warning remains uninvestigated; this session is not counted as error-free. It does not precede or explain the already captured contact discrepancy.

Private C instrumentation and alternative shaders are absent from the normal source and installed release. The restored normal host compiles 139 sources successfully; the existing root suite's latest result remains 88 passing tests (not rerun for private diagnostics that were withdrawn byte for byte). The installed and production APK hashes remain `624f2e15b4c72504f6b444acdb6452eb63778d5c99e1cac0331c6a0a2b48720c`; all 16 primary save hashes and normal settings match again, the app is stopped and the physical display is OFF. Evidence is under `android/optimization/avatar-depth-analysis/{gpu-height,gpu-route,static-floor}/`, with restoration recorded in `avatar-gpu-height-restoration.json`. No performance gain is claimed from these captures; correct outdoor contact and sustained moving native 1080p60 remain open.

### Latest upstream applicability and CPU-capacity probe on 2026-10-30

A further live check at 21:26 UTC on October 29 (05:26 China time on October 30) still finds Android main at `6444d676` and Apple main at `a662f29e`, unchanged since the preceding review. Pending PRs were checked separately from merged changes:

- [Apple PR 668](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/668), head `872b2737`, stages unaligned buffer-texture rows for Metal. Its reported reproducer is alternating lighting in Three Houses. The captured BotW height resource is an R16 2D-array image, not a buffer texture; this is no demonstrated contact fix. Vulkan's existing texel-buffer offset rejection remains a separate potential compatibility gap, with no matching diagnostic in the measured BotW run.
- [Apple PR 562](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/562), head `fc674258`, adds acquire ordering to a particular bare-exclusive spin-lock pattern, reported to fix BotW 1.6.0 crashes. An offline scan of all four original owned BotW 1.9.0 NSO text segments finds zero candidates for the proposed exact instruction shape. That rewrite would not change this build. No guest module was modified.
- [Apple PR 674](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/674), head `8b656e92`, addresses H.264 POC type 2 output ordering in the Apple movie decoder. Android compiles its separate `movie_avc.c`; it is not a direct moving-gameplay throughput fix. [PR 660](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/660) concerns NVN control-memory capacity queries, with no demonstrated connection to the current BotW symptoms. Neither was imported.

A separate private O2/API-31 native-1080p experiment tested present/replay-thread affinity to the highest readable CPU capacity within its startup allowed mask. This device reports capacities 762 on CPUs 0-5 and 1024 on CPUs 6-7; the app itself applied and verified masks `0xff` and `0xc0`. External `run-as taskset` was denied and supplied no measurement. An initial self-affinity audit also supplied no performance result because it incorrectly expected an unsplit main pass; the final audit aggregates matching MRT/depth pass parts.

The completed same-process ABBA test repeatedly moved forward/backward and turned both ways. Each phase has about 12.75 seconds of SurfaceFlinger actual-present timestamps; screenshots and pass audits are outside the measured windows. Main geometry is captured at 1920x1080 in every phase (1,467-1,512 draws across 15-16 parts). Off/on/on/off measures **50.74 / 51.60 / 51.69 / 50.02 fps**. The pair means are 50.38 versus 51.65 fps, a **2.51%** increase; replay CPU time falls by 0.108 ms and mean whole-frame time remains 19.33 ms with affinity enabled. Pipeline/library creation and texture upload counters are zero in all four measurement windows, thermal status stays zero, and no red/fatal/crash line is recorded. GPU timestamps average 16.50 versus 16.07 ms, but these are submission envelopes including gaps, not pure GPU busy times. The experiment establishes neither sustained 60 fps nor real-time simulation speed. Affinity and the temporary scale-ceiling relaxation were withdrawn byte for byte; no default scheduling policy changed.

A screen-off standalone probe confirms `VK_EXT_descriptor_buffer` support on the installed Adreno 840 driver (256-byte descriptor-buffer alignment, 64-byte uniform descriptors, 128-byte combined-image descriptors). Android 16's actual feature query reports graphics-pipeline hinting supported, but surface binding and automatic CPU/GPU timing unsupported; exported symbol presence alone is insufficient. The probe uses the Android 16 release ABI. A previous private probe incorrectly treated configuration setters as returning integers; those values were discarded, and the authoritative output now comes from `APerformanceHint_isFeatureSupported`. No hint session or global game-mode change was made.

The normal debug host again compiles 139 sources with zero failures. Before replacing the private APK, all 16 primary save file hashes matched the baseline. The installed package and production APK again match release SHA-256 `624f2e15b4c72504f6b444acdb6452eb63778d5c99e1cac0331c6a0a2b48720c`; original environment/preferences match byte for byte, the app is stopped and the physical display is OFF. Evidence is in the external game folder's `android/optimization/capacity1080-{evidence,restoration}.json`, `upstream-pr562-owned-build-scan.json` and `upstream-oct30-live-refresh.json`. Correct outdoor contact and sustained moving native 1080p60 remain unresolved.

### Descriptor allocation reuse on 2026-10-30

The preceding native-1080p logs attribute about 3.2 ms of CPU replay to descriptor preparation, including about 1.0 ms for allocating and updating new sets. A moving baseline run captured 6,009 CPU timer samples, decoded against the exact unstripped ELF extracted from its APK and the process's maps. Indexed draws, descriptor binding, render-pass boundaries and descriptor allocation are prominent callers. These are sampling proportions, not exact call durations or GPU busy times. A separate screen-off Vulkan compute probe validates ordinary updates, update templates and descriptor buffers against 256 expected results and 16 guard words per path, before and after timing, with zero mismatches. Descriptor-buffer offset binding is cheaper in that microbenchmark; update-template savings are small. Neither result is a whole-game FPS measurement, and descriptor-buffer transport has not been integrated.

The retained general change reuses descriptor-set allocations within each submission slot after that slot's existing GPU completion wait. Layout-keyed cursors return each allocation at most once per logical submission. Descriptor contents are still rebuilt, with the existing per-submission content-cache epoch unchanged. The allocation cache holds at most 4,096 handles per slot; this is not a limit on game allocations. A larger working set, missing layout-cost metadata or failure to allocate a cache entry/handle vector keeps the normal allocator and schedules a cache/pool reset at the next safe recycle. Existing descriptor-type pool accounting remains in force. Empty pipeline-layout gaps now register their zero descriptor cost. The feature is enabled normally; `DM_VK_SET_REUSE=0` restores pool resets and reallocation, taking effect when each slot is safely recycled.

The native regression compiles the actual allocator and slot-recycle functions. It checks layout isolation, distinct sets within one submission, reuse across completed submissions, both existing fence-wait paths with a blocked worker, descriptor-type capacity, cache-budget overflow without an allocation limit, unknown-layout fallback, entry/vector allocation failure, live mode changes and empty layouts. A negative control that recycles before the completed fence is detected. All **89 root tests pass**. Debug and stripped O2/API-31 release each compile 139 sources. APK v2 content digests and RSA signatures, packaged-library identity, 16 KiB ZIP alignment and all four ELF LOAD alignments pass.

The same-process moving ABBA comparison uses the same APK and input sequence in all phases, without CPU affinity changes. Each phase has about 12.75 seconds of SurfaceFlinger actual-present timestamps; screenshots and pass audits are outside measurement. Every phase captures 1920x1080 main MRT geometry (1,488-1,525 draws across 16-17 pass parts).

| Phase | Allocation reuse | Actual-present FPS | Replay CPU ms | Whole-frame ms |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| off1 | off | 50.89 | 11.606 | 19.624 |
| on1 | on | 51.59 | 11.251 | 19.327 |
| on2 | on | 54.14 | 11.244 | 18.443 |
| off2 | off | 50.95 | 11.715 | 19.620 |

Pair means are **50.92 versus 52.87 fps (+3.82%)**, with replay CPU reduced by **0.413 ms**. A warmed 600-frame interval serves 515,095 allocations from the cache and makes three new ones, with no pool reset or cache discard. All four measured windows have zero pipeline creations and texture uploads, thermal status zero and no red/fatal/crash diagnostics. The enabled phases still differ noticeably; this short result is not a sustained-performance guarantee. GPU submission-envelope means fall from 16.29 to 15.61 ms, but include gaps. Mean whole-frame time with reuse remains 18.88 ms, above the 16.67 ms budget for 60 fps. Outside lower-leg occlusion is still visible in both modes.

Three cloned-save normal-release smoke runs, using direct ADB input with the user's original handheld/30-target preferences, measure **29.69 / 29.75 / 29.81 fps** for default-on/off/default-on over about 24.6 seconds each. Each returns from HOME in the same process and has no red/fatal/crash line. Intervals above 50 ms are 12/6/9, and maxima are 75.00/58.34/66.67 ms; no interval exceeds 100 ms. Hitches therefore remain, and these short runs do not establish their cause or eliminate them. No real-game asynchronous-replay or other-title coverage is claimed from these tests.

The installed package and game-folder production APK now match release SHA-256 `9de9fa7c443237a93d2d72300ebc3269bf1050f2d9151acff45444d8e30a8ec4`; the preceding release is preserved as `android/optimization/before-descriptor-reuse-production-624f2e15.apk`. All 16 original primary save hashes again match after testing, original environment/preferences match byte for byte, the app is stopped and the physical display is OFF. The private native-1080p scale-ceiling relaxation is withdrawn; the normal release retains the original mode/FPS preferences. No shader-chain tool, chain version, game shader bundle or guest module changed. Evidence is in the external game folder's `android/optimization/descriptor-reuse-{evidence,build-manifest,release-verification}.json`, `descriptor-probe-summary.json` and `descriptor-prof1080-full-summary.json`. Correct outdoor contact, regular-build native-1080p configuration and sustained moving native 1080p60 remain open.

### Ground-contact controls and original NVN buffer behavior on 2026-10-30

Private pool-invalidation metadata captures now identify the actual NVN R16_UNORM height resource. The initial tracer mistakenly used Vulkan's format number 70 as an NVN number; its height-storage conclusions are discarded. The corrected tracer uses NVN format 6. Both natural-ground and nearby-rock sessions observe 46 invalidated pool identities, with no table overflow or invalid range. None of their absolute range unions overlaps the initialized 1024x1024x8, four-level height image. This does not validate every CPU/GPU readback path, but it does not support adding a writeback of that image in response to these invalidations. No such writeback is added.

A fresh diagnostic save clone places the player above a nearby `RockSetWhite_A_01`, using only the three `PlayerSavePos` value words in slots 3 and 4; the original seed and primary saves stay unchanged. In the initial downward rock view the lower legs and boots are visible, unlike the natural-ground baseline. Manual saving while standing on the rock retains the edited spawn Y even after the model has landed, so `SavePos` cannot be treated as a continuously live foot or physics position. After a short forward step and a new game-written save, the captured comparisons are:

| Surface | Saved Y | Last captured palette-matrix-zero Y | Difference, approximately |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Natural ground, normal updated host | 252.556412 | 252.001 | 0.555 |
| Nearby rock, private metadata host | 250.647095 | 250.613 | 0.034 |

The palette root, actual physics origin and saved position remain distinct concepts. The comparison narrows the terrain/contact investigation without establishing a physics or animation root cause. A separate normal-host control confines every observed game-process thread to CPU 7: all 47 threads have that allowed mask at each of three captures, and the natural-ground defect and root Y around 252.085 persist. This does not prove that scheduling and memory ordering are correct. The affinity is confined to that standalone process; no normal scheduling setting changes.

The known menu warning, `BufferInitialize: ... size 0x40000 pool 0x24000`, is traced to the call returning to `main+0xec2b00`. The [upstream original-driver oracle](https://github.com/digitalhivehub/nxnative/blob/a6b7191ead2d1ab421287e511d56fdc7985f864f/dm/tools/nvn_layout_diff.py) supplies a stronger reference than guessing from the warning: the owned SDK's original buffer functions run under bounded CPU emulation for 36 flag/range cases. All return before the instruction cap, with no imported function calls and no storage-kind hook in these buffer tests. They accept logical spans beyond a pool, retain the requested size/offset and reject PHYSICAL pools after clearing the object. Thus the warning is a local false positive, not proof of a storage overrun. The same owned driver's plain R16 array layout matches the host: 22,282,240 bytes, alignment 512, layer stride 2,785,280, block-height log2 4 and mip offsets 0 / 2,097,152 / 2,621,440 / 2,752,512. Its documented plain-storage-kind hook is used for the texture layout, separately from the buffer tests.

The retained generic fix changes only `nvnBufferInitialize`: clear host object state before a failed initialization, reject a PHYSICAL pool and preserve logical offset/size without the false range error. The declaration does not expand registered pool storage. A native regression compiles the actual hook, object types and query/map functions; 1,024 flag combinations across six ranges check success/failure, unchanged builders and backing metadata, query values, CPU access, failed-object clearing and guard bytes. Missing-pool/storage failures remain errors. All **91 root tests pass**. Both 139-source builds succeed, and APK v2 signatures/content, packaged-library identity and 16 KiB ZIP/ELF alignment pass. No shader-chain tool, chain version, guest module or game shader bundle changes.

The updated normal host completes copied-save loading, downward view, jumping, a short walk, inventory/System/Save/confirmation, return to play and a new save without red/fatal/crash diagnostics. The former menu warning is absent. The lower-leg defect and approximately 0.555 saved/root-height difference on natural ground remain. These headless captures export a 1920x1080 final image with a 1280x720 game viewport and are correctness evidence only; neither actual-display FPS nor native-1080p throughput is measured here. A foreground release/HOME cycle is not repeated in this review.

The installed and production APKs now match release SHA-256 `2d535f08d5a07eb3e19931decb5603d98a5fc1e744e573a384b53799bd30caf1`; the preceding release is retained as `android/optimization/before-buffer-contract-production-1a234914.apk`. All 16 primary save file hashes and original environment/preferences match again, the app and standalone diagnostics are stopped, and physical display OFF is verified. Private metadata/backtrace code is withdrawn; replacing the new buffer-initialization body with its baseline reproduces the rest of the original HLE source exactly. External evidence is in `android/optimization/contact-investigation-evidence.json`, `contact-nvn-driver-oracle.json`, `contact-*-saved-position.json` and `buffer-contract-{build-manifest,release-verification}.json`. Correct natural-ground contact, regular-build native-1080p configuration and sustained moving native 1080p60 remain open. No performance gain is claimed from these controls or the buffer-contract fix.

### Apple feedback-snapshot review on 2026-10-30

The next review finds Android main still at `37d0e524`, already reviewed above. Its most relevant change replaces per-game same-pass feedback lists with a general rule: snapshot an attachment a draw both samples and writes, and reuse that snapshot until the game's next barrier within the submission. This avoids a full copy for every decal draw. The upstream measurements concern its Metal backend and Apple devices; they do not establish an Android frame-rate improvement.

A private Vulkan metadata probe checks applicability on the owned plateau scene. It records fragment texture resolutions that name the same `VkImage` as a current color attachment, together with fragment output locations and nonzero channel masks. At least nine fragment-shader keys meet that condition on single-level, single-layer, single-sample color images, including `4d3d5a4408c2e8d9` and `753d0169ae3ce4f0`, also named upstream. Separate view handles do not disprove overlapping subresources: the probe's identical-view-handle counts are zero, while the one-level/one-layer image identity establishes the overlap candidates. The 128-entry metadata table fills as dynamic render sizes change and reports overflow, so this is positive applicability evidence with incomplete coverage. Descriptor-set memoization is disabled in this private executable. Its cumulative resolution counts are neither draw counts nor a performance measurement.

The probe loads a copied save, turns the camera downward and jumps/lands, exporting three GPU images while the physical screen remains OFF. There are no red/fatal/crash diagnostics. The lower legs remain occluded in the downward view. No snapshots are introduced by the probe, and it supplies no causal evidence that feedback explains the contact defect. Normal renderer source and host binaries are restored/unchanged byte for byte after the private build, and the diagnostic is stopped.

The other new changes are reviewed separately. [`a68bfd93`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/a68bfd93c80f568970cf717ee182d84c60abe7ff) orders later shader writes after earlier reads when Metal keeps an NVN ordering barrier inside a pass; this Vulkan backend already ends every `OP_BARRIER` pass and issues a full barrier. Its new Metal shader emulation for logic operations is not imported; local Vulkan logic operations remain disabled. [`6670f328`](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/6670f328f821dde105fcae52fc51dda5f5399c5f) emulates custom sampler borders using twin Metal samplers. This device already reports native Vulkan custom-border support enabled, so the Metal shader-chain rewrite is not transferred. That is not validation of all wrap modes or integer/shadow sampling. The new input recorder/file replay is a useful testing candidate, without replacing the locally modified HID/Xbox path. Capture-interface hashes, macOS covered-window handling, capture documentation and Portal 2 bring-up are not treated as BotW Android performance fixes.

No upstream runtime delta, shader-chain change, guest-module rewrite or game shader-bundle rebuild is retained by this review. The feedback rule needs a Vulkan adaptation and controlled correctness/performance testing before adoption. Evidence is in the external build folder's `android/optimization/upstream-review-20261030/review.json`, `feedback-trace-build-manifest.json` in that directory and `contact-upstream-feedback-20261030-004831-*`. ### Copied-pool writeback repair and successful RenderDoc replay on 2026-10-30

The copied-pool startup failure above exposes two host writeback hazards. CPU-cached and default pools copy their entire older GPU shadow back after a submission, even where no GPU write was recorded. The bounded 64-entry write list also merges gaps up to 64 KiB, and eventually merges unrelated spans to stay within capacity. Those gaps can contain newer CPU data. Completion now copies only intersections with recorded GPU write spans, for every pool flag combination. A dynamically retained interval union merges overlaps and adjacency without filling gaps; allocation failure stops with a diagnostic rather than silently dropping coverage. This records conservative binding ranges for shader writers, not the exact byte offsets of each shader invocation.

The owned bundle's storage-buffer reflection write flags are all zero, so they cannot determine writeback coverage. A host-side SPIR-V inspection follows pointer/image provenance through access chains, copies, casts, selects and phis, and identifies stores, image writes and modifying atomics. Unknown provenance conservatively marks every pool-backed binding writable. The actual 103 storage-buffer shaders agree with an independent instruction audit: 101 writer shaders and two read-only vertex shaders, with no unresolved roots or mismatches. Write tracking includes graphics stages and storage texel buffers. No shader bytecode, shader-chain version, game module or game-specific position adjustment changes.

All **93 root tests pass**. Native tests exercise the real completion-copy body, more than 64 disjoint writes, random interval unions, pool boundaries, slot reset and allocation failure; restoring the old whole-pool copy fails the preservation control. Separate SPIR-V cases cover aliases, cyclic phis, local versus external pointers, images, modifying versus load atomics, malformed input and conservative fallback. Both 139-source normal builds and APK signature/content, library identity and alignment checks pass. The private and normal debug binaries' allocated sections match after excluding build IDs; their debug paths differ.

The final private runtime loads a fresh plateau save clone, turns downward and jumps/lands with both normal zero-copy and forced copied pools. The copied-pool startup crash does not recur in these bounded tests. Its expected forced-fallback warning is distinguished from unexpected errors. The lower-leg defect remains in the copied-pool downward image; this does not establish a physics or animation cause. These image exports are correctness tests with a 1280x720 game viewport, not native-1080p throughput or actual-display FPS evidence.

A query-layer debug APK using this runtime records actual-display frame **3186** in `renderdoc-copyback-20261030-040832/outdoor.rdc` (240,521,961 bytes). **Same-device RenderDoc replay now succeeds**, enumerating 3,394 actions, 2,811 draws, 16 dispatches, 450 textures and 443 buffers. The original external-buffer capture still has its documented replay failure; the copied-pool capture is the successful control. Local Intel replay still rejects the enabled Android hardware-buffer extension. Copied pools are used only in the diagnostic environment and remain disabled in the normal preferences. No normal-release performance improvement or corrected contact is claimed from the capture.

A bounded draw-state audit exports 2,139 of the 2,811 draws before its 240-second limit; it is incomplete, not a full-frame census. All 171 exported shader modules match the owned bundle after the normal host load rewrites. Character shader `ae1219f4dc0d4b9c` identifies events 1271, 1325 and 1353; terrain shader `24547fd07e9978c8` identifies 30 events from 5164 through 5231. Exporting the final swapchain image reproduces the live scene's lower-leg occlusion. Intermediate G-buffer images show complete lower legs and boots after event 1353, then terrain covering them after event 5231. In depth texture 22267, pixels (623,515) and (651,515) change from 0.989336 / 0.989395 to the nearer 0.988580 / 0.988611; chest control (640,440) stays 0.988642. These readbacks establish the draw/occlusion sequence, not why the model and terrain have the wrong relative height.

The toolkit has two further limitations on this device. `GetPostVSData` returns collapsed constant positions for both character and terrain despite a coherent ordinary replay image, so those exported vertices are excluded from geometry conclusions. `PixelHistory` crashes the RenderDoc helper with SIGBUS and returns empty histories; no per-fragment history is claimed or retried. The normal game is stopped throughout these replay inspections. Intermediate images, raw depth readbacks, invalid mesh exports and helper logs are retained separately in the capture folder's `draw-audit/`, `geometry-export/`, `replay-visual/` and `pixel-history/` directories.

Installed and production APKs now match release SHA-256 `72b99da5fa21489c6573f236ba8e1d1e79cba5631d541ae77f7df086851f49b2`; the previous release is retained as `before-copyback-ranges-production-2d535f08.apk`. All 16 primary save hashes and original environment/preferences match, the app is stopped and physical display OFF is verified. Evidence is under `android/optimization/copyback-ranges-{build-manifest,release-verification}.json`, `copyback-native-writer-audit.json`, `contact-{copied-ranges-v2,copyback-normal-v2}-20261030-*` and `renderdoc-setup/renderdoc-copyback-20261030-040832/`. Outdoor contact and sustained moving native 1080p60 remain unresolved; the replayable capture enables further geometry and depth inspection.

### Vulkan interface validation and descriptor-buffer experiment on 2026-10-30

Six actual RenderDoc pipeline-state readbacks, covering character events 1271/1325/1353 and terrain events 5164/5173/5231 in the preceding capture, have identical depth state: depth test/write enabled, compare function 3, negative-one-to-one clipping, and depth bias disabled. The stored bias values are therefore inactive. This does not support correcting the relative height by changing depth bias. The state JSON is retained in that capture's `pipeline-state/` directory and linked from `ground-readback-findings.json`.

The official Khronos validation layer from [Vulkan SDK 1.4.363.0](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/Vulkan-ValidationLayers/releases/tag/vulkan-sdk-1.4.363.0) exposes two general host errors. An imageless framebuffer declares the parent image's full usage, while the attachment view explicitly narrows it. The framebuffer now records the actual view usage, including cached views and the distinct inherited usage of a cloned depth image. Separately, unused output-block declarations advertise eight clip distances plus one cull distance on hardware with a combined limit of eight. A conservative host-load transform removes only unused trailing clip/cull members. It keeps all retained indices, IDs and executable instructions unchanged, and declines whole-block accesses, aliases, unknown indices, shared non-output types and transform-feedback members. Capability and constant literals are distinguished from ID references. Neither fix uses a game identity or a position offset.

All **96 root tests pass**. The framebuffer regression exercises the real key construction and creation contract for one through eight colour attachments, ordinary depth and cloned depth; the former parent-usage implementation fails its negative control. Shader regressions cover active distances, whole-block access, aliases, shared types, malformed input and numeric-literal collisions. Across 19,592 owned shader entries, 8,931 change and every changed module passes independent `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`; every changed function body is byte-identical. Both 140-source normal builds and APK signature/content, packaged-library identity and 16 KiB alignment checks pass. The shader bundle, shader-chain tools/version and guest modules are unchanged; only runtime interface metadata is trimmed.

`DM_VK_DESCRIPTOR_BUFFER=1` is an optional, **default-off** experiment using [VK_EXT_descriptor_buffer](https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/descriptor_buffer.html). It queries native layout offsets/sizes, uses addressable buffers and immutable descriptor bytes in submission-fenced arenas, copies descriptor payloads when uniform-buffer addresses change, and keeps graphics/compute offsets and presentation-thread state distinct. Unsupported feature/property combinations retain descriptor sets. Buffer address caches invalidate on destruction and handle reuse. Native regressions exercise encoding, arrays, immutable patches, range guards, empty layouts, binding-state changes and slot-fence lifetime. An initial live validation run finds a missing device-address allocation flag on an auxiliary buffer; all backend buffer-allocation sites are repaired before the final run.

The final validation comparison reaches outdoor native-1080p geometry in both modes. The view-usage mismatch (`04627`), combined-distance overflow (`00710`) and new buffer-memory flag error (`03339`) do not recur. There are no additional VUIDs from enabling the experiment. This is not a clean validation run: existing errors remain for a compressed-format attachment, non-mipmapped sampler LOD limits, integer vertex formats feeding float shader inputs, and colour formats used for depth comparison. Descriptor-buffer validation does not inspect descriptor contents as descriptor-set validation does; the smaller VUID set in that mode is not evidence that its sampled-image issues are repaired. Additional games and memory-pressure/address-space-limit coverage are required before promoting the experiment.

A process-restart off/on/on/off comparison disables validation and descriptor self-checks, uses fresh copies of the same plateau save, and audits main geometry at **1920x1080** before and after every measured window. SurfaceFlinger `actualPresentTime` measures 16-second windows with repeated movement and camera turns:

| Phase | Descriptor buffer | Displayed fps | Replay CPU, ms | Whole frame, ms |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| off1 | off | 53.34 | 11.424 | 18.718 |
| on1 | on | 55.54 | 10.009 | 17.959 |
| on2 | on | 55.72 | 10.237 | 17.929 |
| off2 | off | 55.22 | 10.954 | 18.102 |

Pair means are **54.28 versus 55.63 fps (+2.49%)**, and replay CPU falls by **1.066 ms**. Mean whole-frame time remains **17.944 ms**, above 16.67 ms. All phases have thermal status zero and no red/fatal/crash diagnostics. The off phases vary by 1.87 fps, so this short throughput gain needs further confirmation; it does not prove sustained 60 fps. Lower-leg occlusion remains in both modes. The private render-scale ceiling used to select 1.2 is withdrawn after each benchmark build, and the normal loader remains byte-identical to its earlier version. No affinity, driver, governor or thermal-policy change is made.

A fresh copied-pool RenderDoc capture with the interface fixes records frame **3184**, 240,900,363 bytes, in `renderdoc-distances-20261030-064705/`. Same-device replay enumerates 3,807 actions, 2,940 draws and 17 dispatches. A selected eight-draw audit matches all ten exported shader modules against the owned bundle after the documented host rewrites; it is not a full-frame shader audit. Character events 3855/3909/3937 and terrain event 7842 still each export only one distinct post-VS position despite many vertices. These mesh exports remain invalid and are excluded from geometry conclusions. PixelHistory is not retried. The interface repair therefore does not resolve RenderDoc's vertex-export limitation or the contact defect.

The normal executable also loads a fresh save clone with the physical display OFF, toggles crouch twice and jumps/lands. The first stick click produces the game's crouch tutorial, and the second restores the standing pose; lower legs remain occluded after standing again. This does not support a stuck crouch state as the explanation. These 1920x1080 exports have a 1280x720 game viewport and are correctness evidence only. A normal-release, direct-input smoke test with the original handheld/30-target preferences measures **29.63 fps** while moving and **29.96 fps** after HOME/resume in the same process. The moving window includes a 100.006 ms interval, so hitches remain. There are no red/fatal/crash diagnostics, and thermal status stays zero.

The installed and production APKs now match release SHA-256 `ca571b9161b1ef409eb72c7b69756ed5b24da2d8e609f5bdc9de0c9bb5819fa2`; `before-vk-interfaces-production-72b99da5.apk` preserves the previous release. All 16 primary save hashes and original environment/preferences match; the game is stopped and physical display OFF is verified. The descriptor-buffer experiment remains off by default. External evidence is in `vk-interfaces-{build-manifest,release-verification}.json`, `clip-cull-investigation/owned-trim-verification.json`, `scale-probe-descriptor-buffer-v2-validation-20261030-063330/`, `scale-probe-descriptor-buffer-v2-bench-20261030-063914/`, `descriptor-buffer-v4-summary.json`, `contact-vk-interfaces-ls-20261030-070013-*` and `vk-interfaces-release-smoke-20261030-070431/`. Correct outdoor contact, remaining Vulkan errors, normal-build native-1080p configuration and sustained moving native 1080p60 are still open.

### Vertex input type repair and software replay debugging on 2026-10-30

The next investigation addresses [Vulkan's vertex-input numeric-type requirement](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo.html#VUID-VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo-Input-08733). All 8,931 owned vertex shaders have reflection masks consistent with their actual SPIR-V input declarations. The previous executable's isolated 557-record pipeline file nevertheless contains 85 type mismatches across 37 vertex shaders, including character shader `ae1219f4dc0d4b9c`. Its location 6 reads integer bone indices through a float declaration. Merely changing the format to a scaled format would change the register values; Maxwell's fetch widens according to the attribute format, while the shader can interpret those 32-bit register bits independently.

The host now keeps the format's native fetch class and creates a matching vertex-input shader variant. It retypes affected Input variables and pointer aliases, then converts each load back to the original declared interpretation with bitcasts. NVN's 32-bit UI2F/I2F formats additionally need numeric integer-to-float conversion because Vulkan has no 32-bit scaled format. Normalized, scaled and half formats retain their hardware float conversion before any bitcast. Unsupported pointer uses fail with a diagnostic instead of passing an invalid interface to pipeline creation. This is selected from attribute formats and declared types, with no game key or position adjustment. Shader variants grow beyond the previous eight-entry cap; their keys include the fetch signature. The recorded pipeline key grows from 992 to 1,000 bytes, so its existing embedded-size guard invalidates old records. No shader-chain tool/version, game shader bundle or guest module changes.

All **97 root tests pass**. The new native regression checks register bits, numeric conversion, missing-component values, pointer aliases, rejection paths and allocation failures. Independently compiled GLSL fixtures validate mixed input classes and 32-bit conversion variants. Across all 8,931 owned vertex shaders, forcing float, unsigned and signed input classes covers **26,793 combinations**: 17,776 rewritten modules independently pass `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`, 9,017 stay unchanged, and none are unsupported or invalid. Both 140-source normal builds and APK signature/content, library identity and 16 KiB alignment checks pass. The private correctness executable and normal debug build have identical allocated sections apart from build IDs; debug paths differ.

A standalone Adreno 840 test uses real vertex buffers and graphics pipelines, with vertex shader results read back through storage buffers while the physical display remains OFF. It exercises **78 formats and 234 comparisons**, covering one through four components, signed/unsigned 8/16/32-bit integers, normalized and scaled values, half/float storage and packed 10:10:10:2. All formats are supported and all comparisons match. References are valid typed Vulkan fetches and independently compiled GLSL numeric conversions; widened integer results also have exact CPU references. This is a vertex-fetch differential test, not a full original-NVN rendering oracle or a performance benchmark.

The updated runtime loads a fresh plateau save clone, exports standing/downward/jump-landing frames and stops without red/fatal/crash diagnostics, with the screen OFF. The lower-leg defect remains. A bounded actual-display validation run subsequently reaches outdoor gameplay with **no `08733` vertex-input error and no additional VUIDs**. The earlier repaired `00710` and `04627` errors also stay absent. Eight existing VUID categories remain, including compressed-format attachment handling, non-mipmapped sampler LOD limits and colour formats used for depth comparison; this is still not a clean validation run. Original saves/settings and the preceding release are restored after the test.

A two-APK old/fixed/fixed/old comparison disables the descriptor-buffer experiment in both runtimes, fixes docked main geometry at **1920x1080**, audits geometry before and after each window, and measures SurfaceFlinger `actualPresentTime` during 16-second movement/camera sequences:

| Phase | Input handling | Displayed fps | Replay CPU, ms | Whole frame, ms |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| old1 | previous | 52.14 | 11.352 | 19.158 |
| fixed1 | repaired | 55.93 | 10.903 | 17.897 |
| fixed2 | repaired | 55.14 | 11.061 | 18.142 |
| old2 | previous | 55.53 | 11.028 | 18.053 |

Pair means are **53.84 versus 55.53 fps**, replay CPU **11.190 versus 10.982 ms**, and whole-frame time **18.605 versus 18.019 ms**. The previous phases vary by 3.39 fps, more than the 1.69 fps pair-mean difference. This does not establish a stable throughput gain or sustained 60 fps. All four phases have thermal status zero and no red/fatal/crash diagnostics; the lower-leg defect remains. The private scale-ceiling relaxation used for this measurement is restored byte for byte after building, and is absent from the normal release. No affinity, driver or thermal-policy change is made.

The repaired runtime records RenderDoc frame **2574** in `renderdoc-vertex-inputs-20261030-080625/`. Same-device replay enumerates 3,788 actions, 2,935 draws and 16 dispatches. Exported modules from character events 3779/3833/3861 confirm that location 6 is now unsigned integer. Nevertheless, GPU `GetPostVSData` still collapses each character and sampled terrain mesh to one position. Retyping the interface therefore does not repair that export path; its mesh data remains excluded from geometry conclusions, and PixelHistory is not retried.

The public SDK's **software vertex debugger succeeds** on the same frame. Two skin vertices and one clothes vertex each complete 1,092 debug states, and three terrain samples each complete 514. It reads nonzero character attributes and distinct clip positions: skin samples produce approximately `(-0.08776, -1.50118, 8.61609, 8.81602)` and `(-0.09932, -1.47852, 8.60467, 8.80460)`. They are not a full software mesh, an original-game reference or a collision trace, and do not yet explain the relative height.

The normal release passes a fresh cloned-save direct-input run and HOME/resume in the same process using the original handheld/30-target preferences.
