### Linear image backing storage and outdoor contact on 2026-10-30

The public SDK software debugger recovers all **272 unique vertices** of terrain event 7728 in the existing repaired-input capture. Each completes 514 states. Ten selected low boot vertices each complete 1,092 states. Reconstructing the terrain triangles from their actual index buffer predicts five captured depth pixels within **7.1e-8**. At the ten boot XZ positions, that terrain is **0.531 to 0.566 world units above the boots**. These are shader inputs, software shader results and attachment readbacks from one frame; they do not establish a physics origin. The unusable GPU post-VS exports remain excluded.

[BOTWRecompAndroid's port record](https://github.com/mamcjr/BOTWRecompAndroid/blob/main/docs/BOTW-PORT.md) supplies the next useful cause: its foot IK consumed CPU height tiles rendered by the GPU, and missing or incorrectly pitched linear-target writeback lowered the skeleton by about half a unit. Our device-info item 57 already reports the correct 128-byte row alignment. The missing local path is image-to-backing-buffer publication: Metal's buffer-backed linear textures share their pool memory, while this backend creates separate optimal Vulkan images. Earlier investigation of the 1024x1024 array did not inspect the separate linear CPU height targets.

The Vulkan host records the backing address, row pitch and texel size for uncompressed, single-level, single-sample 2D colour images with NVN linear flags. After rendering, clearing or transferring into such an image, it copies the current image to that buffer in GPU command order and makes the transfer visible to later GPU and host reads before the existing completion fence. Copied pools record only written texel rows for completion copyback; row padding stays untouched. Initial uploads and writeback use the NVN row layout, including explicit pitches, default alignment and block widths in the upload fallback. The rule contains no game ID, shader key or position offset. Arbitrary storage-image writes and non-linear image/buffer alias coherence are separate remaining paths; this change covers render attachments and transfer destinations.

Fresh-save on/off controls of the same diagnostic executable reproduce the defect when `DM_VK_LINEAR_WRITEBACK=0` and restore visible lower legs and boots with the default enabled. The first captured palette matrix's Y changes from approximately **252.086 to 252.624**, a 0.538-unit increase computed by the game. Grass density also increases. A forced copied-pool route confirms visible boots after walking, camera rotation, jumping/landing, a further step and saving the test clone. Its deliberate zero-copy failure emits one expected fallback warning; an initial harness run rejected that warning before the title, and the corrected bounded run completes without other red/fatal/crash diagnostics. Primary save data is not used by these standalone runs.

All **98 root tests pass**, including production-function checks for padded-row preservation, exact backing bounds, offset rejection, transfer-to-host ordering, explicit/default pitches and compressed-block row sizing. Both 140-source normal builds pass APK signature/content and ELF/ZIP 16 KiB alignment checks. Normal debug and standalone diagnostic builds have identical allocated sections apart from build IDs. The final diagnostic repeats standing, movement, camera rotation, jump/landing and test-clone saving with no red/fatal/crash diagnostics and physical display OFF.

The first candidate's native-1080p moving ABBA comparison measures **55.38 / 50.92 / 51.65 / 55.43 fps** for previous/fixed/fixed/previous, with thermal status zero in all phases. Pair means are **55.41 versus 51.28 fps**; replay CPU is **11.345 versus 12.142 ms**, GPU timestamp spans **14.602 versus 15.982 ms**, and whole-frame time **18.059 versus 19.494 ms**. The fixed scene includes restored grass and actor height, so this is a correctness change with greater scene cost. GPU timestamp spans still include stalls. That candidate precedes the compressed/default-width pitch follow-up; its packages and manifest remain in `linear-writeback-candidate1/`. Native1080p60 remains unachieved. The normal loader's scale ceiling and original handheld/30-target preferences remain separate from the private native-1080p benchmark configuration.

The final-source private native-1080p build measures **47.43 fps** in a further 16-second moving window: replay CPU **12.605 ms**, GPU timestamp span **17.238 ms**, and whole frame **21.035 ms**. Main geometry is audited at 1920x1080 before and after the window, and thermal status is zero. This single window varies from the first candidate's short results and establishes no sustained rate or improvement. Its evidence is `linear-writeback-final-bench-summary.json` and `scale-probe-linear-writeback-final-bench-20261030-104046/`.

The final-source actual-display validation run retains the eight existing VUID categories with no additions; repaired 08733/00710/04627 errors remain absent. Validation is still not clean. The deployment guard catches an APK packaging error before publishing: the first optimized-library package still has the debug application flag. That package is rejected and the preceding release restored. Repacking with `--release` leaves the native library unchanged and removes the flag. A fresh smoke test of this actual non-debuggable package reaches outdoor play, moves and returns from HOME in the same process. With the original handheld/30-target preferences it measures **29.57 fps** while moving and **29.65 fps** after resume. Hitches remain; these rates do not validate native1080p60.

Installed and production APKs now match release SHA-256 `0bac09bb2651ebfb5906a60a2c0e3421823761f9e9ba627a8889fc605ff30d51`; `before-linear-writeback-production-26a2278a.apk` retains the preceding version. All 16 primary save hashes and original environment/preferences match. Game and RenderDoc helper are stopped and physical display OFF is verified. The descriptor-buffer experiment stays off. Contact is repaired in the tested plateau route. Sustained moving native1080p60, normal-build native-1080p configuration, remaining Vulkan errors and the other image-coherence paths remain open. Main evidence is `linear-writeback-{normal-build-manifest,contact-summary,private-normal-equivalence,release-verification}.json`, `contact-linear-writeback-final-20261030-103344-*`, `scale-probe-linear-writeback-validation-20261030-103614/`, and `linear-writeback-release-smoke-20261030-104608/` in the external optimization folder.

### Corrected-scene pipeline measurements on 2026-10-30

The corrected scene is profiled before choosing another performance change. A screen-off standalone run audits actual 1920x1080 geometry and records 16 frames with about 3,864 draws and 176 render-pass parts per frame. Pass timestamp intervals cover 22.058 ms inside a 29.445 ms envelope; gaps also include work outside render passes. Full-submission segment reports are longer and include internal stalls. These diagnostic timings depend on the screen-off power state and do not establish actual-display throughput.

A bounded actual-display test uses the unchanged private native-1080p debug APK (`56123b23...`), a fresh save clone and normal zero-copy memory. Geometry is audited at 1920x1080 before and after movement. The existing optional partial descriptor binding is compared off/on/on/off, without reinstalling or restarting between windows:

| Window | Partial descriptor binding | Displayed fps | Replay CPU, ms | Whole frame, ms | Main GPU span, ms |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| a1 | off | 48.44 | 12.557 | 20.647 | 16.905 |
| b1 | on | 49.01 | 12.266 | 20.402 | 16.654 |
| b2 | on | 50.57 | 12.135 | 19.762 | 16.092 |
| a2 | off | 50.69 | 12.328 | 19.698 | 15.986 |

Pair means are 49.566 versus 49.788 fps. This is smaller than the variation between off windows and does not establish a throughput gain; the default stays off. Steady summaries cover 15.03-16.12 ms inside main command-buffer segments, with 0.91-0.97 ms between segments. Including upload segments reduces the uncovered gaps to 0.58-0.64 ms. Covered intervals can still contain internal stalls; they are not a pure shader-work measurement. CPU recording reaches its first queue call in about 0.86-0.92 ms and continues for another 10.8-11.3 ms. The existing early submission path already overlaps much of that recording with GPU execution. GPU and CPU durations must not be added. Short native-1080p moving windows remain around 46-51 fps, not sustained 60.

A second actual-display comparison tests larger draw batches in the order 128/256/512/1024/512/256/128, setting both existing command-buffer and eligible render-pass thresholds. Pair-mean displayed rates are 46.34 / 39.55 / 28.84 fps for 128/256/512 draws; the single 1024-draw window measures 27.32 fps. Returning to 128 recovers 46.86 fps. Larger batches delay the first queue call and increase the observed covered GPU spans in this scene. This rules out increasing the fixed batch size as the next optimization here; it does not prove the cause of those internal stalls. The normal default remains 128. No barriers, LRZ workarounds, image writebacks or completion waits are removed. Both comparisons have thermal status zero, no red/fatal/crash diagnostics, and no memo verification differences in the subsequent sampled checks. Validation layers are disabled in these performance runs; this does not remove the eight previously recorded VUID categories.

The other project's [current sync implementation](https://github.com/mamcjr/BOTWRecompAndroid/blob/0d3731c7973eaccd49517cddb7ca413eb08e67e7/runtime/nvn/objects.c#L303) explicitly documents a correctness tradeoff: its sync-lag option can return after the preceding frame, leaving CPU height readbacks stale and lowering Link's legs in 1.9.0. Waiting for all current writebacks removes that particular opportunity for overlap. Its reported near-60 scene is therefore not evidence for correct native-1080p60 here. The linear image publication fix above remains applicable without adopting that relaxed completion policy.

A private diagnostic build adds condition/flag values to sequence logs, with no synchronization behavior changes. All 140 sources compile. Its bounded screen-off save-clone run observes 20 queue fences, all condition 0 (`ALL_GPU_COMMANDS_COMPLETE`) and flags 0. Swapchain-acquire syncs are already signaled in the traced sequence; the game separately waits for the newest explicit completion fence. Merely implementing the earlier `GRAPHICS_WORLD_SPACE_COMPLETE` condition cannot shorten these particular waits. The temporary logging edits are restored byte for byte after building, and production sources, loader, APK, shaders and guest modules remain unchanged.

Both actual-display harnesses restore the same non-debuggable `0bac09bb...` release, all 16 primary save hashes and original preferences. The game is stopped and physical display OFF is verified after each run. External evidence is `pipeline-feed-summary.json`, `pipeline-chunk-summary.json`, `fence-condition-{build-manifest,summary}.json`, `contact-pipeline-profile-20261030-105810-{result,analysis}.json`, `contact-fence-condition-20261030-112010-*`, `scale-probe-pipeline-feed-bench-20261030-111135/` and `scale-probe-pipeline-chunk-bench-20261030-112253/`. Sustained moving native1080p60 and the remaining correctness/configuration work remain open.

### Non-mipmapped sampler LOD mapping on 2026-10-30

The remaining sampler validation error has a concrete cause: non-mipmapped filters force Vulkan's maximum LOD to 0.25 but retain the guest minimum. The actual game requests minima of 2 and 3, producing an invalid minimum greater than the maximum. The host now also uses minimum zero for these filters. Mipmapped filters retain both guest limits. The positive maximum remains below half a level, so minification still selects `minFilter` while sampling level zero. Setting both limits to zero would always select `magFilter` and change mixed-filter behavior. This follows the [Khronos filter-mode mapping](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkSamplerCreateInfo.html#_description), with no game, shader or device exception.

All **99 root tests pass**. The new regression executes the production sampler-creation code for all six minification modes, both magnification modes, positive/zero/negative LOD minima and two maxima, checking valid ranges, unchanged mipmapped limits and independent min/mag behavior. ### Compressed block format views on 2026-10-30

A private sequence probe rejects the unused-attachment hypothesis. The actual draw has a full channel mask and writes fragment location zero. Its NVN texture is BC4 and its requested view is RGBA16UI, a compatible eight-byte interpretation of each compressed block. The previous backend forced a compressed/uncompressed view back to the parent format, then created a BC4 colour attachment with zero permitted view usage. These are real writes; dropping the attachment would lose generated texture data.

The host now keeps size-compatible uncompressed block views, uses block-grid dimensions for rendering, and retains physical mip dimensions in imageless framebuffer descriptions. These dimensions are distinct in Vulkan. Views currently require one mip and one layer; unsupported ranges or compressed/uncompressed size mismatches report an error instead of silently selecting another format. The rule follows [Vulkan block-view compatibility and dimensions](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkImageViewCreateInfo.html) and uses no game or device identity. Integer-view clear conversion and blend eligibility follow the view format rather than the compressed parent.

A block view needing additional attachment/storage usage expands the physical image through the existing ordered replacement path. It allocates the same format and shape with the added usage, queues all prior writes, copies every mip/layer, queues the copy, invalidates cached views/descriptors and publishes the replacement. Existing deferred fences keep the old resources alive. Failed allocation preserves the old storage and retains the per-frame retry limit. Ordinary cached views keep their query-free fast path. This extends the existing colour-format promotion mechanism; it does not enable broad extra usage on every compressed image at creation. One expansion, with no failures, is observed across the tested plateau route.

All **100 root tests pass**. Production-code regressions cover 8/16-byte blocks, square and non-square block dimensions, mip rounding, attachment/storage role requests, repeated use, unsupported ranges, allocation failures, ordinary cache behavior and ordered content-preserving replacement. The depth-only fixture gains actual view metadata and verifies that integer views disable blending independently of the parent type.

A separate screen-off NativeActivity probe carries the official validation layer. The shell executable could not discover that layer through environment paths, so it is not counted as a validated run. An initial imageless-framebuffer experiment is rejected for using block-grid sizes in physical attachment metadata; a stale-log attempt is also excluded. The corrected harness verifies an empty log before each launch. The successful final probe tests BC1/3/4/5/7 and three ASTC block shapes, power-of-two and odd extents, three mips, two array layers, and ordinary/imageless framebuffers. **192 subresource writes covering 2,064 blocks** have zero byte mismatches and zero validation errors. It additionally runs the exact production allocation/copy helpers on **1,032 existing compressed blocks**, checking both source and replacement bytes with zero mismatches. The synthetic probe is not a whole-game or performance reference. Its temporary APK restores the exact prior release, saves and preferences; the physical display stays OFF throughout.

Normal debug/release and private native-1080p builds compile all 140 sources and pass signing/content and ELF/ZIP 16 KiB alignment checks. Standalone and normal debug allocated sections match apart from build IDs. A 14-capture cloned-save route covers standing, movement, camera rotation, jump/landing, another step and test-clone saving without red/fatal/crash diagnostics. The after-save image retains visible lower legs and boots.

Actual-display validation removes all four compressed attachment/view/framebuffer error categories, with no additions. **Three categories remain**: `vkCmdDispatch/Draw/DrawIndexed-None-06479` for colour formats without depth-comparison sampling support. Earlier sampler, vertex-input, clip/cull and framebuffer repairs remain intact. Validation is still incomplete. A separate 16-second native-1080p movement/camera window, with validation disabled, audits 1920x1080 geometry before and after. SurfaceFlinger measures **48.88 fps**, replay CPU **12.593 ms**, GPU timestamp span **16.643 ms** and whole-frame time **20.423 ms**. This remains within the preceding corrected-scene range; no throughput gain or sustained 60 fps is established.

The first ordinary handheld/30-target release smoke measures **27.96 fps**, including one **1,041.724 ms** displayed interval; HOME/resume in the same process measures 29.72 fps. That long interval remains recorded and its cause is not established. A fresh run of the same release, with 12 seconds of moving warm-up, measures **29.76 fps** while moving, **29.92 fps** after HOME/resume and **29.69 fps** while moving again. The two moving windows have maximum intervals of 66.670 and 75.004 ms, with none over 100 ms. These bounded checks do not establish that cold-start or intermittent hitches are solved. Both runs keep thermal status zero, have no red/fatal/crash diagnostics and preserve all 16 primary save hashes and original preferences.

### Colour comparison sampling on 2026-10-30

The remaining comparison errors are genuine Dref operations. A logging-only executable identifies a written three-layer R16_UNORM texture and an R32_SFLOAT texture used by fragment and compute comparison lookups. Merely disabling comparison would change their results. The [other Android project's sampler separation](https://github.com/mamcjr/BOTWRecompAndroid/blob/4acbf40c9312618e16e1153f3696c64e125d2a2f/runtime/nvn/vulkan/textures.c#L1247) remains useful for ordinary lookups: [Vulkan's sampling rules](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/textures.html) also leave ordinary sampling through a comparison-enabled sampler undefined.

The host derives ordinary/comparison use from actual SPIR-V operations and descriptor provenance. Known ordinary lookups use a cached non-comparison variant of the guest sampler; comparison lookups retain its comparison operation. The ID cache distinguishes these variants and invalidates both on registration changes. Unknown or mixed use retains the previous sampler state. All **19,592 shaders**, with at most 27 reflected bindings, have resolved descriptor roots and no mixed combined-image bindings; their 42,161 ordinary and 1,486 comparison bindings are classified without shader keys or game identifiers.

Colour comparison lookups keep the original colour image and sample a depth-format snapshot: R16_UNORM to D16_UNORM, or R32_SFLOAT to D32_SFLOAT. Ordered image-to-buffer and buffer-to-depth-image transfers preserve the bits across every mip and array layer, without requiring maintenance8. R32 conversion requires the advertised `VK_EXT_depth_range_unrestricted` extension, enabled at device creation, so finite values outside [0,1] remain legal. Unsupported representations report an error. Current conversion is limited to single-sample, non-3D, size-compatible storage. Ordinary sampling, rendering and storage-image access retain the original colour image. Attachment, storage, transfer and CPU-upload writers invalidate existing snapshots; descriptor memoization observes that invalidation. A physical submission boundary freezes a refreshed snapshot before later deferred uploads, and bindings are rebuilt after that boundary. Allocation failures release partial resources and preserve the source image.

All **102 root tests pass**. New production-code checks cover sampler instruction/provenance classes, arrays, SSA joins, mixed and unresolved roots, resource failures with dedicated and suballocated images, odd subresource extents, copy/barrier ordering, reuse and write invalidation. Existing upload and depth-clear fixtures check their write notifications. Conditional-render state and configuration polling remain intact across physical boundaries. One-shot pass diagnostics now cover a complete logical NVN submission: the initial moving comparison was stopped when the older diagnostic ended at a new physical boundary and could not audit enough main geometry. That incomplete run is retained and is not a completed comparison.

An independent screen-off NativeActivity probe runs the exact production allocation, copy, invalidation and sampler helpers with the official validation layer. Two formats, two image shapes, three mips, three array layers and two data generations preserve **13,224 texels** in both source and depth snapshots with zero byte mismatches. All eight comparison operations, nearest/linear filtering and comparison gathers produce **368,640 CPU-checked results**, with zero mismatches, guard damage or validation errors. Snapshot reuse performs no extra copy. The float cases include finite data and references outside [0,1]. This synthetic probe reads no game assets and is not an FPS test.

Fresh actual-display outdoor validation has **zero validation errors and zero VUID categories**, including all three previous 06479 categories. Unused fragment-output warnings remain; this is not a claim of a warning-free API log. The native-1080p moving ABBA batch measures **46.90 / 53.44 / 53.55 / 50.49 fps** for previous/fixed/fixed/previous, with main geometry audited at 1920x1080 before and after every window and thermal status zero. Pair means are **48.69 versus 53.49 fps**; whole-frame means are **20.557 versus 18.681 ms** and replay CPU **12.565 versus 12.379 ms**. GPU query sums are **16.803 versus 16.072 ms**; these sum physical-submission timestamp spans, include internal stalls and exclude gaps between spans, so changed boundaries limit direct attribution to GPU execution savings. Snapshot telemetry shows about one copy per steady frame, not one per draw. This single short ABBA batch does not establish sustained native1080p60 or a portable gain. Both versions already have linear writeback; the inherited `linear_writeback_fixed` field in the original batch is a phase label and is not the actual feature state. The new summary records that distinction, and the harness is corrected for subsequent runs.

The normal handheld/30-target release, after 12 seconds of moving warm-up, measures **29.998 fps**, HOME/resume **29.920 fps**, and resumed movement **29.864 fps**. The two moving windows have maximum intervals of 33.335 and 58.337 ms, with none over 100 ms, thermal status zero and the same process after HOME. Its first immediate screen-off check was false; the initial result is preserved, and a separate follow-up verifies physical OFF, the restored release, original preferences and stopped game before final deployment. A final 14-capture screen-off route covers movement, rotation, jump/landing and saving a test clone; five clone save files change, while all 16 primary save hashes remain intact. The inspected landing and after-save frames retain visible lower legs and boots. These bounded checks do not settle cold-start or intermittent hitch behavior.

Installed and production APKs now match non-debuggable release SHA-256 `f5b2849878f8afa2ad6f9d7bcfa7934b720c032feebf1f21e0c9591f368bc327`; `before-compare-sampling-production-9ad2d7b5.apk` preserves the previous release. Normal debug/release and private native-1080p packages compile all 140 sources and pass APK signature/content and ELF/ZIP 16 KiB alignment checks. The normal build still has the original configuration and scale ceiling. ### Rendering timeline and parallel recording investigation on 2026-10-30

The installed release above is retained. A moving native-1080p diagnostic run uses the existing private scale-ceiling package, verifies main geometry before and after, and records Perfetto scheduling/frequency events together with simpleperf CPU samples. Its 55.24 fps, 12.266 ms render CPU, 15.630 ms GPU submission-span sum and 18.138 ms whole-frame mean are diagnostic observations, not a new optimization result. The GPU sum includes internal stalls and excludes gaps between physical submissions. CPU and GPU work overlap; their durations cannot be added.

The 22-second trace's 32 MiB ring retains about 13.24 seconds of scheduler events. Analysis clips the busiest threads to their common retained interval. The presenting/replay thread runs for 68.04% of that interval, sleeps for 31.50%, and is runnable or preempted for only about 0.45%. It runs almost entirely on the two prime cores. This does not identify scheduler starvation as the first target. The UID-specific Android GPU work-period track reports 86.50% busy over 14 retained periods; this is driver accounting, not shader occupancy. Observed GPU frequency is mostly 826 MHz; prime CPU frequency reaches 2.438 GHz against a 4.608 GHz hardware maximum. Those readings alone establish neither throttling nor a safe clock override. The trace has 38 setup errors for requested tracing features, and the package has no imported FrameTimeline rows; displayed FPS comes independently from SurfaceFlinger FrameTracker. The whole-process frame-pointer sample has many unwinding failures in native game code, but filtering the render thread yields 3,386 samples with only 21 failed callchains (0.62%). No samples are lost. Self-cycle aggregation assigns 34.15% to the Qualcomm Vulkan driver, 36.81% to the host library and 27.95% to libc. Descriptor preparation, copies and buffer queries remain meaningful CPU work, but these percentages do not prove how much can be safely moved off-thread.

A smaller-batch trial completes 128- and 96-draw windows at 53.22 and 49.57 fps. Its 64-draw window is retained but excluded from completed native-1080p comparisons: the before audit passes, while three after-audit attempts capture only about 170 main MRT draws and fail the existing 512-draw gate. The incomplete trial does not justify changing the default 128-draw batch. The one-shot audit covers a logical NVN submission, which can contain only part of the frame; future parallel-recording work needs a complete-frame geometry census instead of weakening this gate.

A generic range-cache candidate expands the exact-query TLS cache from 64 to 256 entries and mixes address/length bits. Its independent overlap/bounds/concurrent-registration regression passes with all 102 root tests, and the original hash fails the added neighboring-binding locality control. A first attempted negative control skips without the host compiler on PATH; only the subsequent compiled, expected-failure control is evidence. The private/native and normal candidate packages build and pass signing/content/alignment checks. The complete moving ABBA result is 52.31 / 53.66 / 54.35 / 54.02 fps; pair means are 53.17 versus 54.00, CPU 12.457 versus 12.169 ms, and whole frame 18.800 versus 18.512 ms. Although cache misses decrease, the gain is smaller than baseline variation, and the final old CPU window matches the new ones. The candidate is archived and withdrawn, never promoted. Both changed source/test files are restored byte-for-byte; all twelve retained-release source hashes, the loader and HLE match, and rebuilt normal debug/release library and executable hashes exactly match their original manifest.

The user asks whether the roughly 12 ms of command processing can run in parallel. Current presentation, completion and pipeline prewarm have workers; the main stateful NVN/Vulkan recording path remains serial. The existing `DM_NVN_ASYNC=1` moves that whole path to one replay worker and can overlap game-side work, but does not divide one frame's recording across cores. Its new screen-off save-clone route completes 14 captures, including movement, jump/landing and saving, with zero red/fatal/crash diagnostics. Inspected landing and after-save frames retain visible boots and lower legs; five clone files change. This is a correctness route with 1280x720 content on a 1920x1080 export canvas, not native-1080p performance evidence.

The separate actual-display asynchronous trial uses the same private APK for both modes, preserves current GPU fences and CPU readbacks, and pins the replay worker to the generically detected prime cores. Completed windows measure synchronous 52.18 fps and asynchronous 48.68 / 48.56 fps, each with native-1080p geometry audited before and after and thermal status zero. The final synchronous phase stops at its before-window geometry audit, so this is explicitly not a complete matched ABBA or a precise regression estimate. It provides no reason to enable asynchronous replay. Its timeline still reports about 12.1-12.4 ms replay time, a 2.5-2.9 ms game gap, and substantial GPU tail; copying submitted command lists adds roughly 0.99 MiB per submission. In this mode, present-thread CPU time is not replay-thread recording time. The trial restores the exact release, preferences, all sixteen primary save hashes, stopped app and physical display OFF.

The next structural candidate is a Vulkan encoding worker following `dm/gfx/nvn/nvn_rec.h`: retain ordered NVN state/resource processing, copy immutable API arguments into bounded batches, and let a worker encode driver calls while translation continues. Command-pool ownership, drains at commit/wait boundaries, resource lifetimes and current readback completion must stay explicit. True same-frame parallel recording with secondary command buffers is another option, requiring independent recording contexts and per-thread pools; the current global state and caches cannot simply be called concurrently. Neither approach is implemented or benchmarked by this investigation. Main evidence is `current-timeline-summary.json`, `current-timeline-20261030-150930/`, `current-small-chunks-summary.json`, `buffer-cache-locality-bench-summary.json`, `buffer-cache-locality-reviewed-source/`, `normal-outputs-restored-after-cache-trial.json`, `async-current-summary.json`, `contact-async-current-20261030-153855-*` and `scale-probe-async-current-bench-20261030-154148/`. Sustained moving native1080p60 remains unachieved.

### Ordered Vulkan encoding worker trials on 2026-10-31

The proposed worker is implemented and tested in three private revisions, then archived and withdrawn because no displayed-FPS gain is established. It preserves ordered NVN state/resource decisions on the producer and snapshots Vulkan arguments into sixteen bounded 32 KiB blocks. Fifty-four API hooks cover every declared recording command, descriptor updates and the necessary lifecycle/wait gates. Nested render-pass, dynamic-rendering and descriptor structures own their pointer graphs. Unknown extension chains and oversized valid packets drain and use the original synchronous API. Command-buffer errors and wait results retain their original semantics. Current GPU completion fences, linear-image publication and CPU readbacks are preserved. This is host translation/driver-encoding overlap, not concurrent access to one command pool or a division of stateful NVN replay among several workers; the [Vulkan threading rules](https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/threading.html) still apply.

All **104 root tests pass** for the final private revision. The production-code native oracle blocks the worker while the producer poisons borrowed input, verifies 12,000 ordered draws and nested argument snapshots, exercises bounded-queue backpressure, direct calls from another thread, repeated producer handoffs, fallback graphs and returned errors, and rejects a deliberate borrowed vertex-array control. A separate complete-frame geometry census retains actual issued draws across logical and physical submissions. It requires at least 512 main MRT draws and every qualifying main target at 1920x1080, with no group overflow. This fixes the earlier partial-submission observation limitation without lowering the geometry gate.

Each revision also passes an independent, screen-off Adreno numerical probe containing the complete production encoder. The final probe includes the exact existing CPU-placement helper too: 13,224 raw texels and 368,640 comparison results match their independent references, guards remain intact, and Vulkan reports zero validation errors. Actual-display native-1080p validation also reports zero errors, with geometry audited before and after movement. Each completed cloned-save route exports fourteen frames spanning walking, camera rotation, jump/landing and saving; five clone files change. Inspected landing and after-save images retain complete boots and lower legs. These standalone correctness captures contain 1280x720 content on a 1920x1080 export canvas and make no native-1080p performance claim. Numerical and validation success do not establish whole-game equivalence.

The completed same-process, same-APK moving ABBA trials have thermal status zero and complete-frame native-1080p audits before and after all four windows. Validation and expensive memo checking are disabled inside the performance windows and enabled separately afterward. Their pair means are:

| Private revision | Worker off | Worker on | Result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Producer-side lifecycle calls after drains | 54.15 fps | 48.80 fps | Regression |
| Lifecycle/queue calls executed on worker with synchronous returned results | 53.45 fps | 41.90 fps | Larger regression |
| First revision plus identical hardware-derived prime affinity in both modes | 53.01 fps | 53.09 fps | Within baseline variation |

The second revision adds about 73 drain gates per frame. A separate 96 MiB Perfetto recording retains the full 21.97-second scheduler interval. Producer and encoder run for 44.42% and 41.44% of it, overlap for 3.985 seconds, and spend 5.35% and 4.63% runnable/preempted. Only 46.03% and 41.81% of their running time is on the two prime cores, unlike the previous serial renderer. This is direct evidence that the work overlaps, while the frequent handoffs and changed placement hurt its critical path. The sampled encoder is predominantly the Qualcomm driver (71.70% self cycles) and libc (24.46%). Its 2,048 samples have no failed callchains; the producer has 47 failures among 2,348 samples. No samples are lost. There are still 38 requested ftrace setup errors and no imported game FrameTimeline rows; SurfaceFlinger FrameTracker independently supplies displayed FPS. GPU work-period accounting reports 78.33% busy, which is not shader occupancy or proof of a hardware limit. Core frequencies and samples do not isolate cache/IPC effects.

An attempted external `run-as taskset` affinity query is denied before any measurement; that incomplete run is excluded and restores the release and display. The third revision instead calls the existing opt-in `DM_PIN_REPLAY=prime` helper from the encoding worker and each producer entering an enabled scope. It derives the fastest CPU set from reported maximum frequencies, with no device/core constants or frequency override. Three startup participants acquire that policy in this title. The successful trial verifies the renderer/encoder masks before and after every window and records the whole process's masks. Both worker modes retain the same placement. Present-thread CPU decreases from **12.788 to 8.540 ms**, recording wall time from **12.697 to 11.347 ms**, but process CPU increases from **39.621 to 41.687 ms**. Whole frame is **18.876 versus 18.827 ms**, GPU submission-span sum **16.211 versus 16.235 ms**, and displayed FPS differs by only 0.15%, below the off-window variation. GPU spans include internal stalls and exclude gaps between submissions; CPU and GPU durations must not be added. The reduced recording work is useful headroom, but does not establish an end-to-end speed gain or sustained 60 fps.

All three exact candidates, APKs, source snapshots and proofs remain outside the repository in `encode-worker-candidate{1,2,3}/`. The four modified runtime files are restored byte-for-byte to the retained release, and only the owned new encoder/census tests and encoder source are removed. All **102 retained-source root tests pass** after withdrawal. All twelve retained-source hashes, normal debug/release library and executable hashes, the normal loader and HLE remain unchanged. The production and installed APK remain non-debuggable `f5b2849878f8afa2ad6f9d7bcfa7934b720c032feebf1f21e0c9591f368bc327`. Installed-APK runs verify that all sixteen primary-save hashes are preserved and restore original preferences; standalone runs use separate cloned saves. The final endpoint verifies the stopped app, no owned standalone, unchanged GPU-debug globals/driver properties and physical display OFF. Main evidence is `encode-worker-investigation-summary.json`, `encode-worker-candidate{1,2,3}-summary.json`, `encode-worker-profile-summary.json`, `encode-worker-withdrawal-verification.json`, `encode-worker-endpoint-verification.json`, `encode-worker-prime-bench-20261030-173349/`, `encode-worker-validation-20261030-173032/` and `contact-encode-worker-prime-20261030-172742-*`. Further optimization must shorten the complete GPU/submission/synchronization critical path; simply enabling more CPU workers does not achieve the target.


### Adaptive batches and encoding overlap on 2026-10-31

A fresh corrected-scene pass profile captures sixteen complete moving frames and sixteen standing frames, with contiguous pass ordinals and complete-frame native-1080p geometry audits. The largest timestamp group is the 1920x1080 main colour work. These headless, instrumented TOP/BOTTOM ranges include dependencies, observer cost and a different execution context: their 29.53/28.82 ms range sums and 44.63/38.95 ms envelopes are not hardware-active GPU cycles or on-screen FPS. A partial logical barrier trace is not a whole-frame barrier census. No barrier, fence, readback or shader semantics are relaxed.

A generic private candidate retains the 128-draw head threshold until a successful real queue submission, then permits a larger tail within that physical submission. The physical-slot query resets with slot reuse; a disabled head or tail retains the original policy. Both ordinary command-buffer cuts and eligible in-pass cuts use that policy. Existing depth/LRZ, transform-feedback and reserved-part guards remain. The experimental tail defaults to zero and accepts 32 through 512 draws, without a game or device identity rule. Its production-code contract test covers physical-slot state, disabled thresholds and routing 4,096 draws. All **103 tail-only root tests pass**.

A same-APK/process serial comparison uses a forward/reverse `0/256/512/512/256/0` sequence, sixteen seconds of movement warmup and twenty-four seconds of measured movement in each window. Complete-frame geometry is audited before and after every window. Actual pass sizes from four frames are sampled only after each FPS window; the query pool stays unarmed during measurement. Pair means are:

| Adaptive tail | Displayed FPS | Replay CPU | Recording wall | GPU submission-span sum |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Disabled | 54.01 | 12.380 ms | 12.270 ms | 15.939 ms |
| 256 draws | 54.22 | 11.980 ms | 11.869 ms | 15.793 ms |
| 512 draws | 45.47 | 12.681 ms | 12.540 ms | 18.990 ms |

An earlier four-window tail-256 comparison measures 53.45 versus 54.20 fps, but its gain is no larger than the serial-window variation. The larger serial batch reduces the number of parts while increasing GPU feed gaps. Tail-only numerical comparisons use the exact production budget, successful-submission query and LRZ helper: **1,792 cases each at 256 and 512**, with zero colour/depth differences against the fixed-128 schedule. Actual-display validation reports zero errors at both settings. The first 256 validation harness nevertheless fails an obsolete encoder-startup assertion after its graphics checks; its original failed result is retained, and `tail-batch-validation-reviewed.json` independently checks the graphics, save and restore gates. The corrected 512 harness completes. Three known unused fragment-output warnings remain.

Combining this policy with the archived ordered encoder and existing opt-in, hardware-derived prime placement is more useful than either isolated trial. All **104 combined root tests pass**, including both the production encoding argument-ownership oracle and the adaptive physical-submission contract. No complete-frame census is added to normal runtime sources; it remains private instrumentation. ### First-batch timing, external image limits and sparse GPU profiling on 2026-10-31

The preceding turn is progress: it establishes a useful encoder/batch combination, while leaving sustained moving native1080p60 unproven. This continuation first revalidates the retained source manifest, installed release and physical display OFF. Further tests reuse the exact archived candidate APK and verify its normal/private source snapshots separately from the retained worktree. No candidate source is silently treated as the current retained source.

A same-process moving `128/64/32/32/64/128` head-threshold sweep keeps the encoder, hardware-derived prime affinity and 256-draw tails constant. Native-1080p geometry is audited before and after each sixteen-second measurement. Pair means are **55.83 / 55.59 / 55.35 fps** for heads 128/64/32. Sampled CPU-feed reports confirm an earlier first queue call at smaller heads, but a longer remaining recording tail and no repeatable displayed-FPS gain. The 128 head is retained; reducing it is not a new optimization result.

A bounded screen-off standalone queries current driver capabilities without installing or starting the game or accessing its assets/saves. The Adreno 840 reports Vulkan 1.4.295 and driver `0x8034a01f`. Maintenance8 is absent, so its colour/depth image-copy relaxation cannot replace the existing bit-preserving comparison conversion. The driver advertises DMA-BUF buffer import and ordinary renderable linear images, including R32F 32x2 with a 128-byte row pitch. Those facts alone do not prove that the same allocation can back an external image. A follow-up matrix tests 108 combinations of three formats, three external handles, linear/optimal tiling, zero/alias flags and three usage sets. Every tested DMA-BUF image combination returns `VK_ERROR_FORMAT_NOT_SUPPORTED`; successful opaque-FD/AHardwareBuffer image queries require dedicated allocations. `VK_EXT_image_drm_format_modifier` is absent too. This rules out the proposed shared-pool linear-image path on this driver before any GPU data oracle or runtime change is attempted. External buffer support is not substituted for image support; the existing copies and CPU publication remain intact. Relevant rules are in [Vulkan external memory import](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkImportMemoryFdInfoKHR.html) and [memory aliasing](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/resources.html).

A new private-only sparse pass profiler follows the expensive all-pass observation. A startup stride cycles true pass ordinals and records at most one timestamp pair per frame. Untimed passes still advance their ordinal. Sparse slots reset only the two queries they can write, and finite sparse captures allow up to 2,048 frames; the disabled path retains the former sixteen-frame limit and query policy. Exact private source snapshots and a guarded builder restore every injected file to its prior bytes after compilation. No normal runtime or shader-chain change is retained.

The existing native timing contract passes against the private block, covering bounded queries, finite captures and concurrent reporting. Additional compiled checks pass strides 1/32/256/1024 and three disabled/invalid settings, including paired query bounds, absent target ordinals, two physical submissions per frame, unchanged true ordinals, capture exhaustion and reset-range safety. Actual-display validation reports **zero errors**, with the same three known unused fragment-output warnings. Its 171 sampled rows contain at most one pair per frame and preserve a constant ordinal/frame offset. Original preferences and all sixteen primary-save hashes are preserved.

The profiler's same-APK/process moving ABBA comparison keeps the encoder and tail 256 enabled throughout. The only measured difference is an unarmed versus armed sparse request; each armed request covers every frame of its sixteen-second FPS window. Subsequent full-capture evidence contains four complete 256-selection cycles per request:

| Profiler | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| Unarmed | 57.54 / 55.66 | 56.60 |
| Sparse | 56.04 / 56.19 | 56.11 |

The roughly 0.49 fps pair difference is smaller than the unarmed-window variation and is not an exact overhead estimate. The new method avoids the gross slowdown seen with all-pass timing. Its two captures contain 737 and 767 valid rows across 1,024 active frames each, with one pair at most per frame and constant ordinal/frame offset. Cross-frame estimates of the selected timestamp-range sums are 13.31/13.48 ms, much closer to ordinary submission spans than the earlier 29 ms all-pass observations. The 1920x1080 RGBA8 group accounts for **49.9% / 41.7%** of sampled ranges; the 1920x1080 packed-HDR group accounts for **9.7% / 13.8%**. These groups include any pass of that shape/first format, rather than uniquely identifying main MRT or individual shaders.

These are dependency-inclusive TOP/BOTTOM ranges combined across different moving frames. Only ordinals 0 through 255 are selected; larger ordinals are unobserved. Neither the estimated sum nor the group shares prove hardware-active GPU cycles, shader occupancy, a bandwidth limit or a full per-frame decomposition. The next action is to correlate selected ranges with shader keys and exact pass roles before choosing a rendering optimization. Native1080p60 remains unachieved.

The final endpoint rechecks all twelve retained-source hashes, loader, HLE, shader bundle, normal debug/release outputs, original preferences, production/installed non-debuggable `f5b2849878f8afa2ad6f9d7bcfa7934b720c032feebf1f21e0c9591f368bc327`, GPU-debug globals and driver properties. The last installed-APK run verifies all sixteen original save hashes. The retained 102-test result remains applicable because those source bytes are identical; it is not rerun merely for restoration. The game and owned standalones are stopped and the physical display is OFF. Private APKs, source snapshots and captures remain outside the repository. ### Shader attribution and unused depth attachments on 2026-10-31

The private sparse profiler now captures immutable vertex/fragment/geometry keys at all six actual issued-draw sites. Each selected pass has a bounded 256-group CPU collector, explicit overflow reporting, actual command counts, attachment shape and pass ordinal. Untimed passes do not collect keys. This addition introduces no Vulkan command or GPU ordering changes. Compiled collector checks cover disabled operation, repeated programs, subsequent state mutation, clear-only/depth-only passes, capture reset/rotation and overflow. The legacy timing contract and seven sparse/disabled-stride cases pass again with the attribution callbacks.

Two 1,024-frame moving captures join **all 1,519 selected GPU points** to their CPU key records, without missing points, overflow or logical/actual draw-count differences. The same-APK/process observer comparison measures unarmed windows at 55.76/54.62 fps and armed windows at 57.21/55.98 fps. Those moving-window differences are not an optimization result or an estimate of negative observer cost. Pure-program observations identify candidate pass roles, including a native-1080p packed-HDR pass, but TOP/BOTTOM ranges still include dependencies and overlap. A range with one program is not shader self time, mixed-program ranges cannot be allocated by draw count, and ordinals above 255 remain unobserved. Extracted shader inspection leaves the production bundle and shader translation chain untouched.

A separate generic candidate defers a bound depth attachment when valid colour attachments exist and NVN state needs no depth/stencil tests, rasterizer discard, transform feedback, depth clamp or depth bias. It retains the logical depth view's extent/sample constraints, preserves the original path for colourless or sample-mismatched passes, and avoids marking invalid views as omitted. Enabling a required depth/stencil operation ends the preceding pass and restores the attachment. Depth/stencil clears explicitly force attachment restoration, including when a colour clear is still pending. The option defaults off and contains no game, shader or device identity exceptions. Its diagnostic counters count attachment preparations, including possible view rebuild attempts, rather than necessarily executed GPU render passes.

Compiled tests exercise the actual production attachment assembly and clear functions, including initial/late guarded states, pending clears, full/partial depth clears, stencil clears, runtime disable, logical extents, missing views and view rebuilds. A negative control that removes forced depth restoration is correctly rejected. The CPU collector's added depth/stencil/write flags accumulate across the entire selected pass rather than reporting only its final state. A cloned-save movement route exports fourteen native-1080p images and changes five clone files; complete boots remain visible in the inspected landing and after-save frames. Actual-display Vulkan validation completes with **zero errors**. These checks do not constitute numerical GPU equivalence or whole-game correctness proof.

The moving ABBA comparison keeps the ordered encoding worker, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256 enabled in every window. Only the attachment candidate changes, in the same APK/process. Each window has sixteen seconds of moving warmup and twenty-four seconds of FPS measurement, with native-1080p geometry audits before and after. GPU queries stay unarmed during FPS measurement; bounded sparse/key samples run afterward. The omission/reactivation counters increase only in enabled measurement windows.

| Unused-depth omission | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean | Recording wall | Whole frame | GPU submission-span sum |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Disabled | 57.47 / 55.42 | 56.45 | 10.183 ms | 17.710 ms | 15.139 ms |
| Enabled | 57.78 / 56.93 | 57.35 | 10.087 ms | 17.420 ms | 14.791 ms |

The paired difference is **0.91 fps (1.61%)**, smaller than the 2.06 fps spread between disabled windows. It does not establish a repeatable gain, and this candidate is withdrawn without a release adoption or sustained native1080p60 claim. The numerical GPU comparison needed before adoption has not been run. The useful CPU-parallel result remains the earlier encoder/tail combination: 53.84 to 55.94 fps with a shorter replay CPU path. CPU and GPU work already overlap; their durations must not be added. Further parallel command-buffer recording would need per-thread pools, immutable state/resource lifetimes and ordered dependency-aware submission, as described by the [Vulkan threading guide](https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/threading.html). More CPU workers alone have not demonstrated the remaining improvement to 60 fps.

### Reusing readonly-depth allocations on 2026-10-31

The moving scene creates roughly seven eligible readonly-depth clones per frame. A private candidate caches their allocations by format and extent, with eight entries and a 64 MiB resident-cache limit. Every acquisition retains the full source copy, original barriers, UNDEFINED discard transition and existing LRZ touches; contents are never reused without refreshing. Capacity/byte eviction uses the original deferred view-before-image retirement. Oversized allocations remain transient, failed resource creation keeps the original fallback, and the option defaults off. This is a general resource policy with no title, shader or device identity rule.

Compiled checks execute the actual production allocation, acquisition and retirement functions. They cover the unchanged fresh control, changing source generations on every cache hit, different sources of the same shape, format/extent keys, live toggles, slot and byte eviction, deferred ownership, oversized bypass, partial failures and suballocations. A missing-refresh negative control is rejected. A screen-off standalone uses the production helpers on Adreno 840 in 512 D16/D32 cases across all eight comparison functions and 32 depth patterns. Two clone acquisitions and renders in each command buffer provide **1,024 colour comparisons**, with zero mismatches or source-depth mutations. Exactly two allocations service 1,024 acquisitions. This probe does not access game assets and does not enable the validation layer; the separate game validation supplies that check.

A cloned-save movement/jump/save route captures ten native-1080p images, changes five clone files and retains complete visible boots in the inspected landing and after-save frames, without red/fatal/crash diagnostics. The completed actual-display Vulkan validation has zero errors. An earlier validation attempt is explicitly incomplete: a log-read observation times out and the former harness stops/restores the run. Its failed result remains archived. The revised runner retries only bounded read-only cat observations against the same live process, without retrying mutations. The subsequent validation completes; no incomplete result is promoted to a pass.

The same-APK/process moving ABBA comparison keeps the ordered encoder, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256 constant. Native-1080p geometry is audited before/after each window, and sparse/key sampling follows measurement with the query pool unarmed during FPS collection. ### Avoiding readonly-clone store accesses on 2026-10-31

A separate private candidate changes only the store operation of a successfully created readonly depth clone. Vulkan 1.3, already requested by this backend, provides `VK_ATTACHMENT_STORE_OP_NONE`; enabled KHR dynamic rendering also supplies it on earlier API versions. The selector otherwise retains STORE and defaults off. The existing eligibility, fresh allocation/copy, barriers, LRZ touches, clears and later writable-depth transition functions remain text-identical. Only the successful clone branch assigns the selected operation. Original guest depth attachments, colour attachments, resolves and other draw paths retain their policy. Under the [official store-operation semantics](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkAttachmentStoreOp.html), NONE avoids a store access when the attachment is not written; it is not a substitute for retaining guest data after writable passes.

Compiled production-selector checks pass all sixteen API-version/dynamic-rendering/configuration cases. A separate screen-off GPU oracle uses the unchanged production copy helper and actual selector: clone passes use NONE while source/FDZ reference passes use STORE. Across 512 D16/D32 cases, all eight comparison functions and 32 depth patterns, colours match byte-for-byte and both the original and cloned depth bytes remain unchanged after the readonly pass. The probe enables no validation layer and accesses no game assets. Actual-display game validation separately completes with zero errors and 10,171 selected NONE stores. Its inspected before/after screenshots retain complete boots without an obvious new visual defect; these observations do not prove whole-game equivalence.

The moving ABBA trial keeps allocation reuse disabled. Both modes use the same experimental APK/process, ordered encoder, verified hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256. Each window has sixteen seconds of warmup, twenty-four seconds of displayed-FPS measurement, and before/after native-1080p geometry audits. GPU queries remain unarmed during measurement; sparse/key samples follow it. Store counters increase only in the selected mode and thermal status stays zero.

| Readonly clone store | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean | Replay CPU | Recording wall | Whole frame | GPU submission-span sum |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| STORE | 56.01 / 56.25 | 56.13 | 8.095 ms | 10.177 ms | 17.811 ms | 15.249 ms |
| NONE | 56.77 / 55.98 | 56.37 | 8.134 ms | 10.245 ms | 17.735 ms | 15.145 ms |

The pair difference is **0.24 fps (0.42%)**, essentially the same as the control-window spread, with about 33.34 ms p99 presentation intervals throughout. No repeatable end-to-end gain or sustained moving native1080p60 is established. The candidate is withdrawn without adoption. Together with the allocation-reuse trial, this rules out treating lower depth-resource CPU cost or an avoided readonly store as sufficient for the target in this scene. Further work should measure remaining global synchronization/queue waits and complete-frame GPU dependencies, without relaxing required copies, readback publication or LRZ protection.

### Block-local global-barrier census and merging on 2026-10-31

A private CPU observer classifies the ordered encoder's owned command packets, without adding, removing or reordering Vulkan calls. It counts exact ALL_COMMANDS-to-ALL_COMMANDS memory read/write barriers and their adjacency. Every owned block restarts observation; different command buffers, nonmatching barriers and synchronous fallbacks cannot bridge that boundary. The relaxed diagnostic also recognizes intervening state-setting commands, but no such additional opportunity is observed in the selected interval. The complete worker FIFO/argument-ownership oracle passes with observation off/on, and its borrowed-pointer negative control remains rejected. Compiled observer checks cover action/state/query breaks, command-buffer/block boundaries and twelve barrier-shape guards, including a rejected missing-image-guard control.

The screen-off cloned-save route completes native-1080p before/after geometry audits and ten captures without red/fatal/crash diagnostics. In its final 600-present interval, the worker records approximately **233,320 full global barriers**, including **86,590 directly adjacent duplicates**: about 389 full barriers and 144 duplicate opportunities per present, or 37.1% of the full-barrier count. These are worker-recorded calls, excluding direct/fallback/cross-thread calls. The initial observer independently samples atomic counters while the worker runs, so these rates are approximate rather than a coherent whole-frame GPU census. Counts alone do not establish synchronization cost.

A new private candidate removes only exact adjacent duplicates on the same command buffer within each owned block, retaining the first barrier. Every state-setting command also breaks strict eligibility. Nonmatching stage/access masks, dependency flags, extension chains, image/buffer barriers, queries, copies, draws and render-pass boundaries remain. Each block owns its selected mode, and reporting uses a coherent snapshot captured by the worker under the existing block-completion mutex. The option defaults off and requires the ordered encoder; no title, build, shader or device identity exception is added. Required copies, LRZ touches and readback publication are preserved.

The complete production worker oracle passes all four observation/merge combinations. An additional actual-worker fixture verifies retained first barriers, mode changes, unknown-chain synchronous fallback, command-buffer boundaries, automatic capacity boundaries, explicit drains, non-owner calls and immutable block-mode capture. A screen-off real-GPU differential contains the entire production encoder and original LRZ helper. Reference and merged runs have identical submission schedules, with intentional duplicate full barriers around real rendering/transfers. Across tails 256 and 512, **3,584 cases have zero colour/depth differences**, including ordinary/oversized depth extents, depth/stencil formats and individual views of three-layer arrays. The probe confirms tens of thousands of actual removals; it does not use game assets or enable the validation layer.

A separate merged cloned-save movement/jump/save route completes ten native-1080p captures, changes five clone files and retains complete visible boots in the inspected landing and after-save images, without obvious new defects or red/fatal/crash diagnostics. Actual-display game validation completes with **zero errors** and confirms that all eligible strict duplicates are removed. These checks do not prove whole-game equivalence or eliminate every intermittent LRZ driver defect.

The moving same-APK/process ABBA trial keeps the encoder, verified hardware-derived prime placement, tail 256 and CPU census enabled in both modes. Only strict merging changes. Each window uses sixteen seconds of moving warmup and twenty-four seconds of displayed-FPS measurement, with native-1080p geometry audits before/after. Sparse GPU/key probes run only afterward. Coherent report deltas prove enabled-only removals and retain all nonduplicate full barriers; their report boundaries differ from FPS windows.

| Strict merge | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| Disabled | 56.50 / 54.79 | 55.64 |
| Enabled | 54.93 / 55.17 | 55.05 |

The pair difference is **-0.59 fps**, smaller than the 1.71 fps control-window spread. Reducing barrier calls has not established a repeatable throughput improvement or a precise degradation. The candidate remains archived without release adoption; sustained moving native1080p60 is still unachieved. Historical encoder reports also show roughly eighteen producer drains and 2.4 ms cumulative drain wait per frame, but these overlapping waits are not an independently removable whole-frame cost. Further work must measure the critical path rather than infer it from call counts or summed CPU/GPU durations.

### Fresh upstream correctness applicability on 2026-10-31

Read-only Git/API checks observe Android head `cd2e5d05ab679bbb801e10f548f03c20b83322e0` and Apple head `2006d5ed8690d79ef4865a60917eb938cdb4c8dc`. Twenty recent commit descriptions from each are inspected, with the relevant shader, masked-clear and acquire-lock patches read in detail; this is not a claim of complete upstream synchronization. The latest Android merge concerns rebuild version codes/GUI options, and the latest Apple change concerns Unreal region sizing. Neither title alone proves native-1080p rendering or 60 fps for this scene.

The local shader loader lacks the general vertex-output initialization introduced by [Android commit 25141ebd37](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/25141ebd37) and its [added-interface follow-up 2a3ae7b68e](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/2a3ae7b68e). The former initializes float32 vec4 Location outputs to (0,0,0,1), matching the Maxwell defaults described by that upstream implementation. A read-only census of the unchanged 19,592-entry game bundle finds 8,931 vertex modules and 50,777 eligible output variables. Of 184 vertex keys present in the selected actual-draw samples, 141 modules contain conservatively partial or unwritten outputs. The scanner unions direct/constant-component stores, treats unknown component paths as whole writes and excludes pointer escapes through calls/phi/select. This is static applicability evidence, not proof that every missing component is consumed or that this omission caused a specific visible colour defect. The next action is a guarded implementation plus numerical shader-interface validation before judging image correctness and performance.

[Android commit d63309fdd0](https://github.com/shirai91/nx2android/commit/d63309fdd0) implements masked colour clears. The current backend still diagnoses a partial channel mask and clears all channels, so that generic correctness gap remains. Adapting it requires reviewing integer/normalized formats and state restoration rather than copying the patch blindly. It is not silently included in the barrier candidate.

The pure acquire-loop scanner from [Apple commit fe6d3dfadb](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/fe6d3dfadb) is applied read-only to the original current 1.9.0 main, rtld, sdk and subsdk0 modules, excluding the rewriter's recognized embedded data blocks. All four have **zero matches** for that exact unfenced LDXR/test-and-set pattern. This result does not justify rewriting those modules for the 1.6.0-specific symptom or prove that no other locking issue exists. The scan uses Capstone 5.0.9 installed only under the game's work tools directory, following the repository's `capstone>=5,<6` requirement; system Python and device contents are unchanged. Main evidence: saved upstream commit JSONs, `upstream-vs-initialization-applicability.json`, `upstream-acquire-lock-applicability.json`, `audit_upstream_applicability.py`, `audit_acquire_lock_sites.py`, and `upstream-correctness-applicability-summary.json`.

### Vertex-output default initialization on 2026-10-31

The Android shader loader now initializes plain float32 vec4 outputs at NVN locations 0 through 31 to (0,0,0,1) at the start of the main vertex entry. Existing executable writes still determine their written components. The initializer also runs for output locations newly introduced by the existing shader-interface variant builder. It selects the entry's actual interface variables, preserves explicit global initializers, excludes built-ins and other stages/types, inserts stores after Function variables/debug declarations, and reuses compatible constants. Repeated application is a byte-preserving no-op. Malformed input or allocation failure fails module creation with a red diagnostic. This is a general backend correctness change with no title, shader-key or device exception. The shader translator, frozen bundle, guest modules and game profile remain unchanged.

All 103 root tests pass without skips. Compiled checks include an independent default-value evaluator, partial and complete writes, selected locations, multiple vector/scalar types, entry/interface selection, insertion order, idempotence, malformed input and all allocation failure points. A deliberately wrong W default is rejected. Every one of the bundle's 8,931 vertex modules passes the transformation/no-op checks, with 8,841 changed and 90 unchanged; the input bytes remain intact and changed modules are idempotent. All 184 selected actual-draw vertex modules validate before and after transformation, with no validation regressions.

A standalone screen-off Adreno 840 graphics oracle independently compares the production transforms against explicit GLSL defaults using full RGBA32_UINT float bits. It separately checks original partial outputs and a newly added interface location, across all 16 component-write masks, both output selections, D16/D32 and all eight depth comparisons. The resulting 1,024 cases have zero colour/default-value differences or source-depth mutations. This oracle opens no game data and enables no validation layer. Separate native-1080p game validation completes with zero errors/VUIDs and confirms actual initialization. A copied-save route exercises movement, camera turns, jumping and saving, captures 13 frames and changes five clone files. Complete boots are visible in the inspected landing frame. These checks do not establish whole-game image equivalence or identify the cause of every reported colour defect.

A negative GPU control substitutes the valid but uninitialized original vertex shader into the same oracle. It fails 224 of 512 colour comparisons while the explicit-default reference still matches its independent CPU expectations. The initialized candidate passes all cases. This demonstrates that the numerical test detects the missing initialization on this GPU; it does not attribute a particular game screenshot to that omission.

The actual-display package ABBA comparison uses a fresh copy of the same save for every phase, sixteen seconds of moving warmup and twenty-four seconds of displayed-FPS collection. Both private packages use the same ordered encoder, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256; only vertex-output initialization differs. Main MRT geometry is audited at 1920x1080 before and after every window. Per-pass GPU queries remain disabled during measurement, while the existing submission-segment timing stays enabled in both packages. Thermal status remains zero throughout.

| Vertex defaults | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean | Replay CPU | Recording wall | Whole frame | GPU submission-span sum |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Previous path | 56.72 / 55.61 | 56.17 | 8.069 ms | 10.079 ms | 17.804 ms | 15.201 ms |
| Initialized | 57.15 / 55.26 | 56.21 | 8.089 ms | 10.095 ms | 17.791 ms | 15.220 ms |

The difference is 0.04 fps, smaller than both the 1.12 fps control spread and 1.88 fps initialized spread. No repeatable gain or regression is established. This generic change is retained for correctness. These bounded experimental-package measurements do not establish normal-release performance, sustained thermal behavior or native1080p60. CPU and GPU intervals overlap, and GPU submission spans include dependencies; their values must not be added as separate frame costs.

The current production and installed release is now non-debuggable `4ceb9b6fed27f852b8719c6a67c572a4962773deb484ab7051e99460e3ca60db`, replacing the preserved `f5b284...` package. Signature and ZIP/ELF 16 KiB alignment checks pass. Normal debug/release outputs match the thirteen fixed source hashes and unchanged normal loader. The private encoder and scale-ceiling changes remain outside normal sources/defaults. All sixteen primary-save hashes are checked using the normal debug package immediately before the final release install, with no subsequent game start. Original preferences, GPU-debug globals and driver properties are unchanged; the game is stopped and the physical display is OFF. The earlier manifest's `fixed_sources_unreleased` describes its build-time state; the release-verification record documents adoption.

Evidence is kept in the game's optimization directory: `vertex-output-{build-inputs,source-manifest,build-manifest,normal-build-manifest,bundle-verification,contact-verification,investigation-summary,release-verification}.json`, `vertex-output-root-tests.log`, `vertex-output-gpu/`, `contact-vertex-output-20261030-234218-*`, `vertex-output-validation-20261030-234510/`, `vertex-output-bench-20261030-234853/` and `vertex-output-candidate/artifact-index.json`.

### Complete-pass dependency census on 2026-10-31

A private CPU-only observer records every pass and its actual-issued shader groups during two bounded 32-frame windows. It adds no Vulkan calls and leaves the original Vulkan-call sequence textually unchanged. Per-pass timestamp queries remain disabled. An atomic full-barrier sequence, command-buffer identity, upload generation and linear-attachment metadata describe the boundaries; they do not measure GPU duration. The capture request is frozen for each open pass, including when the environment changes before its end. Compiled tests check command totals, joins, disabled mode, request changes and the 32-frame cap; an unbounded negative control is rejected.

The completed copied-save standing/rotated run captures 11,202 pass records. Every pass joins to its complete actual-draw shader groups, with no missing points, overflow or logical/actual draw-count differences. Static inspection covers all 422 observed shader modules. Rates exclude the potentially partial first and last frame of each capture, leaving sixty complete interior frames:

| Capture | Interior frames | Passes | Passes/frame | Fresh readonly-depth clone passes | Coarse shader-only adjacent candidates | Same CB/upload generation and only one counted boundary barrier |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Standing | 30 | 5,218 | 173.93 | 210 | 3,226 | 730 |
| Rotated | 30 | 5,308 | 176.93 | 210 | 3,248 | 820 |

The coarse shader rule excludes storage/physical-pointer/transform-feedback declarations in the preceding pass and requires no texture descriptors before rasterization in the next pass. It is a screening condition, not proof that all resources are independent. Most coarse candidates fail the additional boundary checks; many surviving examples are small 8x8 or 16x16 targets. The code also performs one-texel depth transfers for Adreno LRZ workarounds. Consequently, globally weakening a begin-pass barrier is not justified by the shader counts. The [Khronos barrier sample](https://docs.vulkan.org/samples/latest/samples/performance/pipeline_barriers/README.html) explains why a proven dependency can allow vertex/fragment overlap; this census supplies applicability data rather than an optimization or timing result.

An initial non-atomic observer is superseded and archived separately. During the atomic revision, an ENOSPC failure in the former temporary-source injection truncates `gfx_vk_exec.c`; its exact retained bytes are restored from the hash-verified release-source snapshot. All thirteen runtime-source hashes and the normal loader are rechecked. One preserved generated negative-test APK is temporarily moved and then returned with its original hash; no generated artifact remains relocated. The revised private builder checks source/build volume space, saves originals outside the source volume and atomically replaces each temporary source. An injected failed commit confirms that original bytes remain intact and temporary files are cleaned. The successful build restores every original source. This does not alter the normal build pipeline or installed release.

Main evidence: `pass-overlap-applicability.json`, `overlap-trace-{source-manifest,unit-verification,atomic-write-verification,build-manifest,observation-summary}.json`, `contact-overlap-trace-atomic-20261031-001749-*`, `overlap-trace-initial-nonatomic/` and the private `overlap-trace-source/`.

### Fresh readonly-clone entry reads on 2026-10-31

The census observes seven freshly allocated readonly-depth clones per complete frame. A private candidate omits only their additional one-texel LRZ entry read after the existing full source-to-clone copy and barriers. Successful fresh creation is required. Original-image reads, cross-image workarounds, clone-exit texel writeback, allocation/copy policy, clears and writable-depth transitions are unchanged. The option defaults off and has no title, shader-key or device exception.

Compiled checks execute the actual entry helper in all four option/freshness cases. A deliberately broadened selector that also omits original-image reads is rejected. A screen-off standalone uses the actual copy, LRZ and entry helpers on Adreno 840: both modes run 512 D16/D32 cases across eight comparisons and 32 patterns. All 1,024 cases match the independent FDZ colour reference, with zero source-depth mutations. The control retains 1,024 entry reads; the candidate omits 512 fresh-clone reads and retains 512 original-image reads. A second 1,024-case oracle binds each fresh image to the same offset of one persistent device-memory allocation, destroying the preceding image before the next acquisition. All comparisons and source-depth checks still pass across 1,024 fresh image lifetimes. These probes enable no validation layer and access no game data. A copied-save movement/jump/camera/save route completes without red/fatal/crash diagnostics, capturing thirteen frames. Complete boots remain visible in the inspected landing frame. Actual-display native-1080p validation separately completes with zero errors.

The moving same-APK/process ABBA trial holds the ordered encoder, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256 constant. Each window has sixteen seconds of moving warmup and twenty-four seconds of displayed-FPS collection. Main MRT geometry is checked at 1920x1080 before/after every window. Per-pass queries remain unarmed during FPS collection; sparse GPU/key sampling follows each window. Counters confirm omitted reads increase only while enabled and original-image reads continue in both modes. Thermal status remains zero.

| Fresh-clone entry read | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean | Replay CPU | Recording wall | Whole frame | GPU submission-span sum |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Retained | 56.25 / 56.39 | 56.32 | 8.058 ms | 10.090 ms | 17.755 ms | 15.185 ms |
| Omitted for fresh clones only | 56.67 / 55.49 | 56.08 | 8.083 ms | 10.171 ms | 17.831 ms | 15.257 ms |

The pair difference is -0.24 fps. The control spread is 0.13 fps and the enabled spread is 1.18 fps; the enabled samples fall on opposite sides of both controls. No repeatable improvement or precise regression is established. The candidate is withdrawn without adoption. Removing these seven fresh-clone entry transfers does not provide evidence of a shorter complete frame. This does not establish the cost of all LRZ workarounds or justify removing original-image/cross-image synchronization. Resource-dependency-driven stage scopes remain a separate investigation, with unknown dependencies retaining the existing ordering.

The endpoint checks all thirteen retained runtime sources, normal loader/HLE/shader bundle, normal debug/release outputs and unchanged installed/production non-debuggable `4ceb9b6fed27f852b8719c6a67c572a4962773deb484ab7051e99460e3ca60db`. The existing 103-root-test result applies to identical normal source bytes.
