### Complete-pass stage scopes on 2026-10-31

A private, default-off candidate narrows the destination stages of selected begin-pass barriers to fragment shading, early/late depth-stencil tests and colour output. Source stages and both memory-access masks remain unchanged. The initial proof uses resource classes: the preceding complete pass can write only privately allocated framebuffer images disjoint from buffer storage, and every actual draw in the following complete pass must have pre-raster shaders without image access or writable/unknown storage. This is not a complete per-image identity tracker. An explicit image-allocation-origin marker defaults to unknown; imported, linear, aliased, sparse, resolving and otherwise unproven attachments retain full ordering. Copies, LRZ workarounds, readbacks, fences and explicit NVN barriers are unchanged. No title, shader key or device exception is introduced.

The producer tags only the eligible backend begin-pass barriers and accumulates shader classes through the target pass's final draw. It can edit a barrier only while the complete target pass and that barrier remain in one unpublished, producer-owned command block. Publication, an unsupported operation or an unknown dependency cancels the candidate. A preceding pass can span blocks because its completed proof owns no packet pointer. No worker-visible packet is changed, no Vulkan command is removed and no extra render-pass split is introduced. The [Khronos pipeline-barrier sample](https://docs.vulkan.org/samples/latest/samples/performance/pipeline_barriers/README.html) explains the intended vertex/fragment overlap; its example's speedup is not an Adreno performance prediction.

Compiled checks exercise the actual entire ordered encoder in both modes, including FIFO argument snapshots, lifecycle/drain paths, multiple barriers, render-pass and dynamic-rendering paths, late shader changes, storage writers, command-buffer changes, unknown operations and long passes. Negative controls reject a late pre-raster dependency bypass, an explicit-NVN-barrier bypass, borrowed argument pointers and modification after publication while the worker is deliberately blocked. Independent compiled ownership checks reject non-null but unproven image memory and the linear/alias/sparse/resolve cases. Removing the origin guard fails its negative control. Shader classification inspects the unchanged 19,592-module bundle; it remains conservative metadata rather than resource-independence or timing evidence.

A screen-off standalone uses the actual complete encoder, actual SPIR-V classifications and independent CPU colour expectations on Adreno 840. Both modes cover 512 D16/D32 depth/comparison cases plus 512 colour-feedback cases. All 2,048 cases have zero colour differences or source-depth mutations. The enabled mode actually narrows 768 barriers; both modes reject 256 target passes whose later draw introduces a vertex texture read. No game assets or validation layer are used by this numerical probe. A copied-save movement/camera/jump/save route completes with thirteen captures, five changed clone files and no red/fatal/crash diagnostics. Complete boots are visible in the inspected landing and after-save frames. This is limited-scene inspection, not whole-game image equivalence. Separate actual-display native-1080p validation reports zero errors/VUIDs and 40,294 narrowed pass barriers, with primary saves and preferences restored afterwards.

The moving same-APK/process ABBA trial holds the ordered encoder, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256 constant. Each window has sixteen seconds of moving warmup and twenty-four seconds of SurfaceFlinger actual-presentation collection. Main MRT geometry is checked at 1920x1080 before and after every window. Per-pass queries remain unarmed during FPS collection; the same submission-segment timing runs in both modes. Sparse GPU/key sampling follows each window, with complete joins, no overflow and matching logical/actual draw counts. Coherent producer counters confirm that narrowed barriers increase only in enabled windows. Thermal status stays zero.

| Begin-pass destination stages | Individual displayed FPS | Pair mean | Replay CPU | Recording wall | Whole frame | GPU submission-span sum |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Original full stages | 56.14 / 55.39 | 55.77 | 8.333 ms | 10.298 ms | 17.934 ms | 15.339 ms |
| Proven fragment/attachment stages | 56.02 / 55.39 | 55.70 | 8.362 ms | 10.394 ms | 17.947 ms | 15.324 ms |

The difference is -0.06 fps, smaller than the 0.75 fps control spread and 0.62 fps enabled spread. No repeatable improvement or precise regression is established. The candidate is withdrawn without adoption. These proven cases do not shorten the measured whole frame; this does not establish that all remaining synchronization is free or justify weakening unknown dependencies. More specific image/resource identities would be needed to broaden the proof to pre-raster texture readers. No performance result is claimed for that unimplemented extension. Native1080p60 remains unachieved, and these experimental-package rates are not normal-release or sustained thermal measurements.

### Exact sampled-image proofs and bounded pass ownership on 2026-10-31

The next private candidate tracks the actual physical images supplied to vertex/geometry descriptors. Comparison snapshots name their depth-image backing rather than the source colour object. Texture-cache hits and stage-descriptor memo hits retain those exact identities. An additional SPIR-V check requires every sampled-image declaration, binding, set and static array count to match supported reflection; missing/duplicate bindings, wrong types/counts, runtime arrays and allocation failure retain unknown status. Independent compiler/validator fixtures pass, including deliberate reflection omissions. Inspection of the unchanged 19,592-entry bundle proves declaration coverage for all 2,981 vertex modules classified as attachment-only writers with image declarations. This is metadata applicability, not evidence that every declared image is used or independent at run time.

The dependency ledger retains all unretired private image writes, including A when C follows B. It cannot replace history with only the immediately preceding pass. A retained full barrier retires the older history. Unknown operations, unavailable image origins, overlapping/linear/sparse/alias storage and capacity overflow retain full ordering. The history has 256 identities, a pre-raster footprint has 64, and publication never leaves a borrowed packet pointer. Compiled checks execute the actual encoder, texture descriptor/cache and stage-memo helpers. Negative controls reject omitted conflicts, last-pass-only history, stale thread-local cache identities, a comparison source substituted for its actual backing, missing memo identities, borrowed arguments and published-packet edits. A separate screen-off GPU oracle runs 3,072 depth, fragment-feedback and independent/nonadjacent vertex-image scenarios across both modes, with all numerical colour expectations and depth-byte checks passing.

The initial copied-save route captures thirteen frames without red/fatal/crash diagnostics, but **fails its required positive extended-image gate**: no extended image proof completes. It is preserved as an incomplete integration result. A CPU-only explanation observer leaves the resource decisions and Vulkan calls unchanged. Its gameplay checkpoint sees 491,658 image-reading draws, of which only 54 still have a pending target candidate; all 54 have known reads and history, yet no image proof completes. These counters do not identify every rejection's cause. The existing single-block publication boundary prevents analysis of long targets from changing an already shared barrier.

A follow-up retains at most four producer-private blocks from the existing sixteen-block pool while a target pass completes. These blocks have not been published to the worker. Resolution publishes them in FIFO order. Explicit drains, capacity exhaustion and free-block pressure instead publish with the original full barriers; waiting for a free block while withholding blocks is forbidden. No GPU fence, readback, LRZ workaround, copy or explicit NVN barrier is removed. Native tests cover an 800-draw target, a conflicting final draw after that long prefix, a blocked worker, a full free-block pool, capacity fallback, lifecycle paths and immutable forced publication. Removing the pressure release deadlocks the deliberately blocked case and is rejected by its bounded watchdog; removing the private-block cap also fails.

The final GPU oracle adds 320-draw targets spanning blocks, including cases whose last draw introduces the older-image dependency. Across both modes, all 4,096 depth/colour scenarios pass. The enabled path narrows 1,792 barriers, including 1,024 extended image proofs; the disabled path narrows none. Its enabled history detects 1,280 image conflicts. Actual colour results are checked against independent CPU expectations, and the original depth reference/mutation checks pass. These standalone probes use no game assets or validation layer.

The bounded-hold game route completes with thirteen captures, five changed clone files and no red/fatal/crash diagnostics. Complete boots remain visible in the inspected landing capture. It records 26,054 narrowed barriers and 1,914 held blocks, with a one-block observed peak and five pressure releases. Only **one extended image proof** completes, so this does not establish sustained applicability to the scene's textured vertex passes. Separate native-1080p actual-display validation completes with zero errors/VUIDs and also observes one extended proof. The normal runtime and installed release are restored after validation.

An initial FPS attempt is explicitly cancelled and excluded in full. The preparation command used the wrong working directory, so its intended activity-gate update did not reach the runner before launch. The agent verifies the live test-root identity before stopping that package; the existing harness then restores the release, saves/settings and display. Its original runner, incomplete result and cancellation record are preserved. The subsequent separate runner requires narrowed-barrier and held-block activity in each enabled measurement window and no such activity in disabled windows. It also records whether the extended texture branch runs, without attributing throughput to an unexercised branch. This changes the applicability claim, not the native-1080p, movement, save-integrity or correctness requirements.

The completed same-APK/process ABBA comparison holds the encoder, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256 constant. Sixteen seconds of moving warmup precede each twenty-four-second actual-presentation measurement. Main MRT geometry is audited at 1920x1080 before/after every window; pass queries remain unarmed during FPS collection and the same submission-segment timestamps run in both modes. Post-window sparse GPU/key joins are complete with no overflow or command-count differences. Thermal status stays zero.

### Command-buffer context investigation on 2026-10-31

A private per-draw metadata observer first tests whether cached pipeline bindings lose their shader-access class. It snapshots the classes actually used by draw preparation, including cache hits, but leaves its new decision path disabled on the tablet. The completed screen-off copied-save run sees 969,417 annotated draws, 940,638 inside the tracked pass, and **zero** class mismatches or source/pre-raster/texture recoveries. The remaining 28,779 draws have no tracked open pass. This evidence refutes class repair as the next optimization for this scene; the artificial compiled cases are not a reason to enable an inapplicable path. Numerical GPU/performance testing of that unused class-repair branch is not claimed. Evidence: `draw-access-{source-manifest,unit-verification,producer-verification,build-manifest}.json` and `contact-draw-access-observe-20261031-031715-*`.

A separate CPU-only observer records the first cause that clears a tracked open pass. Its Vulkan calls and resource decisions remain unchanged. Two independently reset steady phases are captured before any geometry audit or frame-export instrumentation, each with two complete 600-frame snapshots. Standing has 139,871 outside-pass draws, all attributed to beginning another command buffer. Moving has 189,614 such draws: 189,119 (99.74%) follow another buffer's begin and 495 follow a buffer switch. Timestamp writes also clear an open pass, but are followed by its end rather than further outside-pass draws in these windows. These are metadata-loss counts, not GPU durations or percentages of total frame cost. The runtime begins private upload buffers while the main buffer is still recording and submits uploads before that main part on the same queue. A global metadata reset at an unrelated begin loses information that still belongs to the main buffer. Evidence: `draw-loss-{source-manifest,unit-verification,build-manifest}.json` and `contact-draw-loss-20261031-033719-*`, including the independently preserved standing/moving logs.

The next default-off private candidate banks resource state by command buffer, bounded at 64 contexts. Beginning, ending or resetting one buffer invalidates that buffer; pool/descriptor resets, queue submission/waits, synchronous fallback and scope end retain conservative whole-scope invalidation. Unknown GPU commands invalidate only their active context. Every context must retain its first full global barrier before any narrowing, including after an unknown main-buffer write. This anchor covers the uploads submitted before main work; no cross-queue or arbitrary external-submission ordering is inferred. Pure attachment source passes and exact unretired image history remain required. Private timestamp/query-reset operations preserve the resource metadata while their actual commands and query ordering remain intact, consistent with the Vulkan definitions of [query reset](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/vkCmdResetQueryPool.html) and [timestamp writes](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/vkCmdWriteTimestamp.html). No GPU fence, readback, LRZ policy or explicit NVN barrier is removed.

All pending candidates, including those in inactive contexts, are cancelled before their packets are published. The existing four-block private hold scans inactive contexts and retains FIFO release, capacity fallback and free-block-pressure release. Native tests run the real worker and preserve the parent resource/ownership tests with the feature disabled. Added cases exercise interleaved upload begins, existing upload-buffer recording, cached pipeline state, nonadjacent image dependencies, private queries, unknown main writes, handle reuse, ten lifecycle paths, bank overflow and held-block pressure. Deliberately omitting the first anchor, inactive publication protection, state save, handle invalidation or capacity reset is rejected. A worker blocked before an inactive candidate makes illegal edits after publication deterministic rather than depending on a timing race.

The screen-off Adreno oracle runs 7,168 depth, feedback, image-history, long-target and interleaved-upload scenarios across both context modes, with zero numerical colour/depth differences or unavailable query results. New vertex shaders consume an actual GPU-filled uniform buffer and independently expected clipping. The main CB reuses pipeline/descriptor state while an upload CB records, then the two buffers are submitted upload-first. Parent resource scopes and bounded hold remain enabled in both modes. The context-enabled run restores 1,536 open passes and keeps 15,360 first full barriers; narrowed barriers increase from 1,792 to 4,352. These synthetic applicability counts do not measure game throughput.

The native-1080p copied-save route completes thirteen captures, movement, camera turns, a jump, menus and saving. Five clone files change and no red/fatal/crash lines appear. Complete boots remain visible in the inspected landing and after-save frames. The candidate records 7,778 context restores, including 3,491 open-pass restores, with no bank overflow. It retains 180,136 first full barriers and 19,296 private query commands; 24,088 barriers narrow. The extended image-proof branch still completes only once, so broad applicability to textured vertex passes is not established. Main MRT geometry is 1920x1080 before and after the route; the physical display remains OFF. ### Constant-membership buffer lookup cells on 2026-10-31

A private default-off candidate adds four copied-handle lookup cells per thread alongside the existing 64-entry exact-query cache. The original priority-ordered range search also computes an interval in which the set of pools containing a point is constant. The nearest later start bounds its right edge; endpoints visited by the existing search and the skipped prefix maximum bound its left edge. This needs no additional registry scan or endpoint index. The lowest current slot remains the point winner throughout that interval. A larger request may reuse that winner only if the whole request still fits in its pool; otherwise the original exact-length lookup runs. Registration/removal retains the original release/acquire generation invalidation. No mutable registry pointer is cached. Sample verification deliberately uses the original index, not the new interval helper, and repairs a corrupted snapshot. No GPU command or memory-publication rule changes.

Host and ARM64 tests each pass 1,540,113 independent queries, exhaustive membership checks inside the small test cells, larger requests, overflow, scalar handle reuse and sampled repair. Both architectures also pass the original exact-cache overlap, swap-removal, thread-handoff and concurrent-registration fixture with cells off and on. Removing the skipped-prefix bound, later-start bound, complete-request containment check or generation check is rejected. A distinct-address sequence requires one locked lookup followed by 4,095 cell hits. The ARM64 tests use no Vulkan calls or game assets; they are CPU lookup tests, not GPU numerical or performance tests.

The native-1080p copied-save route completes thirteen captures, movement, camera turns, jumping, menus and saving, with five changed clone files and no red/fatal/crash diagnostics. Inspected landing and after-save frames retain complete boots. Its cumulative cell counts are 7,430,328 hits, 1,304,082 builds and 120,379 sampled checks with zero differences. The overall lookup cache completes 307,319 checks with zero differences. Main MRT geometry is 1920x1080 before/after and the physical display stays OFF. Separate actual-display validation has zero errors/VUIDs, 208,125 cell checks and 479,566 overall lookup checks, all matching the original index.

Post-window sparse GPU/key samples have complete joins, no overflow and no logical/actual command-count differences. Cell hits/builds advance in every enabled window and do not advance in disabled windows. Thermal status remains zero.

### Descriptor memo first-failure and buffer-provenance census on 2026-10-31

Two private observers preserve the original memo guard order and descriptor refresh callbacks. The first records the first failed condition and a bounded history of shader metadata; the second annotates the existing buffer-description result as native-pool, immutable shadow or fallback. Neither observer changes a cache decision, performs an extra resource lookup or changes the Vulkan call sequence. Producer-owned counters publish coherent snapshots after replay through a mutex. Configuration/reset tests and concurrent publication tests exercise the actual original and instrumented memo functions across 200,000 decisions; 20,000 descriptor cases separately compare the actual provenance annotations, original return values and callback effects. Deliberately wrong reasons, reset handling, dirty-history treatment, dynamic-offset decisions, publication state and omitted provenance assignments are rejected.

Both copied-save observations keep the physical display OFF and verify 1920x1080 main MRT geometry before and after. Movement continues while collecting the two moving snapshots. The first observer records 9,001,726 moving shader-stage attempts, with 7,026,222 memo hits (78.05%). Its first failures are shader changes 759,572, binding changes 419,639 and uniform-buffer changes 716,728; other reasons account for the remainder. The provenance observer records 9,076,246 moving attempts and 7,142,273 hits (78.69%). Its first failures include shader changes 761,139, binding changes 328,655 and uniform-buffer changes 751,944. These are event counts, not percentages of CPU time or GPU duration; later conditions can also differ after the first failure.

Of the latter 751,944 first-buffer failures, 747,283 (99.38%) change from one native-pool buffer to another. Pool-to-shadow and shadow-to-shadow transitions account for 2,327 and 2,334 respectively. The average new descriptor range of the first failed native-pool buffer is 273.58 bytes. Summed first-failure ranges are not a measurement of total copied bytes. Enlarging the temporary uniform-shadow allocator would therefore miss almost all observed buffer-handle failures. Moving-window shader-history metadata matches are at most 1.49% and 1.58% of all attempts in the two observations, and most still require the original dirty-buffer refresh. These upper bounds do not support a broadly useful multi-shader memo cache. A metadata match alone does not prove resource validity or restore stage-side image lists.

This census selects a standard descriptor-update-template experiment for unavoidable updates, preserving identical layouts, resource values, dynamic offsets and synchronization. It provides no FPS result or adoption decision. The observer endpoint verifies all thirteen normal source hashes, normal loader/HLE/shader bundle and normal debug/release outputs. A temporary normal debug installation, without launching the game or waking the display, verifies all sixteen primary-save hashes before restoring the non-debuggable release `4ceb9b6fed27f852b8719c6a67c572a4962773deb484ab7051e99460e3ca60db`. Original preferences, GPU-debug globals and driver properties match; owned processes are stopped and the physical display remains OFF. ### Descriptor update templates on 2026-10-31

A private default-off transport uses the Vulkan 1.1 descriptor update template API, without changing descriptor allocation/reuse, layouts, buffer/image handles, ranges, dynamic offsets, shader translation or GPU synchronization. Each shader caches a bounded update shape of at most 96 writes and 16 KiB of typed descriptor data. Unsupported API/entry shapes, extension chains, allocation failures and later shape mismatches preserve ordinary updates. This copies descriptor metadata, not uniform-buffer contents. The optional FIFO worker owns every payload byte; unknown-size public updates drain and execute synchronously, and template destruction drains queued host uses. The [Vulkan update API](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/vkUpdateDescriptorSetWithTemplate.html) and [entry format](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkDescriptorUpdateTemplateEntry.html) define equivalent descriptor contents, not a performance guarantee.

Host tests compare 20,000 changing-resource updates across all eleven core descriptor types, including arrays and nonzero destination array elements. Bounds, unsupported types, extension chains, creation failures, unavailable entry points and fallback paths are checked. The whole original worker fixture still passes with 56 hooks. A blocked-worker payload-poison test verifies ownership, and destruction verifies queued updates finish first. Deliberately wrong strides, wrong payload sources, borrowed pointers and missing destruction drains are rejected. An asset-free Adreno numerical oracle runs 256 cases with the worker disabled and 256 enabled; ten core descriptor types, dynamic UBO/SSBO arrays, static buffers, combined/separate sampled images, storage images and both texel-buffer types produce zero mismatches against independent CPU vectors. Input attachments are covered by the host fixture, not this compute oracle.

The copied-save game route completes fourteen captures including movement, camera turns, jumping, menus and saving; five clone files change. Inspected landing output retains complete boots. Main MRT geometry remains 1920x1080 before/after, the physical display stays OFF and no red/fatal/crash diagnostic appears. Templates perform 1,351,538 updates with zero regular fallbacks. Separate actual-display validation finds zero errors/VUIDs, performs 1,370,068 template updates with zero fallbacks and passes 339,547 sampled original buffer-lookup checks without a difference.

The same-APK, same-process movement ABBA trial retains sixteen-second warmups, twenty-four-second actual-presentation windows, the existing optional worker, hardware-derived prime placement and tail 256 in both modes. Per-pass GPU queries remain unarmed during FPS collection. All windows verify main MRT geometry before/after, complete post-window GPU/shader-key joins, unchanged critical-thread affinity and thermal status zero. Enabled windows advance template updates by 1,166,440 and 1,165,996; disabled windows advance none. The measured ordinary-update windows are 57.14 and 55.96 fps (mean 56.55); template windows are 55.09 and 56.41 fps (mean 55.75). The difference is -0.80 fps, smaller than the largest 1.32 fps within-condition spread. Replay CPU means are 8.159 versus 8.215 ms, recording-wall means 10.258 versus 10.333 ms, and GPU submission-span means 15.166 versus 15.327 ms. These overlapping spans are not additive costs or isolated shader-active time.

No throughput or CPU-time improvement is observed. The trial does not establish a precise regression; it provides no reason to adopt the transport or spend a second moving trial seeking a repeatable gain. The candidate is withdrawn. Normal source/runtime bytes and production/installed release remain unchanged. The endpoint checks all sixteen primary-save hashes from the final post-trial archive, original preferences, GPU-debug globals/driver properties, the non-debuggable release and stopped owned processes with physical display OFF. Sustained moving native1080p60 remains unachieved. ### Offscreen moving CPU leaf samples on 2026-10-31

Two fresh copied-save standalone runs collect twelve seconds of user CPU-cycle samples at 199 Hz, with the physical display OFF, native-1080p geometry, the existing optional worker/prime placement/tail 256, and ordinary descriptor updates. NVN per-call tracing and pool-invalidation tracing are disabled. The initial DWARF capture records 3,805 samples with none lost but its eighteen-second watchdog expires during post-processing. Its recovered file parses with exactly all 3,805 samples and matching event count; callchain completion is unproven. This diagnostic failure is retained rather than relabeled a successful run. A second frame-pointer capture allows bounded post-processing and exits normally, recording 3,831 samples with none lost across a 12.066-second sample span. Before/after complete-frame MRT audits remain 1920x1080, and no red/fatal/crash line appears. These are offscreen CPU samples, not display-FPS or thermal results.

In the completed sample, the presenting/replay thread's largest self-cycle entries are `stage_set` 14.06%, `prepare_draw` 10.25%, libc's non-temporal copy 8.81%, `exec_ops` 5.99%, `vk_ubd_shadow` 5.94% and `vk_buf_range_at` 5.02%. These fractions are weighted sampled user CPU cycles within that thread, not percentages of whole-frame wall time or guaranteed removable work. They favor investigating repeated state/resource preparation and parameter-copy work before another descriptor transport change. Across the whole process, logged module mappings attribute 46.95% of sampled cycles to guest main, 3.00% to guest SDK, 24.07% to the host executable, 13.87% to the Qualcomm Vulkan driver and 11.95% to libc. Cycle weights across differently clocked threads are not CPU-time shares.

Repeated anonymous leaf addresses resolve to two exclusive-load/store acquire sequences in the owned main module. Its local and installed file hashes match; disassembly confirms acquire-load, conditional clear-exclusive/retry and store-exclusive/retry instructions. The selected acquire instruction ranges contain 12.35% and 15.95% of whole-process sampled cycles in the recovered and completed captures. They occur in the main/game-worker threads, with zero samples in the identified presenting/replay thread. Neither capture counts failed acquisitions or proves all these cycles are busy-wait waste. Consequently a general atomic/waiting optimization remains a hypothesis; the samples do not establish critical-path lock contention, justify changing a lock protocol or predict an FPS gain. No guest module is changed. Evidence: `headless_cpu_sampling.py`, `analyze_cpu_sampling.py`, `contact-cpu-sampling-20261031-{061736,062739}-*`, `cpu-sampling-leaf-analysis.json` and `cpu-sampling-hot-disassembly.txt`.

### Native exclusive-lock replacement measurements on 2026-10-31

The 298-candidate census led to an asset-free ARM64 primitive test on the target tablet, rather than rewriting the game. Three six-instruction acquire forms are compared: the existing LDAXR/STXR loop, acquire CASA, and LDAR polling with acquire CASA. Both 32-bit and 64-bit forms pass 60,000 independent checks of the written value, unrelated registers, NZCV and exclusive-monitor closure. A deliberately broken no-lock implementation is rejected. Forty-eight contention cases cover 1/2/4/8 threads and 18,000,000 acquisitions, with counter/checksum and mutual-exclusion checks. HWCAP_ATOMICS gates LSE execution.

For 32-bit locks, the original/CASA/poll-CASA mean wall nanoseconds per acquisition are 8.65/11.45/11.58 at one thread, 33.10/57.18/26.38 at two, 29.69/57.48/36.59 at four and 55.07/216.75/71.15 at eight. The two-thread control varies widely, so its apparent poll-CASA win does not establish a stable improvement. No consistently faster replacement is established; coarse process-CPU-clock deltas are not used as precise CPU-time measurements. No guest image or production atomic implementation changes. This closes the proposed easy LSE substitution without a game FPS claim. Evidence: `lse-lock-study/`, `lse-lock-probe-{verification,analysis}.json`, `build_lse_lock_probe.py`, `run_lse_lock_probe.py`, and `lse-lock-candidate/artifact-index.json`.

### Generic NVN buffer field ABI correction on 2026-10-31

The upstream Apple commit [f5cc73d0](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/commit/f5cc73d0c1cb9bd14a216ccbead2083eb27540d8) identifies a real SDK contract: guest ring allocators can directly update an NVNbuffer's offset at byte 0x18 and size at byte 0x20. The previous compact host object put those fields eight bytes earlier. The owned SDK 20 original-driver oracle independently confirms the same initialized values and public getter loads. Eight reserved bytes now align the two fields with the original driver's contract; compile-time offset assertions and designated initialization preserve that layout. This corrects those guest-visible fields, without claiming byte identity for every other opaque object field or changing the buffer builder's private layout.

The new test reads/writes literal 0x18/0x20 offsets independently of the C field names, checks ring wrap/zero/edge cases with CPU-accessible and inaccessible pools, all public address/size/map/pool queries, builder independence and adjacent object guards. It reproduces a semantic failure against the archived old HLE. The full root suite passes 104 tests with no skips after explicitly providing the installed NDK and native Yaz0 decoder.

Full-frame MRT audits remain 1920x1080 before and after; selected standing/down-view/walking/rotated/after-save images show no apparent regression. Separate actual-display Khronos validation completes with zero errors/VUIDs and 338,888 original buffer-lookup checks with zero differences. All sixteen primary-save file hashes remain unchanged. This is a correctness correction; no new FPS improvement is claimed.

The correction is retained in root HLE SHA-256 `88a551b3febd2fced1b3c76002f4bfc7b2fc889e2f7f51bd4d68ba7bb128b84c`. Normal graphics/loader sources and shader bundle remain unchanged. New verified normal debug/release packages are `buffer-abi-debug.apk` / `buffer-abi-release.apk`; release SHA-256 `41a379882247763a1baada380db1f59e7ae81bfe1d520891e1e0c9af02579423` is both published and installed, non-debuggable, with matching normal host outputs. The previous 4ceb release and host outputs remain archived. Original preferences and GPU-debug globals/driver properties are verified, owned game processes are stopped and the physical display is OFF. `current-normal-build-manifest.json` identifies this new baseline; older closure scripts that explicitly guard the f9c8 HLE/4ceb package are historical and must not be run as current-baseline checks. ### App-local Turnip numerical bring-up on 2026-10-31

Pinned libadrenotools `8fae8ce254dfc1344527e05301e43f37dea2df80` is built from source, including its pinned namespace-bypass submodule. Verified release archives provide whitebelyash mainline V32 and stable V2 drivers. A temporary independent NativeActivity loads each driver from its extracted internal native-library directory through libadrenotools's public custom-driver API. No turbo ioctl, KGSL power setting, global GPU setting or game source is changed. The first harness attempt failed before Vulkan at an external-log-file permission assertion; those six attempts are invalid driver results and are preserved separately. Moving logs/config/shader input to the app-owned internal directory resolves the harness issue.

The corrected run verifies actual VkPhysicalDeviceDriverProperties: stock Qualcomm driver ID 8, mainline Mesa Turnip ID 18 (26.3.0-devel git-9c7e022677, API 1.4.363) and stable Mesa Turnip ID 18 (26.2.3 git-fbc44a3bcc, API 1.4.354). Every driver passes 256 independent CPU-vector compute cases over ten descriptor types with both serial and encoding-worker submission, for six successful runs. Both Turnip builds lack VK_EXT_depth_range_unrestricted, which the stock driver exposes; floating-point comparison snapshots must therefore retain their unsupported-path guard rather than silently change semantics. These numerical results establish loader feasibility, not game rendering correctness or a speed improvement. The temporary probe application is removed, the normal game package remains installed and the physical display is OFF. Evidence: `turnip-study/asset-verification.json`, `turnip-study/adrenotools-build.log`, `turnip-study/numeric-probe/{build-verification.json,device-20261031-081657/verification.json,invalid-external-log-root/}`, `prepare_turnip_probe.py` and `run_turnip_numeric_probe_internal.py`.

### Turnip game incompatibility and current-stock movement retest on 2026-10-31

The private app-local selector reaches the title on mainline V32, but the copied-save outdoor transition reports `comparison sampling has no compatible depth representation: view format 100, parent 100` (R32_SFLOAT). The host's existing fail-closed guard is reached because this driver does not expose VK_EXT_depth_range_unrestricted. Stable V2 reports the same absent prerequisite in the numeric capability query; its full-game FPS test is not attempted. The [Vulkan copy specification](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/Vulkan-Docs/blob/v1.3.296/chapters/copies.adoc) defines out-of-[0,1] data copied into the depth aspect as undefined without the extension, so deleting the guard or clamping values would not preserve the existing general contract. The game test is ineligible for a performance comparison. No custom loader or driver is adopted into normal source/packages. A generic comparison implementation would be required before testing these drivers' game throughput.

A separate fresh-process stock-driver run with the retained buffer ABI correction measures actual SurfaceFlinger presentation during 24 seconds of continuous walking and camera turns after 24 seconds of moving warmup. The window contains 1,360 presentations across 24.751 seconds: **54.946 fps**, median 16.668 ms, P95 25.001 ms, P99 33.335 ms and maximum 150.008 ms; four intervals exceed 50 ms and two exceed 100 ms. Complete-frame MRT audits remain native 1920x1080 before/after. Both critical host threads retain their prime-core masks; thermal status is zero before/after, the optional encoder/tail-256 setup is unchanged, and no per-pass GPU query is armed during measurement. This is a current performance observation, not a causal ABI performance comparison or a new repeatable gain. Sustained native1080p60 remains unachieved.

The stock window logs mean frame 18.195 ms, replay caller CPU 8.305 ms, recording wall 10.341 ms, dependency-inclusive GPU submission span 15.574 ms and explicit pacing wait 0.172 ms. These overlapping intervals must not be added as separate serial costs or interpreted as individual-pass GPU work. At the mean alone, reaching 16.667 ms would require about 1.53 ms less frame time, with the long intervals still to address. The measurement does not establish a single bottleneck or a new driver speed result.

A read-only census decodes 31,144,615 instructions across all 19,592 frozen SPIR-V modules, using the [Khronos opcode grammar](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/SPIRV-Headers/blob/main/include/spirv/unified1/spirv.core.grammar.json). Its 1,486 comparison shaders contain 3,534 OpImageSampleDrefImplicitLod and 210 OpImageSampleDrefExplicitLod operations; no DrefGather instruction exists in the bundle. All 409 shader keys from the earlier sampled moving trace resolve; ten of those shaders contain 23 implicit and three explicit Dref samples. A gather-only lowering cannot address this workload. Opcode coverage alone does not prove image/sampler provenance, filter/LOD semantics, mixed-use safety or rewrite eligibility. No shader, reflection, chain version or guest module changes in this investigation.

The final endpoint verifies all current normal runtime/loader/HLE hashes, matching normal host outputs and production/installed non-debuggable release 41a37988, the unchanged shader bundle, all sixteen primary-save hashes, original preferences and GPU-debug globals/driver properties. The temporary numeric-probe app is removed, owned game/test processes are stopped and the physical display is OFF. Evidence: `turnip-game-tu_v32-validation-20261031-082159/`, `turnip-game-system-bench-20261031-082632/`, `turnip-study/dref-{opcode-definitions,opcode-census}.json`, `audit_dref_operations.py`, `turnip-game-{source-manifest,build-manifest,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification}.json`, and `turnip-game-candidate/artifact-index.json`.


### Constant comparison compatibility and independently traced driver trials on 2026-10-31

A private input observer finds 23 distinct comparison contracts on the copied-save Plateau route. All eight observed R32_SFLOAT contracts use an actually enabled ALWAYS comparison; this observation does not authorize a shader-key or game-ID shortcut. A generic, default-off candidate recognizes actual immutable sampler handles with enabled NEVER/ALWAYS comparison, creates an initialized shape-identical depth surrogate, and retains the original shader and sampler. Source-dependent comparison paths, format guards, cache identity and fenced lifetimes remain separate. This avoids the unsupported floating-point depth copy only where the result is independent of its payload. It does not implement arbitrary R32 comparison on drivers lacking the depth-range extension. No shader translation, bundle or guest-module change is made.

The actual candidate helpers pass host shape/lifetime/failure, concurrent sampler and descriptor-cache tests, including negative controls. An independent GPU oracle passes 460,800 stock-driver and 276,480 cases on each Turnip driver, with 13,224 raw-texel checks per driver and no Vulkan validation errors. Both mainline V32 and stable V2 then complete fresh-save outdoor game validation at native 1920x1080 with no reported validation errors. These checks establish the scoped compatibility path, not complete-game correctness or a throughput gain.

A new bounded Perfetto collection measures actual native-window PresentFenceSignaled events without polling frame statistics during the measurement window. This NativeActivity exposes no application FrameTimeline rows. Explicit device BOOTTIME bounds, trace-loss checks and a single post-window SurfaceFlinger snapshot cross-check the presentation timestamps: 110-112 fences per run match within 52 ns. Recording itself still has instrumentation overhead. A one-second tail drain captures retrospectively exported events; configuration belongs in the Perfetto-readable config directory. Earlier permission and recorder-termination harness failures are retained as invalid captures rather than driver failures.

Independent trace windows after moving warmup measure **56.478 fps on stable V2, 56.949 fps on mainline V32 and 57.021 fps on the stock driver**, each across approximately 24 seconds, with native 1920x1080 MRT audits before/after. P99 intervals are respectively 25.002, 33.335 and 33.335 ms; maxima are 41.669, 133.341 and 50.003 ms. Input repeats a 0.4-second walking or camera press each second and therefore does not establish sustained continuous movement or all-scene performance. Earlier M-S-S-M polling windows measured mainline 57.039/56.914 and stock 56.153/56.517 fps; the independent trace results do not establish a repeatable driver winner. A causal stock constant-on/off comparison remains undone. Sustained native1080p60 remains unachieved.

The stock trace averages replay caller CPU 7.510 ms, recording wall 9.638 ms and dependency-inclusive GPU span 15.027 ms. These overlap and cannot be added as serial costs. A suspected modulo-300 ADPF stall is not established: observed long frames do not share that phase. No ADPF threading change follows from the current evidence. The candidate and custom drivers remain private; all retained normal source hashes and production/installed non-debuggable release 41a37988 are unchanged. ### Deferred comparison submission rejected; engine scheduling reference on 2026-10-31

A private, default-off checkpoint experiment delays the full submission restart after a content-dependent comparison snapshot until a later prepended image upload would require it. Pending reads belong to the recording slot and retire only when their exact command buffers occur in a successful physical submission, including early parts. Image-upload preparation precedes slot-local staging and buffer resolution, and an upload-entry guard rejects an unsatisfied dependency. Host tests exercise 10,000 selective-retirement rounds, all 32 pending main buffers, the unchanged default-off allocation/subresource oracle and five negative controls. The real-GPU oracle passes stock/mainline/stable with zero data or validation mismatches: 96/48/48 upload-order rounds and 317,376/158,688/158,688 checked texels, in addition to the parent sampler tests. It verifies old snapshot versus new source data, natural retirement and unrelated queue submissions.

The initial full-game validation correctly rejects a draw issued outside an active render pass: the first draft preserved the command buffer without preserving the pass-state generation invalidation that had accompanied a full restart. This failed revision and matching packages are retained in `deferred-compare-first-attempt/`; its game run is invalid for performance. The corrected draft retains immediate generation invalidation even when the physical submission is delayed. Host/GPU checks are repeated, and a fresh-save native-1080p full-game run then reports no validation errors or VUIDs. It records 1,436 protected/retired snapshot prefixes and one upload-forced full restart. The inspected screenshot shows complete boots and plausible local terrain; it is scoped visual evidence, not a full rendering proof.

A same-APK on/off/off/on comparison uses independent fresh processes/saves, moving warmup and approximately 24-second native-1080p presentation-fence windows. Rates are **49.377 / 55.224 / 56.245 / 49.461 fps**. Enabled mean **49.419** versus disabled **55.735 fps** is a repeatable **11.33% regression**. Replay recording wall remains approximately 10.25 ms in both modes; dependency-inclusive GPU submission spans increase from approximately 15.34 to 16.48 ms. Almost all snapshot-forced restarts disappear, but throughput worsens. This is consistent with reduced CPU/GPU overlap; isolated GPU busy time and the exact critical path are not established. The candidate is rejected. No normal source, package or preference adopts it, and sustained native1080p60 remains unachieved.

At the user's request, eight official Unreal Engine RHI/VulkanRHI source files are read through the machine's existing authenticated GitHub CLI, pinned to release commit `f45729c0b6f097038e72b52d366a62baeca3275b`. The public connector returns 404 while the existing CLI has private-repository access; no new account or permission flow is needed. In RHICommandList.cpp, parallel translation is conditional on backend support, finalized command lists are submitted in order, and deferred batching defaults off to reduce pipeline latency. Translation chains split at command-count and parallel/serial or parent/child boundaries; each uses an explicit command context. Vulkan context finalization closes active rendering, and command-buffer pools recycle objects/allocations. These provide a concrete next experiment: retain eager ordered comparison-prefix submission, reuse the current submission slot where safe, and fall back to a full restart when early submission is disabled or capacity is exhausted. The 256-command translation threshold is not a draw-count recommendation for this backend. No Unreal source is copied into the runtime.

Final verification confirms all retained normal runtime/loader/HLE hashes, matching normal host outputs and production/installed non-debuggable release 41a37988, the unchanged shader bundle, the last benchmark's sixteen primary-save hashes, original preferences and GPU-debug globals/properties. The temporary GPU oracle is removed, game and owned recorders are stopped, and physical display is OFF. Evidence: `deferred-compare-{source-manifest,build-manifest,unit-verification,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification}.json`, `deferred-compare-gpu/device-20261031-104843/verification.json`, `deferred-compare-system-validation-20261031-104919/`, four `deferred-compare-system-c1-d*-trace-bench-*` folders and `engine-reference-20261031/{source-metadata,review}.json`. The descriptor-transport harness drafts prepared during this investigation have not been run and provide no new transport evidence.

The read-only policy audit finds battery saver disabled and standard game mode; idle maximum-frequency limits do not establish foreground limits. ### Source-level synchronization attribution on 2026-10-31

A new private, default-off `DM_TRACE_SYNC=1` observer adds Android ATrace scopes to the ordered encoding worker, NVN submission/fence waits, submission-slot recycle, GPU and host completion, present queues, swapchain acquisition, and generic Horizon wait objects. The collector enables app trace markers together with scheduling/frequency events. Every instrumented source reconstructs its parent byte for byte after reversing the recorded additions; the actual cleanup-scope header and guest-wait wrapper pass seven startup selections, 600 cycles per selection, 10,800 preserved wait returns per selection and three rejected negative controls. The normal source files are restored before packaging. No wait condition, return value, submission order or shader translation rule is changed.

The actual-display outdoor run uses a fresh copied save and verifies complete-frame 1920x1080 MRT geometry before/after. The bounded 24.063-second trace has 1,335 actual presentations, 1,337 host-present counter events and 554,545 observed scopes, with no trace loss or parser errors. Its 55.480 fps includes instrumentation overhead and is **not** a release performance result. Warmup and measurement alternate 0.4-second movement/camera inputs each second; sound is disabled. This is a bounded diagnostic route, not a sustained audio-enabled gameplay acceptance test.

Using the 1,337 observed host presents as the denominator, the NVN caller spends **2.387 ms/present** in encoding drains (24,320 calls, about 18.2/present): 1.472 ms sleeping, 0.663 ms ready but unscheduled, and 0.252 ms running inside the drain. Its complete NVN submission scope averages 10.496 ms, including 8.314 ms running. The caller also waits on one guest condition for 6.849 ms/present. A different game thread spends **5.339 ms/present** in `mtl_wait`, waiting for GPU completion plus CPU-visible effects/retirement; essentially that entire interval overlaps the caller's condition wait. During this host completion wait, 4.234 ms/present overlaps the completion thread waiting on the GPU timeline and **1.063 ms/present overlaps post-GPU CPU cleanup**. The cleanup scope is dominated by command-buffer reset in this capture. These intersections identify a pipeline dependency; they do not measure isolated GPU busy time or removable latency.

Swapchain acquisition averages 0.158 ms/present on the separate presentation thread, present-ticket wait 0.042 ms on the caller, Vulkan queue mutex acquisition 0.009 ms on the caller, and the caller's direct host completion wait only 0.001 ms. The large completion-thread fence wait and worker idle scopes must not be added to caller costs: their work and waits overlap. The next falsifiable generic experiments are separating guest-visible completion from command-buffer retirement while retaining per-slot reuse safety, and reducing encoding drains only where owned data and Vulkan ordering allow it. Neither is implemented or claimed faster by this observation.

Evidence is indexed by `sync-trace-artifact-index.json`: the source/unit/build manifests, raw Perfetto trace/config and SQL/CSV exports, `sync-trace-profile-system-c1-h100-trace-bench-20261031-130736/{sync-analysis.json,sync-dependencies.json,schedule-analysis.json}`, and `sync-trace-investigation-summary.json`. The final endpoint verification checks normal debug/release host outputs, installed/published release `41a37988...`, unchanged shader bundle `716c5c60...`, restored source/preferences, the sixteen unchanged primary-save hashes from the last test endpoint, clean GPU settings, no owned app/recorder, and physical display OFF. Normal runtime/defaults remain unchanged; sustained native1080p60 is still unachieved.


### Guest-visible completion separated from retirement on 2026-10-31

The private `DM_VK_GUEST_NOTIFY=1` candidate preserves the existing internal `g_done` retirement boundary, `mtl_wait`, slot recycle, finish/drain and resource-completion queries. Only NVN `SyncWait` observes a new earlier boundary. The completion thread publishes it after the GPU timeline, copied-pool readback, query processing and every CPU-visible post operation, before resetting command buffers. A separate mutex/condition pair preserves release/acquire visibility. Android's optional backend hook falls back to the original wait for null/other backends; Apple's path is unchanged. No title, shader key or module address selects this optimization.

Compiled tests execute the actual helpers with reset deliberately held open: an enabled guest observer can return while internal retirement and slot reuse remain blocked. Three startup selections each cover 100 gated concurrent rounds, 2,000 slot-mismatch rounds, zero/short waits, no-slot and no-pre-reset cases. Negative controls reject late guest observation, premature slot retirement and ignored opt-in. The unchanged internal functions compare byte for byte against the parent; the original GPU/query/post-effect prefix precedes guest notification. Three optional-backend linking cases pass. Full-game stock Vulkan validation at native 1920x1080 reports zero errors/VUIDs and 1,474 observations before retirement. Evidence: `guest-notify-{source-manifest,unit-verification,build-manifest}.json` and `guest-notify-system-validation-20261031-132850/result.json`.

Same-APK actual-presentation on/off/off/on windows measure **56.814 / 55.429 / 55.785 / 58.161 fps**: enabled **57.487 fps**, disabled **55.607 fps**, a **3.38%** mean improvement. Both enabled windows exceed both controls, but the 24-second, partly held-input, silent windows do not prove sustained 60 fps. The implementation remains private/default-off pending further work. A separate validated descriptor-buffer on/off/off/on comparison keeps early guest notification enabled and yields **57.960 / 57.065 / 57.208 / 57.508 fps**, means **57.734 / 57.136 (+1.05%)**. This smaller difference remains experimental. The inspected images retain in-world terrain and visible feet; they are scoped visual checks, not cross-mode pixel equivalence. Evidence: `guest-notify-investigation-summary.json` and `guest-notify-descriptor-investigation-summary.json`.

A longer real-audio run warms for 60 seconds and then holds alternating simultaneous movement/camera inputs continuously for 120 seconds. Actual presentation is **58.183 fps** across 120.053 seconds and 6,985 presentations; P95 16.668 ms, P99 33.335 ms, maximum 158.342 ms, thirteen intervals over 50 ms and four over 100 ms. All measured frame-step records are one, thermal status remains zero, and full-frame MRT audits before/after remain 1920x1080. The log verifies 48,000 real device audio callbacks in 24 recorded chunks, with zero logged underruns or zero padding. The end image remains in-world with lower health and changed terrain, so this is not a location-identical causal comparison with the short route. Native1080p60 remains unachieved. Evidence: `guest-notify-audio-long-summary.json` and the corresponding `...135837/` raw trace, geometry audits, TSV, audio log and screenshots.

Two large stalls have caller CPU recording times of 138.353 and 104.823 ms; dependency-inclusive GPU spans grow with them. They do not establish GPU saturation. The copied-save seed contains no pipeline files, and this process logs zero recorded pipelines at boot. To evaluate the existing generic prewarm path separately, only that owned run's 992 pipeline keys and validated driver-cache file are copied into a new diagnostic root. The extraction verifies all sixteen primary-save hashes and restores the installed release without waking the display. Full-game validation of this cache-prewarmed configuration also reports zero Vulkan errors/VUIDs. The subsequent 120-second real-audio warm-cache window measures **59.170 fps**, P99 25.001 ms, maximum 83.338 ms, eight intervals over 50 ms and none over 100 ms; background prewarm threads are verified absent before measurement. Cache reuse improves this observation but is not a cold-cache 60 fps claim.

The archived harness and `guest-notify-invalid-no-tsv-transport/diagnosis.json` retain the failed attempt. ### Audio-enabled controls and frame-worker placement closed on 2026-11-01

Run-directory timestamps below are UTC on 2026-10-31. The replacement private native policy runs in the app process, derives the fastest group from hardware maximum-frequency information, verifies process membership plus thread start identity, intersects original masks and restores only restrictions it still owns. Uniform/unknown topology, fewer than two fastest CPUs and failed operations preserve fallback behavior. The actual policy passes 100,000 state transitions and four rejected negative controls, including reused TIDs, external mask changes and missing restoration. It affects scheduling only; GPU command generation and shader inputs remain the parent candidate's. Real native calls succeed with no API failures, and the warm-cache game run reports zero Vulkan errors/VUIDs.

The original occupancy threshold makes the validation run alternate between five/two selected workers. A lower exit threshold for already owned workers (3.75% versus the unchanged 15% entry threshold) removes the observed oscillation. The actual classifier passes 1,000 accelerated/idle/disabled rounds and three negative controls. However, concentrating all five busy frame participants on the fastest pair is a clear throughput regression: audio-enabled 120-second on/off windows measure **50.900 / 58.660 fps (-13.23%)**, with 108 versus ten intervals over 50 ms. This strategy is rejected. Evidence: `frame-retention-validation-summary.json` and `frame-retention-investigation-summary.json`.

A budgeted alternative leaves already-fast participants alone, adds at most `fast CPU count - 1` workers and retains active owned workers despite changes in raw CPU ranking. On this device that means one extra worker. Its actual selector passes 10,000 selection transitions, the 100,000-transition ownership oracle and four negative controls; warm-cache full-game validation reports no errors/VUIDs. Before measurement, the enabled run verifies three fast-group participants and two broad-mask participants. The same-APK enabled/disabled 120-second real-audio windows measure **58.672 / 58.365 fps (+0.53%)**. This single small difference is below observed run variation, so it is not adopted as an established improvement. Both P99 values remain approximately 25.001 ms; the enabled window still has seventeen intervals over 50 ms and eight over 100 ms. Child-thread affinity inheritance would also need a dedicated lifecycle audit before any production placement policy. Evidence: `frame-balanced-{source-manifest,unit-verification,build-manifest,investigation-summary}.json` and its native validation/actual-presentation folders.

The retained positive result from this investigation is the generic guest-visible completion candidate: its earlier same-APK four-window comparison improves mean presentation by 3.38%.
