Actual VkResult returns, owned begin/inheritance data, conservative extension-chain fallback and output lifetime through the existing drain must be preserved. This is a hypothesis about lifecycle locality and handoffs, not a measured gain or a completed optimization.

### Synchronization inventory and synchronous lifecycle controls on 2026-11-01

The earlier 24.063-second diagnostic capture is mapped to six frozen source sites in `sync-point-inventory.json`. Its renderer condition wait averages 6.849 ms per game present, and a separate thread's 5.339 ms host-completion wait overlaps almost all of that time. Within the latter, 4.234 ms overlaps GPU timeline waiting and 1.063 ms overlaps post-GPU CPU cleanup. The renderer makes 18.190 encoding drains per present, totaling 2.387 ms: 0.252 ms running, 1.472 ms sleeping and 0.663 ms runnable. These are overlapping pipeline measurements, not additive frame costs or entirely removable overhead. The original trace hash is verified again; this is not a new FPS capture of the later candidate.

An offline classifier follows each completed renderer drain to its next same-parent/depth trace sibling, allowing at most a 10 microsecond gap and accepting only a driver scope. It associates 2.016 ms/present (84.46% of drain wall time, 16.120 calls/present) with the following command-buffer end call, and 0.371 ms/present with begin. Six drains remain unclassified. One/ten/one-hundred microsecond sensitivity results and scheduler-state subsets are retained in `encoder-drain-adjacency.json`. This adjacency is consistent with the source wrappers' drain-then-driver order, but untraced intervening code is possible; it is an inference, not an exact call-stack measurement. The associated driver call's own duration is a separate scope and is not additional to the enclosing NVN submission cost.

A private, default-off `DM_VK_CB_WORKER_SYNC` candidate changes only the ordered encoder relative to the guest-notify parent. Begin and end execute on that worker and still return synchronously with the actual VkResult. Plain begin parameters are copied into owned queue storage; any extension chain or non-null inheritance pointer uses the original direct fallback without inspecting potentially ignored inheritance memory. The caller's result storage remains alive through the existing drain and its release/acquire/mutex completion boundary. All other Vulkan proxies, GPU submission order, command-pool retirement, shaders and game modules remain the parent's.

Tests execute the actual bounded pthread queue, including FIFO order, pressure beyond its sixteen blocks, 9,004 marker calls in each startup selection, exact injected driver errors, owned parameter identity, non-owner calls, null/extended/inherited fallback, and worker/caller thread identity. Four deliberately broken versions are rejected. Executing both actual harness cleanup blocks with a corrupted temporary preferences variable verifies restoration from independent disk backups. The archived earlier cleanup defect still fails as the negative control. Each new run preserves its exact executed harness bytes and hash.

Full-game stock-driver Vulkan validation at native 1920x1080 reports zero errors/VUIDs, with 26,554 begins and ends executed on the worker. The inspected validation image remains in-world with visible feet. A same-APK single enabled/disabled pair then warms for 60 seconds and measures 120 seconds with real audio, continuously held simultaneous movement/camera input, owned warm pipeline caches, and CPU TSV/per-pass GPU queries disabled. Actual presentation is **59.049 / 59.021 fps (+0.048%)**; both P99 intervals are 25.001 ms. The enabled/disabled windows have respectively six/eight intervals over 50 ms and two/zero over 100 ms. Both verify 46,000 device audio callbacks, thermal status zero before/after, completed full-frame native-1080 geometry audits and no recovered ADB read gaps. The same seed/input procedure can diverge during real-time movement: inspected end images remain in-world at different climbing/camera positions, so these are not pixel-identical scene controls.

This small single-pair difference establishes no throughput gain; the synchronous lifecycle candidate remains private and is not promoted. Moving the calls retains their synchronous boundary, so this experiment does not establish that an end-related drain can be removed. A future experiment should identify exact drain reasons and defer waiting only where the backend can retain owned inputs, actual error handling and command-pool/resource ordering until results are needed. Native1080p60 remains unachieved. Evidence: `cb-worker-sync-{source-manifest,unit-verification,build-manifest,cleanup-unit-verification,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json`, the `...162634/` validation folder, the `...163011/` enabled and `...163434/` disabled actual-presentation folders, and the synchronization inventory/adjacency analysis. The endpoint verifies unchanged normal sources, host outputs, shader bundle, published/installed non-debuggable release, sixteen primary-save hashes, original preferences, clean GPU settings, stopped owned processes and physical display OFF.

### Deferred backend part submission on 2026-11-01

A second private candidate removes intermediate producer waits through an internal NVN-backend operation rather than changing Vulkan API return semantics. Checked plain command-buffer begins are recorded with owned parameters. Each intermediate part queues its main/upload ends and actual queue submit on the existing ordered worker, then publishes promptly while the producer prepares the next part. The uniform-copy drain still precedes every queued GPU submission; uploads remain first in each batch and the existing queue mutex still serializes submissions/presents. Extension/inheritance begins retain the original checked direct path. All 54 ordinary Vulkan PFN proxies compare unchanged against the parent and still return actual synchronous results.

Worker error/sent/timestamp results live in the persistent backend slot and are collected only after the existing drain. Failed ends stop later early sends and feed the original dropped-submission handling. Actual begin/submit failures remain fatal with their driver codes. Final NVN commit still drains before reading results, recovering/resetting a refused submission or issuing its timeline signal; internal retirement, guest-visible completion, slot reuse and completion reset remain the parent's. The draw-batch query treats already queued parts conservatively as early work, keeping the tail policy while the physical submit is pending. No shader, guest module or game-specific rule selects this path; `DM_VK_DEFER_PARTS` defaults off.

Compiled tests execute the actual bounded worker and actual core split/collection helpers for 96 reset rounds in each absent/disabled/enabled startup selection. A gated driver end proves the producer can return and begin preparing the next buffer before that end finishes. Driver oracles check parameter ownership, command states, upload order, completed CPU copies, queue-mutex ownership, actual sent counts and failed ends before/after earlier successful sends. Cold buffer allocation verifies its mandatory drain before command-pool host access. Switching early submission off with pending jobs verifies collection before fallback. Four fatal-error cases retain actual begin/submit driver codes; five deliberate copy/order/mutex/collection/ownership defects are rejected. Both executed harness cleanup blocks again restore settings, release and display from independent disk backups, and each run retains its executed script snapshot.

Stock-driver full-game validation at native 1920x1080 reports zero Vulkan errors/VUIDs, 25,109 checked worker begins and **17,672 submitted parts**, with zero failed ends or skipped parts. The validation image remains in-world with visible feet. Same-APK fresh-save/cache real-audio controls then warm for 60 seconds and measure 120 seconds with simultaneous continuous movement/camera, no CPU TSV and no detailed per-pass GPU queries. Enabled/disabled actual presentation is **58.875 / 58.824 fps (+0.087%)**. Both P99 values remain approximately 25.001 ms, maxima 83.338 ms; enabled/disabled windows have fourteen/seven intervals over 50 ms and neither exceeds 100 ms. Each verifies 46,000 device audio callbacks, thermal status zero, complete native-1080 geometry censuses and no recovered ADB read gaps. Inspected end images remain in-world at different climbing/camera positions, so this single pair is not an identical-scene causal proof or a stable-60 result.

Separate last-1,800-host-present log windows show an actual transport improvement: enabled/disabled encoding drains average **2.975 / 18.247 per present**, with **0.546 / 1.325 ms** drain wall time and **8.183 / 9.327 ms** replay wall time. These diagnostics are separate from the exact SurfaceFlinger measurement bounds and can include different scene work. Their dependency-inclusive GPU spans are **14.723 / 14.883 ms**, not isolated hardware busy time. Actual pipeline-construction counters also grow by three/four during those log windows; this observation does not establish that pipeline creation caused a particular stall. Normal submit-timing counters retain their original scope; deferred-submit wall time is reported separately.

The candidate demonstrates fewer producer waits but no established FPS gain and remains private/default-off. Native1080p60 remains unachieved. Further diagnosis should correlate remaining stalls with actual pipeline creation and hardware GPU activity rather than assume that the remaining frame span is saturated GPU execution. An idle-device access probe finds `gpubusy` readable, while clock/percentage nodes are denied or absent. Its vendor window/reset semantics and freshness are not yet verified, so no utilization inference follows from the returned values. Official Android documentation now recommends Android Performance Analyzer for system profiling; device counter support still requires verification.

Evidence: `split-submit-{source-manifest,unit-verification,build-manifest,cleanup-unit-verification,control-diagnostics,investigation-summary,gpu-counter-access,endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json`, validation `...170344/`, enabled actual-presentation `...170731/`, disabled `...171250/`, and the source preparation/build/harness/analysis scripts. The endpoint verifies all retained normal source/host/package hashes, unchanged shader bundle, installed non-debuggable release `41a37988...`, the sixteen primary-save hashes from the final test endpoint, original preferences, clean GPU settings, stopped owned processes and physical display OFF. No private candidate is promoted to normal runtime.

### Runtime graphics-pipeline stalls and expanded prewarm on 2026-11-01

A default-off, private work profile adds ATrace scopes to graphics-pipeline construction and its actual Vulkan creation call, shader-module lookup/preparation, NVN submission and selected submission/completion waits. Every observer edit reverses byte for byte to the validated split-submit parent; the existing trace header is unchanged. Backend tests again execute the actual split helpers/worker and retain five rejected negative controls. The actual graphics-call fragment preserves driver arguments and injected results, including trace activation changes during a call; three observer negative controls are rejected. Executed cleanup blocks restore from independent disk backups. The diagnostic APK is `142ef8c3...`; retained normal runtime remains unchanged.

The first 60.033-second native-1080 moving profile displays 3,527 frames (**58.751 fps**, instrumentation included), with thirteen intervals over 50 ms and two over 100 ms. Exact scopes measure **26 synchronous driver graphics-pipeline creations totaling 374.279 ms, maximum 60.186 ms**. Their enclosing build scopes total 374.571 ms, so preparation outside the driver call is small in this capture. Four of the thirteen long display intervals directly overlap driver creation. For example, the 116.673 ms interval overlaps 82.379 ms of two driver calls. Other gaps, including a following 125.007 ms gap, lack direct overlap. Scheduler/scoped intersections are retained without treating all nearby latency as removable or adding nested times. More than one host frame may execute during a display gap; correlation is not full causal accounting.

The lighter work scopes retain 105,237 events and 3,539 host-present counters with no reported trace data loss or ftrace parse errors. Renderer NVN submission averages 9.174 ms/present, including 8.599 ms running; encoding drain averages 0.514 ms. These diagnostics include instrumentation. GPU frequency is captured through the kernel `power/gpu_frequency` source. Decoding structured FtraceStats against the archived official schemas identifies two unavailable requested events: `kgsl/kgsl_gpu_frequency` and `kgsl/kgsl_gpubusy`. Their setup notices do not invalidate the captured CPU/ATrace data, but the requested GPU busy source supplies no activity data. No GPU saturation conclusion is drawn from frequency or dependency-inclusive timestamps. Static GPU clock/limit nodes are denied to shell; the readable idle `gpubusy` node alone does not establish current workload utilization.

To test first-use creation separately, only the ABI-matching, unmodified pipeline-key records from three owned fresh-save runs are unioned with the existing 992-key seed. This produces **1,063 observed keys**. The original validated driver-cache blob is retained exactly; opaque driver blobs are not concatenated. Each source result, APK, record file and any discarded torn tail is recorded. Extraction reads and verifies all sixteen primary-save hashes and restores release/display without launching the game. The second profile uses the same diagnostic APK, owned fresh save, audio, timing flags and original driver blob, with the expanded key file as its controlled cache input.

The expanded run measures **10 driver creations totaling 80.268 ms, maximum 44.780 ms**. Its two display gaps over 50 ms both overlap driver creation; neither exceeds 100 ms. However, its 60.048-second display average is **57.804 fps**, and the inspected end view shows a much broader scene than the first run's close rock/climbing view. Last-600-present logs show 4,994 versus 3,212 draws, respectively. These views are not identical-workload controls, so the reduced creation cost/long-gap observation does not establish a cache-caused FPS increase. The expanded list still misses runtime variants; it is not a complete pipeline census.

Segment-query diagnostics in the first/expanded final log blocks show respectively **13.36/15.00 ms covered GPU command intervals**, **0.56/0.42 ms between parts**, and **13.92/15.42 ms envelopes**. These separate log windows are not the exact SurfaceFlinger bounds, and command-buffer timestamp intervals do not measure isolated ALU utilization. Both runs independently verify completed full-frame 1920x1080 geometry censuses, 36,000 device audio callbacks, thermal status zero before/after and no recovered ADB read gaps. Inspected images remain in-world; they do not prove whole-game rendering correctness. No profile trace is an acceptance claim for stable 60 fps.

The evidence establishes synchronous driver pipeline creation as one measured source of large stalls while leaving steady workload cost and other display gaps unresolved. The next useful diagnosis is fixed scene/camera GPU pass profiling, followed by the existing continuous-movement acceptance scope, and a supported hardware-activity source. Native1080p60 remains unachieved. Evidence: `work-profile-{source-manifest,unit-verification,observer-unit-verification,build-manifest,cleanup-unit-verification,gpu-clock-access,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json`, `work-profile-expanded-cache/verification.json`, the `...174534/` and `...180836/` traces/scopes/scheduler exports and `work-stall-correlation.json`, structured `ftrace-source-coverage.json`, and archived official Perfetto schemas. The endpoint verifies unchanged retained normal sources/host outputs/shader bundle, published and installed non-debuggable release, sixteen primary-save hashes at the final test endpoint, original preferences, clean GPU settings, stopped owned processes and physical display OFF.


### Measured synchronization points and settled-view diagnostics on 2026-11-01

An offline map verifies the existing expanded-work trace hash, build/source manifests and eight unique scoped source sites. These include actual waits and their enclosing work scopes, rather than eight independent stalls. In the 60.048-second instrumented capture, renderer encoding drain averages **0.560 ms per host present** (0.049 running, 0.308 sleeping, 0.203 runnable). The game fence waiter averages **5.428 ms**, while full internal-retirement waits on the renderer average only **0.00152 ms**. The completion thread's GPU-timeline wait averages **9.854 ms per host present**, mostly sleeping; this is a concurrent CPU wait, not measured GPU busy time. Retirement itself averages 1.574 ms, including 1.547 ms running. NVN submission averages 9.223 ms with 8.605 ms running and already contains nested encoding/submission scopes.

The game wait intersects the completion thread's GPU wait by 4.855 ms/present and its retirement scope by 0.539 ms/present. These intersections do not join individual fence values: retirement may belong to another submission, even though guest notification precedes retirement for the same submission. Last observed scheduler wakers provide supporting associations, not a complete dependency graph. The frozen sites and hashes are retained in `current-sync-point-inventory.json`, generated by `analyze_current_sync_points.py`. The separate measured synchronous graphics-pipeline creations remain driver work, not GPU barriers. CPU call duration cannot establish a recorded barrier's later device cost; removing required synchronization is not justified by these measurements.

A subsequent bounded run uses the same private work-profile APK with CPU scopes and segment/upload queries disabled. It loads the owned fresh save and 1,063-key cache, settles the saved camera for 60 seconds, and samples two stationary views for 40 seconds each. Only a camera command between views changes direction; no movement or control commands run during either measurement window. Sparse pass timing rotates through a stride of 512, requests 2,048 game frames and records at most one timestamp pair per frame. The two views retain **716/700 timed samples across 218/216 ordinals**, exact timestamp-to-attribution identity sets and zero shader-group overflow. Each view has before/after complete native-1920x1080 main-geometry audits and thermal status zero. Inspected images remain in-world with visible feet; they are scoped image checks rather than whole-game correctness proof.

Actual presentation is **58.163/58.263 fps**, with P99 approximately 33.335 ms, maxima 191.677/133.341 ms and respectively six/seven intervals over 50 ms. These diagnostic fixed views are not continuous-movement acceptance tests. Individual dependency-inclusive pass samples reach at most 0.876/0.846 ms; repeated higher-ranked groups include full-resolution six-attachment geometry, often with 256 draws. Several groups have only three or four observations and ordinals/content can change with animation. TOP/BOTTOM intervals include dependencies and overlap; group medians must not be summed into measured frame time, shader cost, barrier cost or hardware utilization. The data does not establish a single isolated GPU bottleneck.

`analyze_fixed_pass_profile.py` validates the sparse captures and writes `fixed-pass-investigation-summary.json`. Both actual harness cleanup and its executed positive/negative unit control preserve independent on-disk restoration inputs. The completed run verifies all sixteen primary saves, restores the normal release and original preferences, stops the game/owned recorders and turns the physical display OFF. `fixed-pass-{endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json` retain the final normal-state and evidence hashes. No runtime candidate is promoted, and native1080p60 remains unachieved. Further synchronization diagnosis should carry fence/submission identities across waits and publication, or obtain supported GPU activity evidence, while retaining continuous-movement native-1080p60 as the acceptance scope.


### Exact fence identity and publication attribution on 2026-11-01

A private observer extends the tested work-profile snapshot with full-width fence IDs at final submission, GPU timeline waiting, CPU-visible post-processing, guest publication, internal retirement and guest/internal waits. NVN wrappers distinguish explicit syncs, acquire syncs and unfenced waits. Zero-time polls have distinct labels. Formatting occurs only with the startup trace opt-in and active collection; balanced cleanup handles a collection stop inside a driver call. All edits reverse byte for byte to the tested parent. Compiled tests execute the actual final-submit fragment, guest/internal wait functions and NVN SyncWait wrapper with absent/disabled/enabled/invalid flags, 64-bit boundary IDs and preserved driver results. Four injected identity/gating/balance/result defects are rejected. No shader, resource policy or Vulkan command changes; normal sources are restored after building.

The real-audio moving profile uses fresh owned save/cache, warms for 60 seconds, then measures 60.032 seconds at verified native 1920x1080. It captures **142,279 scoped events and 3,590 host-present counters**, with no trace data-loss or parse errors. Exact full-ID joins retain **7,179 complete submission chains and 3,106 nonpoll guest waits**, without unmatched waits or returns preceding their matched visibility marker. Every matched NVN parent is an **explicit sync wait**, not an acquire sync. Source/order checks place each final submit before its matching GPU-wait return, CPU effects, guest publication and later internal retirement.

The joined nonpoll waits total **4.487 ms per host present**. Their disjoint same-fence partition is 0.475 ms before that fence's GPU wait starts, 3.940 ms during its CPU-observed GPU wait, 0.0084 ms between GPU-wait return and guest visibility, and 0.0642 ms after visibility. The first category intersects **0.256 ms/present of earlier fences' retirement**, rather than retirement of the fence being awaited. This resolves an ambiguity in the earlier overlap-only analysis. It does not establish that all intersected latency is removable, nor that the GPU-wait phase is isolated GPU busy time. Completion retirement itself totals 1.447 ms/present, while CPU-visible effects total 0.0322 ms/present; the renderer's full internal wait remains about 0.0016 ms/present. An independent ordered retirement worker is therefore a specific candidate to evaluate without weakening fence or resource-lifetime contracts.

Actual presentation is **59.685 fps**, P99 16.668 ms, maximum 58.337 ms and one interval above 50 ms. There are no runtime driver graphics-pipeline creations in this capture; its long display gap has no such direct overlap. The end image remains in-world in a close tree/climbing view, so its average cannot establish a gain against earlier wider views, actual unrestricted traversal, or stable 1080p60. The same two requested KGSL events remain unavailable; no GPU hardware-utilization inference follows. The run verifies 36,000 device audio callbacks, before/after native geometry audits, no recovered ADB read gaps and original save/settings/release restoration, then stops and turns the physical display OFF.

Evidence is in `fence-profile-{source-manifest,observer-unit-verification,build-manifest,cleanup-unit-verification,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json`, the source/preparation/build/test/analysis scripts and `fence-profile-system-c1-e1-d1-s1-trace-bench-20261031-190809/`, including full-ID `fence-chain-jobs.csv`, `fence-chain-waits.csv` and their verified analysis. Normal runtime is unchanged and no private candidate is promoted. Native1080p60 remains unachieved; the next controlled candidate must be tested without observer overhead and with the full moving acceptance scope.


### Ordered retirement worker and per-sensor thermal evidence on 2026-11-01

The exact-fence diagnosis is tested with a private, startup-opt-in FIFO retirement worker (`DM_VK_RETIRE_THREAD=1`, default off). GPU waiting, query/readback, pool copyback, CPU-visible post-operations and guest publication remain on the original completion thread. Only the subsequent unchanged retirement/reset/free work transfers to the new worker, with at most 32 pending records including the active record and bounded backpressure. Internal completion still follows all resets, preserving command-pool/slot reuse and full-wait contracts. Tests execute the actual worker, publication tail, retirement and wait helpers across five startup selections and 96 gated rounds per selection; five injected ordering, lifetime, bound and opt-in defects are rejected. A full-game Vulkan-validation run reports zero validation errors/VUIDs, 4,433 queued and finished retirements, peak one pending and no backpressure. This is correctness evidence for the exercised path, not a performance result.

The first moving route reaches a Game Over screen and is excluded, regardless of its frame count. A separate warmup abort caused by a recovered ADB read timeout is also excluded. The revised harness tests recovery using its actual warmup/window loop: read recovery does not restart the original game, an affected measurement window is archived before same-process recollection, and a real PID change is rejected. Executed cleanup tests cover the benchmark, validation and navigation harnesses, including independent on-disk restoration inputs and a rejected old cleanup defect.

The valid controls use a new owned scene seed. Only positions in copies of two source-save slots are selected using the game's owned map-placement data; the game then writes a new manual `save/2` at approximately (-813.082, 192.654, 1862.140). Original seed and all sixteen primary-save hashes remain unchanged. This is test-scene selection, not a runtime physics, speed or graphics change. All four inspected end images remain in-world near a grassy slope and ruins, but camera distance, lighting and trajectory differ; one close camera intersects the avatar. These checks do not establish unrestricted open-field traversal or full rendering correctness.

One **ON/OFF/OFF/ON** group uses the same private APK `e091ee86...`, owned game-written seed, 1,063-key cache and driver blob, real audio, verified native 1920x1080 geometry, 60-second warmup and 120-second simultaneous movement/camera windows. CPU scopes and sparse/segment/upload GPU queries are disabled. Actual SurfaceFlinger presentation is respectively **58.791, 58.571, 57.174 and 54.497 fps**. P99 is 25.001, 25.001, 33.335 and 33.335 ms; maximum intervals are 175.010, 83.338, 241.680 and 250.014 ms. The enabled raw mean is 56.644 versus 57.873 disabled (-2.123%), with no repeatable gain. Changing operating conditions and nonidentical trajectories prevent treating this as a causal regression estimate. Both enabled samples show peak two pending retirements and zero backpressure. Audio callbacks reach 46,000/46,000/48,000/48,000. Every accepted window is the first attempt and has no recovered observation gaps.

The old thermal check is insufficient: overall `Thermal Status: 0` holds throughout, with status override false, but the current HAL CPU/GPU maximum statuses before-to-after are **(0,0) to (0,0); (0,0) to (3,0); (0,0) to (3,3); (3,0) to (3,3)**. Cached notifications and current HAL readings are retained separately. The archived [Android 16 thermal-manager implementation](https://github.com/aosp-mirror/platform_frameworks_base/blob/android16-release/services/core/java/com/android/server/power/ThermalManagerService.java) computes aggregate status from skin sensors; aggregate zero cannot exclude nonzero CPU/GPU severity. Vendor-reported values are not assumed to be calibrated physical die temperatures. Before/after dumps do not measure continuous frequency, power or throttling cost.

Separate last-three-600-present log windows average roughly 4,700-4,800 draws, while CPU replay rises from **8.377 to 10.887 ms** and dependency-inclusive GPU spans from **15.237 to 16.940 ms** across the group. These are different windows from the exact display measurement, concurrent CPU/GPU times must not be added, and the GPU spans do not measure hardware utilization. The observations are consistent with changing operating conditions but do not establish that thermal restrictions explain all lost performance. The next diagnosis must record actual CPU/GPU frequencies and per-sensor states during a fixed diagnostic workload before another sustained-power candidate; continuous-movement native-1080p60 remains the acceptance scope. No thermal protections are bypassed.

A read-only upstream review observes Android commit `0d0468dd...` and Apple `5474c6f3...`. Android's first-use asynchronous graphics path omits eligible draws while their pipeline is pending, so its apparent FPS cannot be adopted as an equal-quality result. Background compute prewarm and moving pipeline-record writes outside the pipeline mutex are separable ideas needing local measurement; the private tests already use an internal data path. Apple's offline PSO archive work is not Android driver-cache compatibility evidence. No upstream code is adopted in this experiment.

The retirement worker remains private and default off. Native1080p60 is unachieved. Evidence is in `retire-worker-{source-manifest,unit-verification,build-manifest,cleanup-unit-verification,measurement-recovery-verification,control-summary,thermal-analysis,image-review,upstream-review,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json`, the preparation/build/test/analysis/closure scripts, owned-seed proofs, full validation and all retained valid/invalid run directories. The final endpoint verifies retained normal sources/host outputs/shader bundle and production APK, installed non-debuggable release `41a37988...`, the sixteen primary-save hashes at the final test endpoint, original preferences, clean GPU settings, stopped owned processes and physical display OFF. No private candidate is promoted.


### Fixed-view frequency limits and official Game Mode control on 2026-11-01

Two bounded, private fixed-camera diagnostics retain the same owned game-written field seed, expanded 1,063-key cache, real audio and verified 1920x1080 geometry. The ordered retirement worker, CPU scopes and sparse/segment/upload GPU queries are off. A SurfaceFlinger trace adds only CPU/GPU frequency events and 500 ms sysfs CPU-frequency samples, with 10-second CPU-policy/HAL snapshots. Each run warms for 60 seconds and measures about 300 seconds without movement/camera commands. The initial unlock preflight and an initial CRLF parser failure provide no gameplay performance result and are excluded. Sampler tests execute the actual function against captured output, four line-ending variants and stale cached-state input, rejecting two defects; actual interval SQL tests verify boundary clipping and duplicate timestamps with two rejected defects. Independent-disk cleanup blocks are executed in tests.

The first run displays **17,302 frames in 300.031 seconds (57.667 fps)**, P99 25.001 ms, maximum 33.335 ms and no interval over 50 ms. Its ten complete 30-second bins range from 57.067 to 58.033 fps without a progressive decline. All 36 current-HAL CPU/GPU/skin snapshots report severity zero. CPU policy limits remain **1,996,800/2,438,400 kHz**, versus declared hardware maxima 3,628,800/4,608,000. Read-only idle snapshots outside the game instead show limits 3,513,600/4,512,000. These observations establish a different active-game frequency envelope but do not identify its requesting policy or causal FPS cost. CPU6 wall-weighted reported frequency remains approximately 2.276-2.291 GHz across bins. Raw GPU state means remain approximately 825,653-825,932 in the trace's units. Frequency includes idle periods and does not measure hardware utilization.

The trace retains 1,627,632 CPU-frequency samples and 178 GPU-frequency samples, with complete per-bin coverage, no data-loss errors and no unknown/failed/parse-error ftrace events. Read-only thermal probe envelopes average 142.182 ms, including host/device round trips; this is not measured CPU overhead and is not subtracted from frame time. Other HAL signals are preserved: the vendor voltage-type `vbat` signal reports status 6 throughout both captures. Its value is not assumed to be a calibrated physical voltage or temperature. CPU/GPU/skin severity zero cannot establish that every electrical, thermal or power constraint is inactive.

The normal APK already declares the game category. The tablet initially exposes only standard/custom Game Modes, with no configured intervention for this package and battery saver off. The official [Game Mode documentation](https://developer.android.com/games/optimize/adpf/gamemode/gamemode-api) requires app-controlled optimization when declaring support. Archived Android 16 GameManager sources show the legacy boolean metadata fallback and select the generic GAME power mode for foreground game-category apps independently of the user-selected standard/performance mode. Availability alone does not guarantee higher clocks.

A private metadata-only APK `a3b4c09d...` adds `com.android.app.gamemode.performance.enabled=true`. Every executable, native library, resource and other APK entry is byte-identical to parent `e091ee86...`; only AndroidManifest.xml changes, with the same signer, signature/alignment verification, package and game category. This is an OS-policy diagnostic, not production mode handling. The device now exposes standard/performance/custom and confirms actual selection of performance. In one live game process the camera, native scale and 60-fps target stay unchanged through **standard 60 s, performance 120 s, standard 120 s**. Mode-command intervals are bracketed and excluded from stable-phase analysis.

The three stable phases present **57.413/57.501/57.532 fps**, respectively. All sampled CPU limits remain 1,996,800/2,438,400 kHz. CPU6 reported means are about 2.279/2.281/2.285 GHz and raw GPU means 825,827/825,861/825,808, providing no measured clock response or established performance benefit. Whole-window presentation is **57.496 fps**, maximum 83.338 ms with two intervals over 50 ms. One final CPU snapshot reaches severity 3 while sampled limits remain unchanged; prior snapshots are zero. The single sequence cannot quantify a small causal gain/regression, thermal enforcement effects or unobserved effective-clock changes. No metadata optimization is promoted. Game Mode is restored to its independently saved original standard state before reinstalling the retained release.

Before/after images of both runs remain in-world at the same broad view near grass and ruins, with animation/lighting changes. Grass hides the lower legs; this is not unrestricted-traversal, contact correctness or whole-game visual verification. Audio callbacks reach 82,000/84,000. A fresh screen-off capability query confirms Adreno 840, Vulkan 1.4.295, driver 0x8034a01f and no VK_KHR_performance_query. The matching KGSL module is loaded but its file is denied to shell (including a cat error returned with exit zero), so no exact handler semantics are inferred from a successful process exit. Readable gpubusy remains uncalibrated as a current-activity source. Neither frequency nor existing dependency-inclusive timestamps prove GPU saturation. Further diagnosis should calibrate an available activity source or controlled GPU-only work to separate execution from command-supply/dependency gaps, then optimize the measured generic cost and return to full moving acceptance.

Evidence is in `frequency-thermal-{preparation,sampler-verification,interval-verification,cleanup-unit-verification,investigation-summary,endpoint-verification,artifact-index}.json`, `game-mode-{build-verification,harness-preparation,cleanup-unit-verification}.json`, source/test/analysis/closure scripts, both complete run directories, excluded-run records and `frequency-thermal-system-study/` with raw policy/HAL readings, official sources and current capability/access proofs. Normal runtime remains unchanged and native1080p60 is unachieved. The fresh endpoint verifies retained sources/host outputs/shaders and production APK, installed non-debuggable release `41a37988...`, both runs' sixteen unchanged primary-save hashes, original preferences, original standard Game Mode, clean GPU globals/properties, stopped owned processes and physical display OFF.


### Controlled GPU activity probes and local pipeline-key CRC experiment on 2026-11-01

The stock driver's standard Vulkan performance-query extension is unavailable. A read-only KGSL query opens the device and successfully queries fourteen counter groups, but all four reads of existing active counters return EPERM. A second probe obtains its own CP and RBBM leases using the archived AOSP UAPI and releases both successfully; all six owned-counter reads also return EPERM. This establishes denial in the tested shell context, not every possible app or profiling context. No denied register access is bypassed, and the downloaded header is an ABI reference rather than proof of this device's exact kernel implementation.

Three bounded compute probes independently verify their results and retain source, executable, shader and observations. The first runs offscreen with idle/continuous/pulsed phases; the second adds approximately 60 Hz paced compute. A final content-free framework NativeActivity probe uses the game's existing package, signer, UID, game category and foreground display state without loading game code. It restores the normal release, original preferences, standard Game Mode and all sixteen primary-save hashes, then turns the physical screen off. The native workload has a 50-second alarm; the foreground wrapper also bounds startup. Cleanup tests execute the actual restoration block, including an injected post-restoration audit failure.

The foreground probe's interior continuous phase has valid nonzero-total gpubusy readings for **32/40 samples**, with conditional busy fraction about 0.967 and contained-job timestamp-envelope fraction about 0.958. Pulsed compute has only **2/41** valid samples despite a timestamp fraction about 0.149; its conditional busy fraction is about 0.427. Frame-paced compute has **16/60** valid samples, conditional fraction about 0.432 and timestamp-envelope fraction about 0.433. Zero-total observations remain unknown rather than zero activity. Results depend on workload cadence and do not establish accounting-window semantics, current game GPU utilization or saturation. Timestamp envelopes include dependencies/barriers; nonzero-subset agreement cannot fill missing windows. The same foreground content-free probe observes CPU policy caps 1,996,800/2,438,400 kHz at both saved policy snapshots, without identifying the policy requesting them.

A separate CPU-only benchmark on 1,063 owned, unmodified 1,000-byte pipeline-key records compares the actual existing hash table with a generic dual IEEE/CRC32C hash on AArch64. Independent bitwise references verify 3,111 length/alignment/record cases and actual insert/get/delete/reinsert behavior. Median table lookup is 185.271 ns with the original hash and 104.250 ns with CRC. The isolated prime-core process reports 4.608 GHz, whereas active-game frequency limits differ; this microbenchmark does not predict FPS or remove the key-construction cost.

The local experimental source copy changes only shared map hash dispatch, a hardware-gated CRC helper and graphics-pipeline-map selection relative to its experimental parent. Full key bytes, memcmp equality, pipeline objects and shader chain/cache ABI remain unchanged. Unsupported hardware, absent settings and default settings use the original hash. The shared map preserves its selected hash through growth, deletion and backshift. A transactionally allocated rebuild switches a populated table under its existing mutex; allocation failure retains the original table and value ownership. Host/native tests execute the actual headers and live-update fragment, including 20,000 mixed operations, forced collisions, eight settings transitions, CRC reference checks, forced hardware fallback and both allocation-failure points. Five injected implementation defects are rejected. The build compiles 141 sources with zero failures and restores all normal source/output hashes, including the shared map; the CRC helper remains absent from normal runtime.

The debug APK is `8012113c...` and full-validation APK `b96ab63d...`; both contain the same native library `b33ecfe0...` and have verified signatures and 16 KB ZIP/ELF alignment. Full Vulkan validation exercises four live hash switches on the populated 1,079-entry map with **zero validation errors**. These are local experiments and remain separate from retained production release `41a37988...`.

One fresh process then warms for 60 seconds and performs four fixed-camera 60-second windows in **original/CRC/CRC/original** order, followed by **120 seconds of CRC-enabled simultaneous movement and camera input**. All five windows verify native 1920x1080 passes, real audio, bounded independent trace collection and critical-thread affinity. Detailed/sparse GPU queries, CPU scopes and the experimental retirement worker are off. The graphics map stays at 1,093 entries across all four fixed windows, so no first-use graphics-map growth is observed in them. Original/CRC fixed presentation is **59.009/58.892/58.810/58.846 fps**. Pooled CRC versus original presentation changes by **-0.130%**, providing no established end-to-end benefit from this single sequence. Existing 600-frame CPU log scopes, with each first potentially partial block excluded, report pipeline-key scope means **1.113/1.010 ms** for original/CRC; this scope includes construction and matching as well as hashing. Draw counts and animation/lighting can still vary, so workload-normalized comparisons are retained in the analysis rather than asserted to be isolated hash speed.

### Ordered descriptor-byte worker experiment on 2026-11-01

A local, default-off experiment moves descriptor-buffer byte encoding into the existing bounded Vulkan FIFO. It changes only `gfx_vk.h`, `gfx_vk_descriptor.c` and `gfx_vk_encode.c` relative to the retained experimental parent. The producer still allocates immutable descriptor scratch for its original submission-fence lifetime. Each job owns the complete typed write graph and native binding offsets. For a patched set, copying predecessor bytes also runs on the worker after its predecessor, before the patch and later bind/submission commands. Unsupported or oversized graphs drain and use the original direct path. An immediate CPU byte comparison drains pending jobs. The exact `DM_VK_DB_ENCODE_THREAD=1` opt-in is sampled once per outer scope; mode changes take effect after the existing end-of-scope drain. The original 54 Vulkan proxies, queue order, shaders, pipeline keys, game code and GPU completion/retirement semantics remain unchanged.

The [Vulkan descriptor API](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/vkGetDescriptorEXT.html) defines host-generated descriptor bytes and requires the referenced resources to remain valid through their last dynamic use. The experiment preserves those resources' existing submission-fence ownership and coherent scratch allocation. Moving host work alone does not establish lower total CPU time or a throughput benefit.

Host checks execute the whole actual worker and descriptor runtime for **512 immutable three-job chains** across eight supported descriptor types. They poison every original argument graph, check an ordered bind and immediate CPU reader, preserve original data through successive patches, check exact outer-scope flags, and exercise bounded/unsupported fallback. Eight deliberately broken pointer/copy/drain variants are rejected. The identical positive fixture passes on the tablet's AArch64 CPU with a 20-second native alarm. An asset-free stock-Adreno GPU oracle then passes **768 independent CPU-vector cases**, covering direct calls, command-only worker and descriptor-byte worker. All 512 queued descriptor jobs in the enabled GPU run complete with no fallback or numerical mismatch. The probes leave the game/preferences unchanged, remove their helpers and keep the physical display OFF.

The experimental packages compile **141 sources with zero failures**, restore all normal source/output bytes and pass signature and 16 KB ZIP/ELF alignment checks. Debug APK `8d3f13b5...` and full-validation APK `fd89488d...` use the same native library. The first game validation attempt remains excluded: its final logged comparison counter is 842 against the original requirement above 1,000, although no Vulkan errors are observed. A separate run retains that gate and adds a bounded exposure wait; it reaches 1156 reads after 8.297 extra seconds. Four live mode switches complete, **3,672,695 queued/3,672,695 completed descriptor jobs** have zero direct fallbacks, and full validation reports **zero errors/VUIDs**. Nonfatal unused-fragment-output warnings remain in the preserved log. Native 1920x1080 geometry, original buffer-lookup verification and descriptor memo verification are retained. Actual restoration blocks are tested against a corrupted temporary variable and the known broken predecessor.

One new process warms with a fixed camera for 60 seconds, then measures **off/on/on/off for 60 seconds each**, followed by a 120-second enabled moving-input diagnostic. Real audio, prime replay/encoder affinity, native 1920x1080, constant-comparison compatibility and existing ordered split submission remain enabled. The separate retirement worker, CPU scopes and detailed/sparse/segment/upload GPU queries remain off. Pure 600-frame logs confirm descriptor-job counts advance in enabled windows and remain constant in disabled windows; each first potentially partial block/counter snapshot is excluded. Before/after current-HAL CPU/GPU/skin snapshots all report zero severity, with CPU policy caps unchanged at 1,996,800/2,438,400 kHz. Logged pipeline counts remain unchanged across the fixed windows; these are 600-frame snapshots rather than exact instantaneous boundary counts.

Fixed presentation is **57.095/57.268/57.687/57.409 fps**. Pooled off/on values are **57.252/57.478 fps (+0.395%)**, a difference below the observed within-condition spread; both enabled windows do not exceed both controls. Producer replay changes **8.048 to 7.740 ms**, descriptor scope **3.803 to 3.527 ms**, and descriptor-new scope **0.935 to 0.642 ms**. The moved work is excluded from these producer scopes and still executes on the encoder. This sequence establishes no reliable end-to-end FPS improvement or total-CPU saving. Dependency-inclusive GPU spans are 15.833/15.759 ms, not shader-active utilization.

The moving-input window acknowledges **240 half-second paired stick commands** and displays **6885 frames in 120.038 seconds (57.357 fps)**, P99 41.669 ms and maximum 83.338 ms, with 41 intervals over 50 ms. Its initial inspected view shows the avatar among grass and ruins, with feet obscured by grass. The final camera points into the sky with no visible terrain/avatar. ### Public Android 16 graphics-hint capability observation on 2026-11-01

A bounded, asset-free native helper checks public `libandroid.so` symbols and device capabilities with the physical display OFF. The archived [AOSP Android 16 public header](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/native/+/refs/heads/android16-release/include/android/performance_hint.h) declares `APerformanceHint_createSessionUsingConfig`, `ASessionCreationConfig_setGraphicsPipeline`, `APerformanceHint_getMaxGraphicsPipelineThreadsCount` and `APerformanceHint_isFeatureSupported` as API 36. This is an ADPF scheduling mode, distinct from a Vulkan graphics-pipeline object. No private/system entry point is called.

On this API 36 tablet, all fourteen inspected symbols are present. A standalone shell-UID-2000 query reports hint sessions, power-efficiency control and graphics-pipeline mode supported, with a maximum of **five cumulative graphics-pipeline threads per app**. Surface association and automatic CPU/GPU timing report **unsupported**. The helper creates no session, launches no app and changes no APK/settings; its own executable is removed after the query. These are capability observations under the shell UID, not proof of successful game-UID session creation or improved performance.

The preceding archived game-UID-10324 service dump has five TIDs, target 16,666,666 ns, allowed/unpaused state and `GraphicsPipeline: false`; power efficiency is already false. A future optional generic implementation can investigate the public graphics mode, but must first validate app-UID creation/readback, provide honest monotonic timing from frame production through completed GPU drawing, serialize session methods and retain the supported legacy fallback. Unsupported auto-timing/surface features must stay disabled. The API documents that exceeding the cumulative graphics-thread limit can still create a session and return EBUSY while invalidating graphics-session behavior, so any returned session must be closed before fallback. Setting a mode flag alone or manipulating timing is not an accepted optimization.

The initial local compile failed before any helper was pushed because this older NDK header required `stdbool.h` first. Correcting the include order produces a warning-free AArch64 executable; the bounded device observation and cleanup then complete. This source-only probe leaves the normal runtime unchanged. A fresh independent endpoint verifies normal sources/host/shader/production bytes, installed non-debuggable release `41a37988...`, original preferences and Game Mode, the last benchmark's sixteen unchanged save hashes, clean GPU settings, stopped owned processes and physical display OFF. Evidence is in `graphics-hint-study/`, `graphics-hint-endpoint-verification.json` and `graphics-hint-artifact-index.json`. No FPS improvement is claimed; representative sustained native1080p60 remains unachieved.

### Cooling and power readback on 2026-11-01

Read-only observations distinguish current cooling levels from sampled CPU/GPU/skin severity. Across nine completed diagnostic runs, 84 of 108 foreground observations have zero severity and nonzero CPU cooling levels; the original raw data remains unchanged. The immutable AOSP CoolingDevice definition describes zero as unthrottled and positive levels as deeper throttling, without a universal mapping to GHz. This does not prove the source of the foreground frequency ceilings.

A stopped/display-OFF readback reports battery service 4,434 mV and battery sysfs 4,434,000 uV, versus Thermal HAL BCL-voltage value 0.139 with status6. CPU policy ceilings are then3,513,600/4,512,000 kHz, versus1,996,800/2,438,400 kHz in the foreground measurements. CPU-cluster cooling level1 remains present in the stopped readback and corresponding readable kernel cooling nodes, so level1 alone does not explain the lower foreground ceiling. Read queries are timestamped separately and do not form a synchronized causal intervention.

Some profiles contain individual matching frequency values, but their active selection is unproven; the Power/Performance HAL dump calls return empty bodies and provide no active-vote proof. No vendor profile, frequency limit, protection, service state, thermal override or Android setting is changed. ### Mali robustBufferAccess probe, 2026-11-04

- Hypothesis (from BOTWRecompAndroid, which enables it for freely indexed cbufs): the Plateau world's Mali `GROUP_ERROR_FATAL` (11-03 logcat: READ translation faults 0xC1/0xC2, 5 addresses, all page+0x100) are out-of-range buffer reads. Upstream nx2android 3d98ec0 still disables robustBufferAccess; no related fix.
- Change: debug-only start-up selector `DM_VK_X_ROBUST=1` (mali_probe.h, vendor 0x13B5 only, absent from release). First launch crashed: APK4's GameNativeActivity never called System.loadLibrary, so external-data APKs hit UnsatisfiedLinkError on setOverlayInsets at the first insets dispatch; fixed (load before super.onCreate), DEX rebuilt.

- ON: world renders (Link, Temple of Time, HUD); last internal frame 44.44% nonzero = whole 1280x720 game area (11-03: 0.059%); GPU busy 15.9/33.6 ms; 4651 draws/frame; PID stable; only the approved zero-copy RED.
- OFF (same APK): world black again, game frames 0.6/4.5/3.5% nonzero, GPU busy 0.00 ms, as on 11-03. App logcat returned 0 lines in both runs (even streamed), so today's fault counts are unknown.
- ON frame rate in the world ~18 fps (17-20 fps per 5 s window, title 44-60); long-frame GPU median 47.5 ms. Debug build with AU/captures; CPU caps fell to 0.6-1.0/1.3-1.9/1.0-2.0 GHz (max 2.4/3.3/3.6), CPU/GPU 64.6 C, severity 0. Not a performance result.

### Dimensity performance acceptance (user-confirmed), 2026-11-04

- Scope: performance first; robustBufferAccess on by default on Mali; 06041/04553 deferred (measurements keep the debug AU selectors). Release-default rendering follows later.
- Acceptance: BotW 1.9.0 v1114112 + DLC1/2 (ASTC sidecar pack), V2405A handheld, stock DRS (<= 1280x720), 60 fps target, HOLD off, audio on, balanced unchanged; owned Plateau seed, fixed view, 60 s warmup + 120 s x2: mean >= 55 fps (1% low and >50 ms recorded). A/B as OFF/ON/ON/OFF, >= 1 fps and both ON above both OFF. Correctness gate: world renders, no GPU fault, no new RED beyond the approved baseline.
- Start: ~16-18 fps (debug probe, robust + AU). User was told 55 fps is likely out of reach under the balanced-mode caps.

### Dimensity replay pinning A/B, 2026-11-04

- Hypothesis: `DM_PIN_REPLAY=prime` picks only cpu7 (the single X925) on V2405A, so the replay thread and encode worker share one core; `big` (cpu4-7: 3x X4 + X925) lets them run in parallel.
- Protocol: probe APK f5eb8584 (robust + AU by env), the acceptance configuration above; one driver-cache run, then OFF/ON/ON/OFF from fresh roots with the same cache, 60 s warmup + 120 s SurfaceFlinger present-fence window, 120 s cooldown between runs.
- Result: prime 16.34 / 16.08 fps, big 16.74 / 16.65 (+0.48 fps; both ON above both OFF but below the 1 fps gate: not kept). 1% low 8.43 / 8.37 vs 8.73 / 8.55; p99 116 / 116 vs 100 / 116 ms.
- Placement: the single-CPU prime mask does not survive on this phone (all 97-98 threads 0-7 at window start in both OFF runs; cause not investigated). big holds 4-7 for the replay, encode and present threads.
- big makes the encode worker faster (12.5-12.9 to 8.6-8.7 ms a frame, drain waits 1.9-2.1 to 0.8 ms) but the replay barely moves (41.1-41.6 to 40.3-40.4 ms a frame; descriptor sets 29 to 28.5).
- Replay thread CPU is ~49 s per 120 s window (~25 ms a frame) in every arm, against ~40 ms of measured replay wall time: ~15 ms a frame of the replay is off-CPU, mostly inside the descriptor-set timer. Core speed and placement are not the limit; a wait is.
- Thermal: CPU/GPU sensors 64.6 C throughout; instantaneous clocks at window ends as low as 0.4/0.8/0.8 GHz.
- Conclusion/next: no profile change. Off-CPU callchains and scheduler states of the replay thread, and the zero-copy failure. Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-pin-ab. Phone restored: release3 e85e77ce, 17 seed files, test roots removed, stopped/OFF, 600000 ms kept.

### Mali zero-copy probe and replay off-CPU attribution, 2026-11-04

- vkzc (shell, `probes/vkzc.c`): MediaTek's AHB native handle carries 3 fds and only fd 1 maps; `pool_zero_copy` maps `fds[0]`, so every pool falls back to copied (the -1 baseline RED). With fd 1 and a dedicated allocation the import works (type 0xb: HOST_CACHED, not coherent) at anonymous-memory CPU speed (64 MB read 21.0 / write 3.7 ms vs 21.5 / 4.1), but without cache maintenance the GPU copy test fails: 573,295 then 1,146,624 of 16.8M words wrong. Opaque-fd buffers are refused (-1000072003); a moved driver mapping works once, then allocation fails (-2).
- So zero-copy on Mali needs explicit cache cleaning/invalidation (NVN's own FlushMappedRange/InvalidateMappedRange for CPU_CACHED pools, or uncached AHBs). Not attempted.
- Diag run (probe APK, prime, `DM_TRACE_SYNC=1`, `DM_VK_SEGMENT_TIMES=1`, 20 s Perfetto: sched states, sync scopes, 100 Hz callstacks): 16.85 fps. Replay thread: Running 10.8 s, Sleeping 12.7 s, Runnable 0.5 s, D 0.3 s. 840 `fence: host wait` inside 420 NVN submits: 6.54 s (~19.5 ms a frame; avg 7.8, max 36 ms).
- CPU-sample chain: stage_set > tex_desc_for > vk_tex_compare_view_mode > compare_depth (compare_refresh) > vk_sub_restart > sub_begin > copied-pool `mtl_wait(g_fence)` + `vk_pools_sync` (memcpy + mprotect, ~4 ms a frame). The depth-compare snapshot's restart drains the GPU and resyncs every pool inside the descriptor-set timer: the "descriptor sets 26-29 ms" and the second submission a frame. Adreno has no copied pools, so the same restart costs nothing there.
- GPU segments a frame: 26.1 ms covered, 7.9 ms between parts, 34.2 ms envelope (6.7 parts in 2.0 submissions). CPU feed: setup + slot wait 10.6 ms a submission.
- Upstream nx2android d47cb00 keeps the same copied-pool drain in `mtl_sub_begin` and has no compare snapshot; nothing to port.
- Next: no wait/sync at continuations (A/B with `DM_VK_X_RESTART_SYNC`). Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-diag. Phone: release3 reinstalled, seed restored and roots removed before USB dropped at screen-off; the final readback is pending.

### No copied-pool sync at submission continuations, 2026-11-04

- Hypothesis: the depth-compare snapshot's `vk_sub_restart` is a continuation of the same NVN submission; its GPU wait + copy-back + pool sync (needed only where every pool is copied, i.e. Mali) is the ~19.5 ms a frame the diag trace attributed to the replay thread.
- Change: `sub_begin(1)` no longer waits or syncs copied pools; full submissions unchanged. `DM_VK_X_RESTART_SYNC=1` (debug) restores the old behavior. Upstream nx2android d47cb00 has the same unconditional wait and no compare snapshot; nothing to port.
- Protocol: restart-sync-debug.apk 2bb07831 (ecbd4cc3 + change; robust now default), the pin A/B protocol and phone cache, prime pin as shipped, OFF (=1) / ON / ON / OFF.
- Result: 16.64 / 23.00 / 23.37 / 17.65 fps (+6.04, valid). Replay 39.6 / 38.5 -> 17.4 / 17.3 ms a frame (descriptor sets 28.5 -> 7.9); GPU envelope 37.2 / 33.9 -> 31.3 / 30.7 ms; 1% low 8.80 / 9.08 -> 10.17 / 10.22; >50 ms 1055 / 943 -> 397 / 385; >100 ms 15 / 10 -> 1 / 3.
- Checks: "CPU reads racing a GPU write" 0 in all four runs; only the approved zero-copy RED; same scene in all start/end shots (lighting moves with game time). 189 tests pass, debug/release 157 sources each.
- Thermal: sensors 64.6 C; caps at window ends 0.8-2.7 GHz, not controlled.
- Conclusion: kept (perf(vk) commit). ~23 fps vs the 55 fps target. Next: the full-submission drain + pool sync (zero-copy with explicit cache maintenance: fd 1, non-coherent HOST_CACHED import). Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-restart; phone restored to release3, 17 seed files, roots removed, stopped/OFF.

### Mali non-coherent zero-copy pools (rejected), 2026-11-04

- Hypothesis: with the continuation fix in place, the remaining full-submission GPU wait + pool sync (copied pools, ~2 a frame) is the next serial cost; zero-copy removes it.
- Probe first: vkzc `ahbcm` (MediaTek handle fd 1, dedicated import, type 0xb) with DC CVAC before GPU reads and DC CIVAC after GPU writes: 0 wrong words in both 64 MB passes; 64 MB clean 1.15 ms, clean+invalidate 0.94 ms (without maintenance 388,464 / 670,480 wrong).
- Change (not kept): map the first handle fd that maps; non-coherent imports clean the dirty-tracked blocks at each full submission (no GPU wait) and clean+invalidate recorded GPU write spans at completion before the guest sees the fence. All pools zero-copy on V2405A; 191 tests passed.
- A/B (zerocopy-debug 83a664b5, OFF = `DM_VK_NO_ZEROCOPY=1`): OFF 24.83 / 23.40, ON 23.45 / 23.46 fps (-0.66, invalid). GPU envelope 29.1 / 30.9 vs 31.6 / 31.8 ms; replay 16.0 / 17.5 vs 17.6 / 17.8; ~4.9 MB cleaned + ~1.1 MB invalidated a submission; CPU reads racing GPU writes 0, world rendered.
- Conclusion: the serial pool wait no longer limits the frame; the GPU (~30 ms envelope) does. Source reverted; the diff, header and test are kept in BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-zerocopy. The vkzc `ahbcm` mode stays as a probe.
- Finalize hit a transient seed readback mismatch (exec-in returned before the remote tar finished; complete on re-read); the harness now re-reads. release3 reinstall pending a vivo installer tap.

### Dimensity GPU attribution and pass-split control, 2026-11-04

- Diagnostic only (user-approved): gyro-debug 294dae4b (= ea76e131), acceptance configuration, `DM_VK_PASS_TIMES=1` + `DM_VK_SEGMENT_TIMES=1`, one process, 60 s windows A default / B `DM_VK_PASS_SPLIT=0` (live) / C default.

Rejected: pin big (+0.48), non-coherent zero-copy (-0.66). Remaining: fewer/lighter render passes on Mali (load/store ops, pass merging; days, artifact risk), scoping robustness to the faulting reads (pipeline robustness; source unknown), or trade-offs (30 fps cap/HOLD, resolution). User decision needed; 55 fps is not reachable on present evidence.

### Clear-only passes keep only cleared attachments (rejected), 2026-11-04

- Hypothesis: the 0-draw 1024x576 passes at 1.2-2.0 ms each (GPU attribution above) are folded-clear passes that still load and store every attachment through tile memory; seedds/nxnative attaches only the cleared ones (nvn_mtl.m `rp_begin_ex` clear_only).
- Change (not kept): at `vk_end_pass`, a pass with only folded clears drops its uncleared colours and an uncleared depth-stencil (kept whole if either aspect clears); `DM_VK_CLEAR_TRIM=0` restored the old pass. 193 tests passed.
- A/B (cleartrim-debug 7d27a924, AU selectors, prime pin, pin A/B cache, OFF = `DM_VK_CLEAR_TRIM=0`): OFF 22.98 / 21.79, ON 22.92 / 22.63 fps (+0.39, invalid: ON1 < OFF1). ON trimmed 13 passes and 15 attachments a frame; GPU busy 31.5 / 33.1 vs 31.8 / 32.0 ms; replay ~17.5 ms.
- Conclusion: those passes do not cost their attachments' load/store; the per-pass times likely include the full pass-start barrier drain. Source reverted; diff and test kept in BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-cleartrim.

### Adreno LRZ workarounds off on Mali (rejected), 2026-11-04

### Pass-start barrier upper bound on Mali (diagnostic), 2026-11-04

- Question: does the ALL_COMMANDS -> ALL_COMMANDS barrier before each of ~168 render passes a frame serialize Mali's vertex and fragment work enough to explain the ~32 ms GPU envelope?
- One process (cmptier-debug 2ea62709, `DM_VK_SEGMENT_TIMES=1`), 60 s windows: A default 20.26 fps (envelope 36.6 ms), B `DM_VK_X_NOBAR=1` (live) 18.98 (39.2 ms), C default 18.16 (40.9 ms). B is no better than the A/C mean (19.2); the phone was hot and declining throughout. No GPU fault.
- Conclusion: the pass-start barriers do not limit the frame; no barrier-refinement work. Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-nobar.

### Colour-view depth comparison tiers, 2026-11-04

- Problem: without VK_EXT_depth_range_unrestricted (V2405A Mali r49p1, Turnip), a content-dependent R32_SFLOAT comparison failed closed (dummy view). nx2android 70c8cb8 and BOTWRecompAndroid 06111e4 bind the colour view with a comparison sampler on every device (undefined by Vulkan, works in practice).
- Change: (1) a driver declaring SAMPLED_IMAGE_DEPTH_COMPARISON on R32_SFLOAT / R16_UNORM (VkFormatProperties3) samples the colour view; (2) otherwise the D16 / D32 snapshots where the copy is defined, as before; (3) otherwise R32_SFLOAT through its colour view, logged once. `DM_VK_CMP_COLOR=0` restores the failure. The init log reports the capability.
- V2405A declares neither format and lacks the extension: the Plateau path is unchanged (one R16 -> D16 snapshot a frame, the R32F ALWAYS surrogate, 0 failures, tier 3 not reached). The tablet's 10-30 validation errors (06479) imply no declaration; its init log will confirm. Not a performance change; 195 tests pass.

V2405A: 22-23 fps cool, 18-20 hot, against the 55 fps target (GPU busy ~30-33 ms a frame, replay ~17 ms).

### Mali hardware counters in the fixed view (diagnostic), 2026-11-04

- Method: the vendor producer (/vendor/lib64/libgpudataproducer.so, Arm hwcpipe, 409 counters) started as AGI does (`probes/gpuprod_launch.c` from /data/local/tmp); 80 counters at 10 ms for 20 s inside a 60 s window (cmptier-debug 2ea62709, acceptance env, 23.08 fps, no overhead seen). The driver's render stages (`debug.graphics.gpu.profiler.perfetto=1`) hung the game before the world on the first try; counters only after. Phone, property and /data/local/tmp restored.
- The GPU is not saturated: per frame (43.3 ms) it is active ~31M cycles; busy 10 ms samples run at ~1.0-1.3 GHz (max 1.57), and each frame has 10-15 ms of near-idle samples. "GPU utilization" (96.7 %) is only the share of samples with any activity.
- Inside the busy part, shader cores do little: execution core ~6.5M cycles a frame per core (~5.5 ms), shader core usage 22 %, arithmetic 8 %, texture 14 %, load/store 11 %. Front ends dominate: main-phase (fragment) iterator 13.9M issue cycles a frame, tiler 5.5M, MCU (command stream firmware) 13.7M (45 %), command stream 0 waiting 6.9M.
- Work a frame: ~59k 32x32 tiles (about 100 full-screen 1024x576 passes' worth) with shaded fragments for only ~6 % of their pixels; 4,745 vertex jobs (one per draw), 4,575 tiler jobs, 486 fragment jobs, 282 compute jobs; 41 L2 flushes. External memory 2.6 GB/s read, 1.3 GB/s write: not bandwidth bound.
- Conclusion: the frame is a serial chain of ~28 ms of front-end-bound GPU work and ~15 ms with the GPU waiting for the CPU; that idle share also keeps the clock below its maximum. Next candidates: what the CPU threads wait for during the GPU gap (CPU trace with the counters), and fewer tiles/jobs per frame. Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-gpuc (+ -try1-stages).

### CPU-GPU serial chain in the fixed view (diagnostic), 2026-11-04

- One 20 s trace (cmptier-debug 2ea62709, acceptance env, `DM_TRACE_SYNC=1`, 23.79 fps): scheduler states, the app's sync scopes and Mali counters at 1 ms (`analyze_chain.py`). GPU idle runs of >= 2 ms: 11.5 ms a frame (median 5.9 ms each); the GPU works ~30.5 ms a frame.
- The game waits on the last fence of the frame it has just submitted (main thread `NVN explicit wait`, e.g. 37 ms), so frames do not overlap; the GPU then idles until the next frame's first part arrives.
- The gap, in order: completion copy-back (`fence: CPU effects`, 0.7-2.7 ms) -> guest visible -> next NVN submit -> `sync: sub begin` (copied-pool sync, 2-3.4 ms, running) -> recording until the first part is cut and sent (~7-8 ms; parts then follow every 2-3 ms). During GPU idle the render thread runs 9.6 ms a frame (6.4 recording, 3.1 sub begin).
- Why the first part is late: ordinary cuts happen only between passes and only before a pass with a depth buffer new to the submission (the Adreno LRZ rule, `DM_VK_X_CUTANY` lifts it), and depth passes are split only on Adreno (`pass_split_depth_auto`; `DM_VK_PASS_SPLIT_DEPTH=1` forces it).
- Ceiling: with the gap gone the frame is ~30 ms of GPU work (~33 fps at the observed clock). Candidates: earlier first part on non-Adreno (env A/B first), pool sync overlapped with recording, a shorter completion copy-back. Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-chain.

### Early first part on GPUs without Adreno LRZ, 2026-11-04 (kept)

- Hypothesis (serial-chain diagnostic): the GPU idles ~7-8 ms a frame until the first part is cut, because cuts wait for a pass with a depth buffer new to the submission and depth passes are split only on Adreno. Both rules exist for Adreno's LRZ; Mali has none.
- A/B, env only (cmptier-debug 2ea62709, acceptance env, prime pin, pin A/B cache; ON = `DM_VK_X_CUTANY=1` + `DM_VK_PASS_SPLIT_DEPTH=1` + `DM_VK_LRZ_TOUCH=0`, the last because the forced split path adds Adreno-only depth write-backs): OFF 22.70 / 21.31, ON 27.07 / 26.78 fps (+4.92, valid).
- ON: replay 16.4 / 16.7 vs 17.7 / 19.0 ms; GPU busy 28.5 / 28.8 vs 31.9 / 33.4 ms; >50 ms intervals 276 / 272 vs 433 / 506; 1% low unchanged (~11.3). CPU reads racing GPU writes 0, only the approved RED (plus the audio watchdog notice in both arms), world rendered.
- Change: non-Qualcomm GPUs default to the ON behaviour (cut before any pass, depth passes split like colour ones, no LRZ touches); Adreno (stock and Turnip) is unchanged by construction (tests/test_vk_lrz_vendor.py). The three variables still override. 195 tests; debug/release 157 sources each.
- Status: ~27 fps against 55. Next on the chain: overlap the copied-pool sync with recording (2-3 ms), a shorter completion copy-back (1-2 ms). Evidence: BENCH/astc-phone-validation/session-20261104-earlycut.
- Confirmation with the committed default (defcheck-debug 4b8ee070 = 5db26c41, no override variables), 2026-11-05 09:40: 26.06 fps, GPU busy 28.8 ms, replay 17.7 ms, >50 ms 344, >100 ms 0, 1% low 11.2; racing reads 0, only the approved RED; world rendered. Matches the A/B's ON arm. release3 reinstalled on the second installer prompt (the first was not seen); seed and roots restored.
